# Spiritual Ethos Of Ali

*Source: ksessions-group, group 7 (Spiritual Ethos Of Ali). 20 sections.*

<!-- category 13: Spirit Of Intellect -->

<!-- section 1 (id=1242, raw_sort=1): The Spiritual Heart -->

# Preface

To speak of ⟪ar:علی ابن ابی طالب⟫, the cousin and son-in-law of the Prophet Muhammad, is to speak of the quintessential (*representing the most perfect or typical example of a qualit*y *or class*) spirituality of the Islamic tradition. For in this seminal (influential) figure, one finds an integral expression of the two fundamental sources of Islamic spirituality - the Quranic revelation and the inspired Sunna of the Prophet. By his Sunna we do not mean simply the outward imitation of the Prophet's actions, rather, we mean the spiritual substance of the prophetic perfection to which the Quran itself refers

The Pen [68:4]
[ سورۃ ۶۸] القلم

‏وَإِنَّكَ لَعَلَىٰ خُلُقٍ عَظِيمٍۢ ‎۴
And verily, you are on an exalted standard of character.

The Prophet's words and actions express but do not exhaust this spiritual character. The inner assimilation of this character, rather than the merely formal imitation of the words and actions, is the goal of every spiritually inclined Muslim. There is a profound affinity between the believer's soul and the prophetic nature as expressed in the verse:

The Clans [33:6]
[ سورۃ ۳۳] الاحزاب

**‏ٱلنَّبِىُّ أَوْلَىٰ بِٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ مِنْ أَنفُسِهِمْ** ۖ وَأَزْوَجُهُۥٓ أُمَّهَتُهُمْ ۗ وَأُو۟لُوا۟ ٱلْأَرْحَامِ بَعْضُهُمْ أَوْلَىٰ بِبَعْضٍۢ فِى كِتَبِ ٱللَّهِ مِنَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ وَٱلْمُهَجِرِينَ إِلَّآ أَن تَفْعَلُوٓا۟ إِلَىٰٓ أَوْلِيَآئِكُم مَّعْرُوفًۭا ۚ كَانَ ذَلِكَ فِى ٱلْكِتَبِ مَسْطُورًۭا ‎۶
The Prophet has a stronger affinity to the believers than they do themselves. And his wives are their mothers. As ordained by Allah, blood relatives are more entitled "to inheritance" than other believers and immigrants, unless you want to show kindness to your close associates through bequest. This is decreed in the Record.

The Prophet, thus, outwardly manifests the perfection which the spiritually sensitive believer (to some degree or another) possesses in intuition in their soul. It is not surprising, therefore, to find the Prophet saying
عليٌّ مِنِّي بِمَنْزِلَةِ نَفْسِي

Ali is to me in the position of my own soul

This affirms not only the extraordinarily close relationship between Maulana Ali and the prophet, but also the affinity and ultimately the identity between every sanctified soul and the prophetic nature. More mysteriously, he said to Maulana Ali:
أنْتَ مِنِّی وَ أِنَا مِنْکَ

You are from me, and I am from you

Likewise, in regards to the assimilation of the substance (⟪ar:مواد⟫) of the Quran, we have this prophetic saying:
إِنَّ عَلِيًّا مَعَ الْقُرْآنِ وَالْقُرْآنُ مَعَ عَلِيٍّ، لَنْ يَفْتَرِقَا حَتَّى يَرِدَا عَلَيَّ الْحَوْضَ

Indeed, Ali is with the Quran and the Quran is with Ali. They will not separate from each other until they return to me at the (Paradise) Pool

Quran and the soul of the Prophet were thus fully interiorized by Maulana Ali, and it is this interiorization of the twin sources of the Islamic revelation lies the spiritual path of Islam. No other companion of the Prophet has anything approaching the corpus attributed to Maulana Ali.

Maulana Ali was a 'follower' of the Prophet in the deepest sense of the term. He lived physically in the shadow of the Prophet and absorbed spiritually all that radiated from him. The intimacy of the relationship between them is summed up in the words of one of the Imam's sermons:

⟪ar:لَمَّا كُنْتُ وَلِيدًا ضَمَّنِي إِلَى صَدْرِهِ، فَكُنْتُ أتَّبِعُهُ اتّبَاعَ الفَصِيلِ أُمَّهُ⟫
When I was a child he (the prophet) took me under his wing. I would follow him like a baby camel follows the footsteps of its mother.

⟪ar:يَرْفَعُ لِي فِي كُلِّ يَوْمٍ عَلَمًا مِنْ أَخْلاقِهِ الْكَرِيمَةِ وَيَأمُرُنِي بِالاِقْتِدَاءِ بِهِ⟫
Every day he would raise up for me a sign of his noble character, commanding me to follow it.

⟪ar:وَكَانَ يَتَعَاهَدُ غَارَ حِرَاءٍ فِي كُلِّ سَنَةٍ، فَأَرَاهُ وَلَا يَرَاهُ غَيْرِي⟫
He would go each year into seclusion at [the mountain of] Hira'. I saw him and nobody else saw him.

⟪ar:وَلَمْ يَجْمَعْ بَيْتٌ وَاحِدٌ يَوْمَئِذٍ فِي الإِسْلامِ غَيْرَ رَسُولِ اللهِ صَلَّى اللهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ وَخَدِيجَةَ وَأَنَا ثَالِثُهُمَا⟫
At that time no house was brought together for the religion of Islam, except the Messenger of God, Khadija and myself as the third.

⟪ar:أَرَى نُورَ الْوَحْيِ وَالرِّسَالَةِ، وَأَشُمُّ رِيحَ النُّبُوَّةِ⟫
I saw the light of the revelation and the message, and I smelt the fragrance of prophecy (in him)

# Recognizing our master through our soul

There is a ⟪ar:حدیث⟫of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) in which he said:

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/1242/ed515237-f669-4258-997c-3f322945f2cb.jpg}}

Our end goal is to recognize Allah, but the only means of doing that is by recognizing the self. And so this ⟪ar:حدیث⟫is understood in two ways:
1. That the human being, the human psyche is so complicated and so intricate, that we'll never be able to fully grasp it. So the first way to look at this ⟪ar:حدیث⟫is the union of two impossibilities. It is impossible for the human intellect to fully comprehend the faculties of the spirit, which then makes impossible to fully understand Allah - who is the **creator**of spirits.
2. The second way in which this ⟪ar:حدیث⟫is understood, is that to the extent that we are able to understand the faculties of the spirit, only to that extent we will be able to understand the **intent** of the creator. In other words, the two are directly proportional. Allah created human beings in his own image. So the more we understand this created image called ⟪ar:انسان⟫, the more we can understand Allah's intent of why he created us. **The goal is not to understand the creator, rather the **intent**of the creator**. This is similar to analyzing the work of an artist in order to determine what motivated him to create his work of art. The more we reverse engineer the faculties of our own existence, the more we will be able to understand Allah's intent behind our creation.

Our DAI ⟪ar:سیدنا مؤید الشیرازی قدس اللہ روحہ و نور ضریحہ⟫ explains that reflecting on our own existence will reveal that we are made up of two distinct components. We have a body and we have a soul. Our bodies come from this earth, because there was a time when our material bodies were being formed inside somebody else. Because for semen and ovum to exist,  they first need to be created through nutrition. Moving back in time, there was a point when even that nutrition did not exist; because nutrition then had to come from something else. Allah says in the Quran:

Time or Man [76:1-2]
[ سورۃ ۷۶] الانسان

‏هَلْ أَتَىٰ عَلَى ٱلْإِنسَنِ حِينٌۭ مِّنَ ٱلدَّهْرِ لَمْ يَكُن شَيْـًۭٔا مَّذْكُورًا ‎۱
Has there not been over man a period of time, when he was nothing worth mentioning

‏إِنَّا خَلَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ مِن نُّطْفَةٍ أَمْشَاجٍۢ نَّبْتَلِيهِ فَجَعَلْنَهُ سَمِيعًۢا بَصِيرًا ‎۲
For indeed, We alone created humans from a drop of mixed fluids, in order to test them, so We made them hear and see.

Our material bodies come from this earth in the form of nutrition that is consumed by our parents. Once we make it into the wombs of our mothers, our fetus develops over a period of 9 months - going from one stage to the next, until we finally come into this world as a fully developed human. The human body literally comes from the earth, and is a product of the earth.

Reflecting on this earth reveals there are 4 primary elements that this world is made of: **Fire, Air, Water and Earth**- and the human body contains all 4 elements. It possesses fire in the form of body **heat**. The body requires oxygen to survive which is taken in through breathing from "**air**". Much like the earth, 60% of our bodies is made up of **water**. And just like the earth, the human body is a composition of minerals and chemicals (**earth**) - proteins and DNA. Therefore our body is made up of the same 4 elements that this earth is made up of.  Then there are other similarities. For example, like everything else on this earth, our bodies exist in 3 dimensions - length, width and depth; and it is surrounded by 6 directions - front, back, left, right, top and bottom. We live on this earth; we get our nutrition from this earth, and when we die our bodies are returned back to the earth where it once again disintegrates into the 4 primary components. This is what the bible calls, "dust to dust, ashes to ashes". In other words, the physical aspect and attributes of the human body are identical to those of the earth.

Earth is the macrocosm, and the body is its microcosm. It's a miniature version of the earth itself.

Think about the ratio between the two. If you compare the size of the human body against the size of the earth the ratio would be negligible. Similarly, human body is made from the 4 elements of the earth. If you compare the heat that exists within the human body, to the heat and fire that exists in the world; or if you compare the water within the body to the oceans of the earth; or the air and gases within the body to the amount of air in the atmosphere; or the amount of minerals and chemicals within the body to those found on this earth, what ratio would that be? It would be negligible in size and proportion. Therefore:
1. The human body is entirely made from this earth. It is exactly identical to this earth. It is a product of this earth.
2. The size and elements of the human body are insignificant and negligible compared to the size of the earth and its elements

However, the human being is not just a body. It also has a subtle energy that is animating the body which is pure mass. Scientifically speaking, mass cannot move itself unless a force is applied to it by an energy. So if our bodies are in motion, there has to be a subtle force working behind the scenes that is animating it. And this force must by extremely powerful.

If you think about a corpse, it is just a blob of dead mass that can no longer move - which means that the energy that was moving it, is no longer acting upon it. For a person of average weight, it takes 4 to 6 strong men to carry this dead weight on their shoulder to its grave. Yet while this man was alive, this energy was moving this same heavy mass all by itself. It was moving it, making it run, making it jump, making it climb stairs - easily - without effort. So this should tell us something about the strength and the power of this energy that we call the spirit or the soul.

Understanding that the earth is the source of our bodies, the next logical questions is, where did this energy (soul/spirit) come from? It must also have a source. Since we've concluded that the body is from exactly the same material as its source (the earth), the soul must also be from an exactly identical source. This means that if there is a physical world for the body, there must exist a spiritual world for the spirit. And if the body is a tiny/minute portion of this enormous earth, the soul must also be a minute portion of that spiritual world. And if this small portion of energy is so powerful that it can easily animate the massive weight of the body, we can only imagine how powerful that spiritual world must be from which it came. And if upon death, our body returns back to its original source as the Arabs say:
ِکُلَّ شَئٍی یَرجِعُ اِلی أَصلِہ

Every thing returns to its original source. Water returns to water, earth to earth, fire to fire. Then logically speaking, our soul must also return back to its original source, which is the spiritual world.

## Worship (body and soul)

Now if we look at make up of the body and soul, they are two entirely different entities. The body is physical whereas the soul is metaphysical. Yet they have been joined together in a particular hierarchy. The body has been made obedient and subservient to the soul. When the soul commands, the body **willingly**obeys without hesitation or reservations - which is the very definition of ⟪ar:عبادۃ -⟫ Willing obedience.

Linguistically, the word ⟪ar:عبادۃ⟫(worship) means two things in Arabic. It is a combination of obedience (⟪ar:طاعۃ⟫) and free will (⟪ar:حُرَّیَّۃ⟫).  It is an willing obedience that is done out of love and trust. Forced obedience, done unwillingly is slavery. Worship is a lover's obedience of the beloved, as a lover will go to extremes in fulfilling the desires and wishes of the beloved - not because he has to, but because he wants to. This is the concept of ⟪ar:عبادۃ⟫and this is how the body loves the soul. It obeys every command of the soul happily, willingly, without any objections.  And Allah says in the Quran:

The Winnowing Winds [51:56]
[ سورۃ ۵۱] الذاریات

‏وَمَا خَلَقْتُ ٱلْجِنَّ وَٱلْإِنسَ إِلَّا لِيَعْبُدُونِ ‎۵۶
And I created not the jinns and humans except they should worship Me.

From this discussion we conclude that Allah created us us for willing, loving and eager obedience based on love and trust. The only way to achieve that is by getting to know our creator. The more we learn and come to know Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), the more we fall in love with him and develop trust, which engenders willing obedience in fulfill his desires and commands.

# Human Components

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/e8886e76-94d6-4369-b367-caa1effa0fa5.jpg}}

## Body (⟪ar:جسم⟫)

Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created the soul first in the spiritual world, but since physicality must exist in order for spirituality to animate it, the body is created first in the physical world. When Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) decides to create a human being in this world, he places the essence of his body in the earth (through rain ⟪ar:مزاج ممتزج⟫) in the form of nutrition. That essence then reaches the womb of its mother, where it goes through 7 stages of embryonic development. The Quran says:

The Believers [23:12-14]
[ سورۃ ۲۳] المؤمنون

‏وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ مِن سُلَلَةٍۢ مِّن طِينٍۢ ‎۱۲‏ثُمَّ جَعَلْنَهُ نُطْفَةًۭ فِى قَرَارٍۢ مَّكِينٍۢ ‎۱۳‏ثُمَّ خَلَقْنَا ٱلنُّطْفَةَ عَلَقَةًۭ فَخَلَقْنَا ٱلْعَلَقَةَ مُضْغَةًۭ فَخَلَقْنَا ٱلْمُضْغَةَ عِظَمًۭا فَكَسَوْنَا ٱلْعِظَمَ لَحْمًۭا ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَهُ خَلْقًا آخَرَ ۚ فَتَبَارَكَ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنُ ٱلْخَلِقِينَ ‎۱۴
( 12 ) And certainly We have created man from an extract of clay. ( 13 ) Then We placed him as a sperm-drop in a firm lodging. ( 14 ) Then We made the sperm-drop into a clinging clot, and We made the clot into a lump [of flesh], and We made [from] the lump, bones, and We covered the bones with flesh; then We developed him into another creation. So blessed is Allah , the best of creators 

The human body goes through these 7 stages of systematic and purposeful development in the womb of its mother

| 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ⟪ar:سلالۃ⟫ | ⟪ar:نطفۃ⟫ | ⟪ar:علقۃ⟫ | ⟪ar:مضغۃ⟫ | ⟪ar:عظام⟫ | ⟪ar:لحم⟫ | ⟪ar:خلق آخر⟫ |
| Extract | sperm | clot | lump | bone | flesh | final form |
|  |  |  | ⟪ar:روح⟫ | ⟪ar:روح⟫ | ⟪ar:روح⟫ | ⟪ar:روح⟫ |

The time period for the first 3 stages (⟪ar:نطفۃ⟫، ⟪ar:علقۃ⟫، ⟪ar:مضغۃ⟫) is 40 days each. So at the completion of 120 days (or 4 months), Allah joins the spirit, (or the ⟪ar:روح⟫) with the body, and the fetus becomes alive. This is also the time when the heartbeat becomes audible. This is where life begins. Prior to that it is simply a clot of a blood ⟪ar:علقۃ⟫, as the Quran describes it. At the end of 9 months, the extract is formed into a perfect human being, this is why Allah says:
ثُمَّ أَنشَأْنَهُ خَلْقًا آخَرَ

The word ⟪ar:آخر⟫comes from the word ⟪ar:خیر⟫which means better. Allah uses this word because that shapeless, worthless drop of vile fluid, with Allah's glory, develops into a complete, fully functional baby - with eyes that can see, ears that can hear, nose that can smell, tongue that can taste and so on. This transformation, in itself, is a phenomenal "miracle".

# The Spirit (⟪ar:روح⟫)

The spirit/soul or the ⟪ar:روح⟫is the pure, original, primordial energy that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created in the spiritual realm (⟪ar:عالم روحانی⟫). The word ⟪ar:روح⟫comes from the root word ⟪ar:ریح⟫which means wind; because the spirit is "subtle" like the wind. Wind or air is all around us, but we are rarely aware of its presence. Similarly, we all have a spirit (⟪ar:روح⟫), but we never feel it's presence. Interestingly, that the Quran does not say much about the ⟪ar:روح⟫except:

The Children of Israel [17:85]
[ سورۃ ۱۷] الاسرا

‏وَيَسْـَٔلُونَكَ عَنِ ٱلرُّوحِ ۖ قُلِ ٱلرُّوحُ مِنْ أَمْرِ رَبِّى وَمَآ أُوتِيتُم مِّنَ ٱلْعِلْمِ إِلَّا قَلِيلًۭا ‎۸۵
And they ask you, [O Muhammad], about the soul. Say, "The soul is of the affair of my Lord. And mankind have not been given of knowledge except a little."

Allah states that the ⟪ar:روح⟫is from ⟪ar:عالم الأمر⟫ (the world of commands) where creation is instant and without hesitation. It is the world of ⟪ar:کُن⟫"Be!" - ⟪ar:فیکون⟫and it was. In this verse Allah is saying that ⟪ar:روح⟫is the entity that came into existence out of the command ⟪ar:کُن⟫. The Quran also tells us that before Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) sent us into the wombs of our mothers, and joined our ⟪ar:روح⟫with our bodies, he made us testify something.

The Heights [7:172]
[ سورۃ ۷] الاعراف

‏وَإِذْ أَخَذَ رَبُّكَ مِنۢ بَنِىٓ آدَمَ مِن ظُهُورِهِمْ ذُرِّيَّتَهُمْ وَأَشْهَدَهُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ أَلَسْتُ بِرَبِّكُمْ ۖ قَالُوا۟ بَلَىٰ ۛ شَهِدْنَآ ۛ أَن تَقُولُوا۟ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَمَةِ إِنَّا كُنَّا عَنْ هَذَا غَفِلِينَ ‎۱۷۲
And remember when your Lord brought forth from the loins of the children of Adam their descendants and had them testify regarding themselves. ?Allah asked,? “Am I not your Lord?” They replied, “Yes, You are! We testify.” ?He cautioned,? “Now you have no right to say on Judgment Day, We were not aware"

This testimony was universal for every human being regardless of race or religion. This is why, according to the Islamic tradition, we have this belief that all human beings inherently have the awareness of god. Because in our original, spiritual form, as ⟪ar:ارواح⟫ we all **witnessed**him; and we **testified** that he is our master. Therefore every ⟪ar:روح⟫inherently recognizes its master because of that primordial testimony. That witnessing becomes clouded by layers of physical distractions and sins, but there is always that potential for a disbeliever to one day return to their original testimony once those veils are removed. This is why Islam never closes its doors to anyone.

---

## All Children are born Muslim

Now this ⟪ar:روح⟫that was created in the image of its lord and witnessed and testified the mastery of its lord, in its original pure form, not only possesses all the attributes of its master, it is also in perfect submission to its master. Because when Allah asked the question: ⟪ar:الست بربکم؟ -⟫Am I not your master? It testified ⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫The word ⟪ar:بلی⟫is used in Arabic rhetoric when there is not an atom's worth of doubt in a statement. In doing so this ⟪ar:روح⟫not only accepted Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) as its master (⟪ar:رب⟫), it also accepted the status of  ⟪ar:عبد⟫ for itself.

{{IMG_URL:http://localhost:786/Resources/IMAGES/32/f94906a0-71b5-4284-aa97-dcb75aea5711.jpg}}

The word ⟪ar:اسلام⟫comes from the root word ⟪ar:سلَّم⟫which means "to submit". So the linguistic meaning of the word ⟪ar:اسلام⟫is "submission"; And the one who submits is called a "Muslim". So when Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) asked this RUH  ⟪ar:الست بربکم؟,⟫and it replied ⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫- Indeed I testify, it accepted it's state of submission and accepted ⟪ar:اسلام⟫. This is why Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said in a HADEES:
مَا مِنْ مَوْلُودٍ إِلَّا يُولَدُ عَلَى الْفِطْرَةِ فَأَبَوَاهُ يُهَوِّدَانِهِ وَيُنَصِّرَانِهِ وَيُمَجِّسَانِهِ

There is not a single child; that is not born upon this pure state "Fitrah"; of natural disposition. It is then his parents (in other words, the external influences) that make him a Jew, Christian or a Zoroastrian.

In other words, the flaws in our character is something that we develop in this world, over time - by the influences of our environment. ⟪ar:کفر⟫or disbelief is an impurity that we acquire later in life through nurture, and because of the company we keep. But in our original pure form, no matter what the religion of our parents, we're all born Muslims. We're all born in a state of submission.

This is why the nature of a child is extremely pure. A child is an embodiment of all the teachings of Islam.  In fact, every child is a true Muslim in action because they are born in a state of submission which is called ⟪ar:فطرۃ⟫ or natural disposition.

The word ⟪ar:فطرۃ⟫  comes from the root word ⟪ar:فطر⟫ which means:

**To cleave, split open, or break**: This is the most basic meaning of the verb. It can be used literally to describe splitting something open, like a fruit or the earth.
**To create, originate, or bring into existence**: This meaning emphasizes the act of something being brought into its initial state or form.
**To begin something new**: This meaning suggests starting or initiating something, often related to a specific time or event.
**To break one's fast**: In Islamic context, it specifically refers to breaking the fast after sunrise during Ramadan.

A child manifest all the beautiful qualities of Islam in its pure state. This is why we love children just as we love a true Muslim - because they're both the same in exhibiting pure qualities of natural disposition that exists within the ⟪ar:روح⟫.
- A child will never lie. It is impossible to make a child lie. It just doesn't know how to.
- A child will exhibit "perfect", "unshakable" belief. When you tell a child that there is no god but Allah, He is the sole creator of the universe; the child will have no problem in believing that. It will believe that statement without a shred of a doubt. It will not ask for evidence. These are all indicative of a state of complete submission.
- You a child is told that Allah is merciful and loves you more than your parents, the child will believe that. He will accept it and not let go of that belief in the most trying of situations.
- A child will never blame Allah for its suffering; because it has no doubts about the love of Allah that was taught to it by its parents. They say the best display of this fact is in cancer wards for children. When they find out that they have cancer, they don't blame Allah for it. Parents will cry and complain, but the child will not.
- Now if a child is taught the inverse of this, they will also believe that just as strongly - because acceptance and surrender, is part of a child's natural disposition.
- Every child is selfless in its pure state. A child will always be happy to share its toys with others kids.
- A child will exhibit perfect love, obedience and servitude to its parents. When asked to bring a glass of water, a child will happily run 100 times in a row
- I child will always forgive instantly and unconditionally, no matter how many times the same event occurs. A child does not keep count, and once he forgives the event is completely erased from his memory. A child never keeps bringing up the past and holding it against its parents.
- A child will never over-eat or drink no matter how delicious the food is
- Given the choice between alcohol and milk, the child will always choose milk.
- Children our extremely compassionate. When they see someone is sad they hover around the person and try to make them smile.

Note, these are all moral qualities of a true Muslim - Love, affection, compassion, courage; and a child exhibits each and everyone of them. This is why the messenger (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said,
مَا مِنْ مَوْلُودٍ إِلَّا يُولَدُ عَلَى الْفِطْرَةِ 

Every child is born upon this pure state of natural disposition - which is ISLAM.

# Soul (⟪ar:نفس⟫)

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/1242/d7986d9b-c0b6-433e-acf9-9e1ae8e30e52.jpg}}As the child grows into this world of sensory perceptions, the next component that activates and begins to develop in the child, is the ⟪ar:نفس⟫. Modern psychology calls this the "self" or the "ego". It's also referred to as ⟪ar:أنا⟫ in the Arabic language, which means "I or me". When you and I use the word "I", or "me" in a sentence, we're actually referring to this particular component, which is the ⟪ar:نفس⟫- the soul. There is a very clear distinction in the Arabic language between the spirit and the soul. The spirit (or the ⟪ar:روح⟫) is the original, pure, primordial energy, that what was created in the spiritual realm; whereas the soul (the ⟪ar:نفس⟫) is a component of that spirit, that develops in the earlier years of childhood.

The word ⟪ar:نفس⟫ is used in many different ways in the Quran. It is used to describe the self, the psyche, the soul, the ego. And the reason the Quran calls this component the "self", is to indicate  **responsibility**. The idea is, that even though humanity as a whole, is united in possessing the qualities of a "soul" - individually they are responsible for exercising the agencies of their "free will" that the soul provides to them - because you cannot have free will, unless you are conscious of the "self". You have to be able to recognize your individual self, in order to exercise free will.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/25f3a027-0e83-4f68-8f5d-a71d7522ad12.jpg}}

So the infant, who has now taken control of its body, slowly starts becoming conscious and aware of his own individual existence. This happens around 2.5 - 3 years of age. It can now separate itself from the body. It no longer views itself as its body. Because initially, a baby cannot even differentiate between itself and the breast of its mother. It looks at the breast as an extension of itself. At this stage, its perception changes from "I **am**my body" to "I **have** a body". In other words, the child now gets into the driving seat of its body.  With the development of this component (the ⟪ar:نفس⟫), "the self" emerges and the beastly nature of the child manifests.

There are two terms that are used to define this behavior. There is the **childlike**behavior and the "**childish**" behavior. Childish is a very negative trait; where as childlike, is a very positive trait. Its a summation of all the angelic qualities of children. It comprises of the innocence and the pure nature of children coming from the ⟪ar:روح⟫. This is why Rasul Allah (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) would smell new born babies and said that they reminded him of the fragrance of paradise.

But then the beast like aspects also have to be dealt with; where it begins manifesting the traits of the ego; in the form of selfishness. It begins using the word "Me" and "I" more frequently, and the deviation from that pure state begins to occur.

This selfish state is also very important in the child's development. {{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/196bf7b0-d78b-4b41-89d4-03380f64e2e4.jpg}}Because as a child's ego begins to develop, the very first word that it really comes in contact with, is the word "No". It deeply connects wit "Denial" or "rejection" which requires a sense of identity. Up till this point the child is compulsively obedient. It will never refuse because it doesn't know how to. It begins to assert its own "identity", and declare its own presence in time and space.

I am my own person! I am not just an extension of my parents, I actually have my own existential presence. At this stage toys turn into *my* toys! "I" won't eat this, "I" don't like that, I will do that. I want that! So the "I" (⟪ar:أنا⟫) begins to manifest. Parents will generally look at this behavior as sheer disobedience - but from the child's perspective, it is literally wiring itself up to see what happens when I say "NO"? And this is an extremely important component of the child's spiritual development process - because this child was created to say "No". It was created to say ⟪ar:لا الہ -⟫ It was created to refuse and reject false god, and testify for the one true God - ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫ - There is **no**deity except Allah. The formula of our creed begins with a denial and rejection of false gods (⟪ar:لا الہ⟫) so we can affirm and accept the one true God (⟪ar:الا اللہ⟫). This is all part of the creators divine plan in humanizing the child. This is where knowledge and education comes into play; where parents teach the children how to balance negation with affirmation. To summarize, the control of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫begins to manifest in the form of the word "NO". And at this stage you'll see both qualities of submission as well as rejection manifesting within the child.

# Lubb (⟪ar:لُب⟫)

As the child continues to grow, and as his ⟪ar:نفس⟫ develops, based on the information he takes in from various sources (his parents, his family, his school, his friends, his environment), two more components begin to develop. These are the ⟪ar:قلب⟫ and the ⟪ar:عقل⟫ (the spiritual heart and the spiritual intellect). These have nothing to do with the physical heart and the physical brain. In the Quranic context, both these terms always refer to the components of the ⟪ar:روح⟫(the spirit). This pair is referred to as ⟪ar:لُب⟫in the Quran.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/1242/95d0a102-b491-4870-a859-3ada1265aa86.jpg}}

## Spiritual Heart (⟪ar:قلب⟫)

The spiritual heart is the center of free will - ⟪ar:ارادۃ⟫ and desires. When someone says, "I want to acquire knowledge", it is in fact the spiritual heart (⟪ar:قلب⟫) of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫desiring to acquire knowledge. The heart is the command center. This is the component that commands the ⟪ar:نفس⟫.  The soul is driven by desires. The spiritual heart desires and commands the ⟪ar:نفس⟫, who executes the command of the heart.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/9b24d1aa-4395-4ab6-981d-42e8c282110f.jpg}}The heart is the center of all good and bad emotions. Courage comes from the heart, cowardice also comes from the heart; love comes from the heart, and so does hatred. Generosity comes from the heart, and so does stinginess. This is why it's call ⟪ar:قلب⟫.

The word ⟪ar:قلب⟫ in Arabic means "to flip" - to turn something around on its face; to flip something over; or to invert something - such that what was on top goes to the bottom, or vice versa. Arabs would say ⟪ar:قَلَبَ کلاماً⟫ he flipped the conversation - when a person is talking about one thing and then switches to the opposite subject. The word ⟪ar:انقلاب⟫ also comes from the same root word, which means "a revolution", as the idea of every revolt is to flip the existing conditions around.

The reason the the **physical**heart is called ⟪ar:قلب⟫ is because it never rests. It is constant flipping between two states - expansion and contraction in order to nourish the entire body. It is constantly working to pump and deliver oxygenated blood to all parts of the body. The body is functional because of this constant delivery mechanism. That is exactly what the spiritual heart does. It is constantly feeding commands and desires to the ⟪ar:نفس⟫ (the soul) through emotions. It constantly pumps desires and free will into the ⟪ar:نفس⟫ - and similar to the physical heart it never stops. This is also why human emotions are constantly flipping between two opposites.

## Spiritual Intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫)

Desires and emotions are blind. They are like a flood that can overwhelm and take over the ⟪ar:نفس⟫ completely. Therefore these emotions need some sort of direction, boundaries and restraint. Even good and healthy emotions can be disastrous if there are no boundaries set for it. For instance, generosity is a beautiful emotion, but without any defined boundaries, one would end up giving all their money to someone, and their family would starve to death in the process. Similarly, courage and bravery are praiseworthy emotions, but not when faced with a lion. The spiritual heart needs to have guidelines and direction on when to exercise which emotion; when to be courageous and stand its ground, and when to run. When to be generous and when to hold back; when to love and when to show anger. Even love without boundaries can be a very dangerous emotion. This is where intellect or the ⟪ar:عقل⟫ comes into play. The word ⟪ar:عقل⟫means to bind or to restrain.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/74483ccd-787e-49d3-96a0-a0cd10d69e3a.jpg}}

The word ⟪ar:عِقال⟫in Arabic is a rope that is used to tie a camel. Once the camel was tied to a tree it could still move, but it's movement would become limited. It could only move within a specified boundary to a certain extent. It could no longer wander off anywhere it wanted. Its verbal form ⟪ar:عَقَلَ⟫ means "to tie", or "to bind", which is exactly what the spiritual intellect does. It binds the emotions. It sets boundaries for desires and the free will of the heart.

The intellect binds restrains and restricts the blind emotions and desires of the heart to keep it on track towards its goals. It navigates, sets precedents and priorities for the desires of the heart to protect the ⟪ar:نفس⟫.

The soul or the "self" operates under the desires and commands of the heart with the guidance of the intellect.

This process continues until the child grows into a fully grown adult. His ⟪ar:نفس⟫has now taken complete control over his body. He is now able to obey the commands of his spiritual heart, under the guidance and confines of his intellect. In other words, he or she is now a fully responsible adult. And it is to this ⟪ar:لُب⟫(combination of the heart and intellect) that the Quran speaks to.  Quran is a map and a guide for the soul. It is a believers navigation system (GPS), and without its clear directions the ⟪ar:نفس⟫ would be lost. It would just wander off into any valley the heart would command, and end up destroying itself. So the Quran serves as an external intellect, that works with the internal intellect to direct the soul towards the specific goal of paradise. At this point ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫becomes applicable. Until this stage there is no ⟪ar:تکلیف⟫(responsibility) on the individual, as responsibility comes with the intellect. This is the reason why in Islam ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫ does not apply to 3 types of people as the intellect is not present.
1. A child
2. An insane person
3. A person who is asleep until he wakes up

A child's intellect has not developed; an insane person, and someone who is asleep does not have control over his intellect - and so ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫ does not apply. However, as soon as the child develops its intellect, the insane regains his sanity, and the asleep wakes up, they are fully responsible for their acts, and capable of shouldering the burden of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫ . Their intellect is now able to understand the message of revelation.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/32/7341ad43-bf76-4e73-accd-0fdcd8a6a24d.jpg}}

In essence Quran enables us to achieve the state of "second" perfection. The first perfection is the state of ⟪ar:فطرۃ⟫  which is the original primordial state of **dormant**Islam - **dormant**submission exhibited by a new born child, which is based on faith and not backed up by any intellectual or rational knowledge. But now as an adult, equipped with the gift of intellect and reason, the goal becomes to return to that child-like nature of ⟪ar:فطرۃ⟫that we were created with. In other words, as infants we exhibited all the angelic qualities of a true believer without understanding and comprehension operating on our natural disposition. The goal now is to return to that same pure state of ⟪ar:اسلام⟫ (submission) once again with belief and a conviction that is backed up by knowledge and intellectual reasoning. To become "child-like" once again in our nature as adults and to give up our "childish" behaviors. This is the level of **second perfection**.

The next thing we must learn is that the spirit/soul contains a clear hierarchy. First is the ⟪ar:روح⟫itself. Then under that there is the intellect (⟪ar:العقل⟫), then the spiritual heart (⟪ar:قلب⟫), the soul (⟪ar:نفس⟫) and the body (⟪ar:جسم⟫). Intellect is lower in the hierarchy than the spirit. This is why human intellect cannot directly grasp the faculties of the ⟪ar:روح⟫ in the same way that it can understand the other components below it. Now if the intellect cannot understand the ⟪ar:روح⟫, that it is a component of, how can it possibly understand Allah who is the creator of that ⟪ar:روح⟫?

As discussed, the body is made from the earth and its elements and eventually returns back to the earth and is dispersed in the four elements. Due to their physical and chemical nature, the body has a natural affinity and attraction towards material objects. But in the end, just like this earth, our bodies are dead mass.

What brings it to life is the ⟪ar:نفس⟫, which is right above the body in the hierarchy. When we use words like "I", or "me", we are referring to this particular faculty of the ⟪ar:روح⟫, which is the ⟪ar:نفس⟫. Asif is the name that has been attributed to my ⟪ar:نفس⟫. Now the way in which the ⟪ar:نفس⟫interacts with the body is through the acquisition of pleasurable or painful sensations that are generated via the 5 senses. It sees something beautiful, smells a delightful fragrance, tastes something delicious, hears a melodious sound, or feels something nice, it stores that sensual experience as pleasure. If it interacts with something ugly, smelly, disgusting, repulsing or discomforting, it stores that information as pain.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/111c3b00-76da-4762-897e-91afd517221d.jpg}}We then looked at the 3 states of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫. We studied ⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ بالسوء⟫, which is the soul that commands to evil; ⟪ar:نفس الوامۃ⟫, the self blaming soul; and ⟪ar:نفس المطمأنۃ⟫, which is the content or the satisfied soul. We learnt that ⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ⟫is the lowest state, while ⟪ar:نفس المطمأنۃ⟫ is the highest state, and then there is the ⟪ar:لوامۃ⟫in the middle that is struggling to move upward, but is being pulled down by the ⟪ar:الامارۃ⟫. And so directly within the model of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫, we discover the human potential in terms of his upward movement, and also its degradation in terms of its downward movement.

Most people are in a state of conflict. Many have submitted completely to this downward motion. And there are others who are struggling to move up, realizing that they "**should**want to" move up. And then there are the very few who are actually moving up.

And the way in which we were designed and commanded by Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), is to live in harmony with these three spheres within us. We were created to live in harmony with all three of them. And what creates the harmony between them is the faculty of the highest state called ⟪ar:قوۃ العقلیۃ⟫ - the faculty of reason. This is the faculty that is meant to rule over the mid brain and the reptilian brain. It's purpose is to hold them, and constrain them, and to use them as vehicles - just as the rider controls his beast. Because a wild horse is of no benefit to its rider. But a horse that is saddled, and bridled, and is fed only what it needs, so that it does not grow fat and become useless - that horse can be of great benefit to the rider. It's the exact same thing with these two lower aspects of ⟪ar:نفس⟫. If they are bridled and controlled, they will serve us. If they become unbridled, and left without restraint, then they rule over us - they take control of us, because:
انَّ الھوی، أِذا ما  تولَّی، یُسمِیُ او یَصِمِی

When passion takes control, it either destroys or defiles. So this is how Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) designed the ⟪ar:نفس⟫.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/4eaf4d6f-0dcf-421f-85f3-4cccc1d042b1.jpg}}

And then on top of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫is the ⟪ar:قلب⟫which is the spiritual heart. This spiritual heart lies at the center of this spiritual hierarchy, which makes it the most **essential**component of our existence. It is the king! Everything else (⟪ar:نفس، عقل، جسم⟫) are subjects within its kingdom. Because the spiritual heart is the legislative component! This is the component that commands the ⟪ar:نفس⟫to do things. Notice, I'm not saying this is the most **important**component! No! That would be the intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫)! I'm saying that the spiritual heart ⟪ar:قلب⟫is the most **essential**component - and intellect, despite its critical role in this hierarchy, is still one of its subject - because the heart can overrule the decision of the intellect. This is why it's right at the center - because a circle cannot exist without a center. I say center, because there is the intellect and the spirit above it; and there is the soul and the body below it.

Now what this spiritual heart desires to do in its default state, is to constantly recreate pleasure and avoid pain. And so, it puts the soul to work, which in turn interacts with this material world, in order to create and experience those pleasurable sensations over and over again. In this state, human beings are exactly identical to animals. Because animals operate based on instinct - and just like us, their instinct is to acquire pleasure and avoid pain. An animal feels hunger, it hunts. When it sees its mate and feels the urge, it satisfies its lust. This instinct comes from the R-Brain which is reactionary in its nature. It only knows how to react. So at this base level, a human begin is identical to a dog, or a monkey or a lizard. And there are many people in this world that are operating at this base level. We see it all around us. There are people in this so called "civilized" world that will eat anything - snakes, lizards, insects, cockroaches, monkey brain - anything. To them, snake tastes just like chicken, because they're both meat. So it makes absolutely no difference. There are people that will sleep with anyone they feel attracted to - regardless of age or gender or any sense of responsibility. And so you have the homosexuals, the bisexuals, and the transsexuals. Even in heterosexuals - you have people having sex with multiple partners - at the same time! See animals don't do that! But a human being will actually choose to fall below the level of animals - and the Quran verifies this:
The Criterion [25:44]
[ سورۃ ۲۵] الفرقان

‏أَمْ تَحْسَبُ أَنَّ أَكْثَرَهُمْ يَسْمَعُونَ أَوْ يَعْقِلُونَ ۚ إِنْ هُمْ إِلَّا كَٱلْأَنْعَمِ ۖ بَلْ هُمْ أَضَلُّ سَبِيلًا ‎۴۴
Do you think that most of them hear or understand? They are only like animals. **No! they are even worse!**They stray away from the true path, farther than animals do.

Animals have limits. But when it comes to human beings - there are no limits. Sky is the limit! Except that mankind is not headed in an upward direction - we're not heading towards ⟪ar:درجات⟫ - we're heading towards ⟪ar:درکات.⟫ We are climbing down the ladder in the name of modernism. Because all mankind is able to perceive today is the ⟪ar:ظاھر⟫- the external form of things. That's what animals are limited to. Animals cannot understand the essence of things. They do not possess the ability to understand the ⟪ar:باطن⟫ - so they're forced to limit themselves to the outward forms. But they at least have an excuse! They don't have intellects! But when we as human beings, **with**our gifts of intellect, when we choose to follow the example of animals, we can actually fall much lower than them.

# Spiritual heart

Today I want to talk about the spiritual heart (⟪ar:قلب⟫), and explain why Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) gave it such a central position in the hierarchy of the spirit. So what do we know about this spiritual heart? The most prominent characteristic of the heart, is its craving for attachment. The heart attaches to anything that it finds pleasing or comforting. In its default state, which is lust, these would be material things, or human beings in an objectified sense. Now the question is, Is this why Allah created the heart? So that it could attach itself to lifeless material objects? Or does this attachment have a loftier target?

Because we know that every single thing in this world has a primary purpose, even if it can be used for secondary purposes. For example, the primary purpose of a phone is communication. Now can it be used for other things, like surfing the internet, checking emails, and playing games? Sure! But the primary function of a phone is to make calls. So there is always this distinction between primary and secondary purpose.

So who is that central and primary object of attachment that the heart was created for? And how do we even begin to identify that target?

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/4a893e43-8aaa-4e1b-9a2a-87705fc3cf49.jpg}}To answer this question, Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in his divine wisdom, he made the physical heart, a symbol for that spiritual heart. So today we're going to study the physical heart in order to understand things about the spiritual heart.

We all know that the physical heart is **the** most astonishing organ of the body. We now know, that the human heart is pulsating approximately 40 million times a year. In an average human lifespan, the human heart pulsates about 3 billion times. It moves about 40 gallons of blood in our body. If you get 40 buckets of water - not 40 gallons - just 40 buckets of water; and you start moving those buckets from one room to another, you'll see how quickly you get tired, right? And yet this extraordinary organ that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) has put into our bodies, it continues to pump blood throughout our lives, effortlessly, never taking a break; not asking us for anything in return; not even asking us to tell it to pump this blood for us.

And what's even more unusual, is that we now know, that the human heart is not only self-regulating, it begins beating even before there is a central nervous system. During embryonic development, the heart is one of the few organs that must function almost as soon as it is formed. And the fact that the physical heart is one of the earliest organs to develop, speaks to its importance. The human heart begins to beat and pump blood through the embryo around the 22nd day of pregnancy. Who made the heart beat before there was even a central nervous system? Who made the heartbeat? The one who created the heart made it beat! and then taught it to sustain its beat. Why? **Because it is the heart that has been created to recognize Allah**. That's why it is the first thing that develops,  as a sign, to show us that this is the most essential organ of the entire body - because it is only this heart, that is one day going to recognize its master. It is this heart, that is going to find the ultimate "one".

Where do you think, the human need of finding **the one** comes from? Everyone is searching for **the one** in terms of their life partner or soulmate. Where did this idea of **the one**come from? It came from this need of the heart to find the ultimate one. Because Allah is the ultimate beloved that the heart was created to attach to. And the only way the heart can find satisfaction (⟪ar:اطمأنان⟫) is through the remembrance of Allah
The Thunder [13:28]
[ سورۃ ۱۳] الرعد

‏ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ وَتَطْمَىِٕنُّ قُلُوبُهُم بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ ۗ 
أَلَا بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ تَطْمَىِٕنُّ ٱلْقُلُوبُ ‎۲۸
Those who have believed and whose hearts are satisfied by the remembrance of Allah . 
Indeed! It is by the remembrance of Allah hearts are satisfied".

Now if you look at this word ⟪ar:قلب⟫, it is spelled ⟪ar:ق ل ب.⟫ Now if you take these letters and you switch them around, you get the word ⟪ar:قبل⟫, which means to come before, or to precede; to be the first and foremost.  And there is this science in the Arabic language, that when you switch the letters of a word to form a new word -

for example you have a word like ⟪ar:علم⟫which means knowledge. When you switch the same letters around you get the word ⟪ar:عمل⟫, which means action. Because knowledge is very closely related to action.

So in Arabic when the letters of a word are switched around to form a different word, they're always closely connected in meaning. This is another miraculous aspect of the Arabic language. So ⟪ar:علم⟫and ⟪ar:عمل⟫are related, and ⟪ar:قلب⟫and ⟪ar:قبل⟫- (heart and to precede) are related. Because heart is what takes precedence in the spiritual hierarchy of the human being in recognizing its master.

And then from the root word ⟪ar:قبل⟫comes the word ⟪ar:قبلۃ⟫, which means direction. And a direction is always pointed towards a destination. If there is no destination, there is no need for a direction. So then what is the direction of the heart?  Who is the destination of the heart? Who is this heart yearning for? Who is it pointing to? Who is it taking precedence for? Who is it beating for? Who does it want to attach to?

It's doing it for the **one**that it knew about before it knew anything else? It is searching for Allah! Because this spiritual heart knows something, that it knew even before it came into this realm. You see the heart knows!!  ⟪ar:الست بربکم؟⟫ The heart knows the answer to that question. Am I not your master?⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫  - Indeed we testify that you are. So every human heart that came into this world knows this truth, and so it can recognize that truth when it sees it. I bet you're feeling it right now - because I'm feeling it.

So Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) made us witness his glory and his majesty in the spiritual realm; and then sent us into this physical realm, to once again recognize his mastery and lordship over us through his signs. What signs? The signs that he created for us in the horizons and our soul.
Fusilat [41:53]
[ سورۃ ۴۱] فصلت

‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۗ أَوَلَمْ يَكْفِ بِرَبِّكَ أَنَّهُۥ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ شَهِيدٌ ‎۵۳
 We will show them Our Signs in the universe, and in their own selves, until it becomes manifest to them that this is the truth. Is it not sufficient in regard to your Lord that He is a Witness over all things?

Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) has placed these signs in creation to remind us of him. And our job now is to recognize him through these signs, and prove the sincerity of our testimony ⟪ar:صدقاً من قلب⟫ - with the sincerity of our hearts. We have to prove our sincerity of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫. There is no deity except Allah. Because this world stands before us as an ⟪ar:الہ⟫.

The word ⟪ar:الہ⟫comes from ⟪ar:اَلَہَ یَعلُہُ⟫and the word ⟪ar:اَلَہَ⟫literally means the entity that is the focus of one's worship and obedience.

Allah has designed the heart for attachment; and he created this world that offers so many things, that are perfect candidates for the heart to attach itself to. They come before us as gods and they make us submit to them. But even though they present themselves as gods, they appear to be ⟪ar:الہ⟫, they are false gods; they are lifeless gods. And what are lifeless gods called? They are called idols! These are lifeless idols that we create with our own hands. We decide that we will worship the idol of wealth! We build the idol of greed and bow down before it. We do that! Nobody does that for us. We create and choose our own idols. And there is no shortage of idols that this world offers. It offers the idol of power, the idol of fame, the idol of lust, the idol of beauty, the idol of wealth, the idol of strength, the idol of fashion, the idol of entertainment, musicians and actors. There are numerous man-made, lifeless idols that the heart can worship and obey! Or it can reject them all and worship Allah - who is not a lifeless god; who is not a man-made god. He is a living God! and not just a living, but an "ever-living" god. He is ⟪ar:الحی القیوم⟫ - the ever living and the eternal maintainer. He is a life **giving**God. These idols, they take away our soul. They force us to sacrifice ourselves at their alter. But Allah is the one who gives us, helps us, provides for us.

So by design, the heart can become a ⟪ar:غلام⟫of these lifeless idols, or it can choose to become a ⟪ar:عبد⟫ (a free slave) of the ever-living. It has a choice! This is why we have been given the **freedom of choice** over every other creation in this world. This is the test of human existence. Freedom of choice, is the test! To see who we choose! Freedom of choice is a test, to prove the sincerity of that primordial question - ⟪ar:الست بربکم؟⟫  Am I not your master? See... it's easy to choose Allah as our master, when there isn't another option. In the spiritual realm there was nothing other than Allah! So when he asked the question, we all naturally responded with the only possible answer -⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫ - indeed we testify that you are our master. That was easy to do - because that was the only option available.

But things are different in this world, aren't they? Because this world is full of distractions. This world is full of pleasures and comforts. And sincerity is always tested in conflict. It's easy to be friends with someone when things are good. When is the sincerity of that friendship tested? In times of need! A friend in need, is a friend in deed, isn't it? So acknowledging Allah as ⟪ar:رب⟫was easy in the spiritual realm where there were no other distractions; where there was nothing else for the heart to attach to.

So Allah designed this world, and created us within it, and gave us freedom of choice. He put us to the test - Am I still your master in the midst of all of these distractions? Or are you going to choose this filthy ⟪ar:دنیا⟫over me? And this is a continuous test! Because after every idol there is another idol. After every temptation there is another temptation. You break one idol, there will be a bigger one, a stronger one, standing before you that you will need to break again. And this constant purification has been symbolized by the heartbeat!

Why does the heart beat? Why does it go from contraction to expansion? Diastolic - systolic, you see? Why does it do that? . There are lots of ways you could get the blood around to the different parts of the body, without a beat. Why did Allah make it a beat? And then why did Allah make the breadth expansion and contraction? Doctors can't answer that. Because medical science can only explain the how - that's all science can do. But the Quran explains the why! The heart expands and contracts, because one of the names of Allah is ⟪ar:الباسط⟫- he is the expander. He is ⟪ar:الباسط⟫and he is ⟪ar:القابض⟫. He is the expander and the contractor. And that's why the heart beats. It is in a constant state of expansion and contraction, because it is testifying! What is it testifying? That there is no ⟪ar:الہ⟫except Allah - ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/adc6001c-8000-4dd2-ae8a-75ab03fb0787.jpg}}

Look at the statement - ⟪ar:لا الہ⟫ - that's the contraction! That's the negation of all false deities, all false ⟪ar:الہ⟫. It is breaking down and rejecting all forms of false lifeless gods; contracting the space; removing and squeezing out every drop of deoxygenated blood, until there is no false ⟪ar:الہ⟫left in our lives. That is ⟪ar:لا الہ⟫ - complete contraction. And then it is followed by an expansion - ⟪ar:الا اللہ⟫. Where the spiritual heart is then filled with the life giving oxygen of faith. Filled with the recognition and the awareness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) - that's expansion. This is what the physical heart is continuously engaged in - purification! Replacing deoxygenated blood with oxygenated blood,  which is a perfect metaphor for what the spiritual heart was designed to do. To replace ⟪ar:کفر⟫with ⟪ar:ایمان⟫, and then pump that oxygenated blood of ⟪ar:ایمان⟫to every single tissue, every single muscle of our spiritual practices of obedience and worship.

Isn't that incredible? The spiritual heart is constantly beating to the tune of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫. It is contracting and expanding to testify to a statement that is made up of contraction and expansion. SubhanAllah! You cannot make this stuff up! And this is why it is the spiritual heart, and not the intellect that is the component of cognition.
The Thunder [13:28]
[ سورۃ ۱۳] الرعد

أَلَا بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ تَطْمَىِٕنُّ ٱلْقُلُوبُ ‎۲۸
Indeed, It is only by the remembrance of Allah hearts are satisfied - *Not the intellect - but the heart!*

# Sound of the heart

Now when you hear the heart through a stethoscope, what sound does it make? Doctors call that sound lubb-a-dubb right? They say it's making a lubb-a-dubb sound. But when one of the Imams was asked, "I hear my heart beating and it seems to me like it's saying something. What is my heart saying?", and the Imam replied, "It's saying Allah, Allah, Allah". Now if you actually listen to the sound through a device, you don't hear the word Allah right? You hear is lubb-a-dubb. So then what did Imam AS mean when he said it's saying Allah? Because that was not the statement of an ordinary man. Those were words of an Imam -⟪ar:کلام الامیر. امیر الکلام⟫ - Speech of a king, that is the king of all speech! So what does it mean that the heart is saying Allah? If you look at how this name Allah is spoken, you'll see that it is a also a contraction and an expansion. That's what the tongue is doing in order to pronounce the word Allah!

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/be7551f1-4531-4df8-87df-e3d63c4f6897.jpg}}All - lah   All-laah

That's what its doing. I want all of you to do it with me right now! Say the word Allah! And as you do, see what happens when you pronounce the ⟪ar:لام⟫? You have to push your tongue against the ceiling of your mouth - that's contraction. That's ⟪ar:لا الہ⟫.  And then you have to expand your tongue to complete the word. You bring it down, opening your mouth to complete the word - that's expansion! That's ⟪ar:الا اللہ⟫.  The name Allah is a contraction and expansion. ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫ is a contraction and expansion. In both cases, the contraction comes first and expansion comes second. Because the heart must be emptied out from all forms of false lifeless gods, before it can recognize the one true ever living God! The cup has to be completely emptied, before it can be filled again with something new. And Allah placed all of this in the metaphor of the heart. It is constantly and continuously calling out to Allah because the heart remembers its master. See the ⟪ar:نفس⟫(the soul) has forgotten who the master is - because it has become tainted with the ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. The ⟪ar:نفس⟫has become so engaged in trying to recreate the sensations of the body through this material world, that it has forgotten its one true lord. And this is why Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) sent messengers, with a clear message to remind us who our true master is, so that we can return to him. And the heart recognizes the truth of that message.

But the thing about the heartbeat, is that it is very subtle. Even though our hearts are beating; they're constantly contracting and expanding but we're not consciously aware of it. And that's the problem with us Muslims today. Our spiritual hearts are dead. We went into auto-pilot a long time ago; and now we're in this state of ⟪ar:غفلۃ⟫-  heedlessness. That's what a pilot does when he gets up in the air. If you ever open that cockpit door you'll have a heart attack. Because they're snoring! They're in deep sleep, and the plane is on auto pilot. And that's what has happened to us. We've gone into this mechanical mode with Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). We say ⟪ar:اللہ اکبر⟫ (Allah is great) without actually meaning it in any way shape or form. We begin our SALAAT and few minutes later we forget which ⟪ar:رکعۃ⟫we're in. Did i do one ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫or two ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫, right? And this is why everything in Islam begins with an intention. A ⟪ar:نیۃ⟫? Why is that? Because intentions controls our actions! And a ⟪ar:نیۃ⟫is a conscious reminder of what we're about to do. It's supposed to wake us up. It is supposed to pull us out of our auto-pilot mode and make us mindful to what we're about to do. This is why:
انما الاعمال بالنیات

Actions are based on intentions. Because without intention, the action can take on a lifeless mechanical form, you see? And where does intention lie?
محلُ النِّیَّۃ القلب

The place of intention is in the heart. So that's how it is supposed to happen. But we make the ⟪ar:نیۃ⟫ and we go to sleep - and then there are different levels and degrees  of sleep. That's why Maulana Ali said ⟪ar:الناس النیام⟫. People of this world are in a state of sleep.

# Protection of the heart

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/26f37f1b-609b-47cc-817b-12f7f15f6ad3.jpg}}

So I want all of us to truly recognize today, that our spiritual heart is the most precious gift that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) has given us - and we all must protect this precious gift at all cost. How do we know we must protect it?  Just look where Allah put it in the human body. He put it behind the sternum, which is the strongest thing in the human body. And as if that wasn't good enough, he then built a rib cage around it. He didn't put it out there like the genitals - where there is no protection. And that's why if you want to bring a man down in martial arts, that's the first thing they teach you. Go for the groin! Or they'll tell you to go for the abdomen. Because there is no protection there. And when it comes to defense, those are again the first two things they will tell you to always guard and protect. Because a successful attack on the groin or the abdomen will bring down the strongest man on the planet. And those are the very two things this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫goes after, to bring us down spiritually - the genital and the abdomen - lust for sex and appetites.  And so we must defend them at every cost if we want to remain standing in this fight against this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫.

And the genitals and the abdomen have been left exposed in the design of human beings, to reveal a very particular reality - that these two things are related to ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. Because ⟪ar:دنیا⟫is completely exposed. It is right there in front of you - within your reach. But ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫has been hidden, just as the heart is hidden behind the rib cage and the sternum. If ⟪ar:دنیا⟫is what you're after, you'll get it very easily. If ⟪ar:درکات⟫, if descent is what you desire, it will happen effortlessly. But not ⟪ar:درجات⟫. Ascension is not going to be easy. Achieving the ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫is not going to be effortless. It is going to take hard work. And this is why the heart is protected with barriers, just as the benefits of ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫are protected from the undeserving.

# Entrainment

So Allah protected the heart, and he then he gave it this oscillating power, that makes the blood flow into each single cell of the body with every pulse. That's the reason blood reaches every single cell of the body. And if you know anything about oscillatory physics - it is the study of entrainment. It is the science of how things entrain with other things. Entrainment happens when there is a rhythm, which causes other things to gradually synchronize with it. When the rhythm of an oscillating device synchronizes the rhythm of another device, that's called entrainment .

We know soldiers entrain naturally when they march. It becomes hard to walk with someone out of step. You naturally entrain and sync up with the steps of the other person, right? And that's why soldiers naturally entrain. And what does entrainment results in? It results in **amplification**- which is even more frighting. You know what happens to a bridge when soldiers walk in entrainment, right? That synchronization creates an amplification that can collapse the bridge. That's the first thing taught to every engineer. When soldiers walk on a bridge in one step, the force of the weight gets amplified exponentially. because it was all together you see? And that's the power of being unified. When you are unified you have power. And when you are disunited you are weak.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/28fe9096-3299-41d6-9e58-2a14986f66d4.jpg}}

Now its the same thing when it comes to the spiritual heart. The more we understand the meanings of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫ - the more our intellect, our soul and our body will entrain with it. And when this entrainment occurs, when everything gets synchronized, there is exponential amplification in the form of actions. The intensity of every action changes. And that's why Rasul Allah said.
قال رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ وآلہ و سلم
اِنَّ فِی الجَسَدِ مُضغَۃً۔ وَأِذَا صَلَحَت صَلَحَ الْجَسَدُ کُلُّہُ، وَأِذَا فَسَدَتْ فَسَدَ الْجَسَدُ کُلُّہُ۔ اَلا وَ ھِیَ القَلْبُ
Indeed within the body of a human being, there is a lump of flesh. When it is sound, the entire body is sound; and when it is corrupt the entire body is corrupt. Know that it is the heart

Why? Because the entire body is in entrainment with the heart. If the heart is diseased, the entrainment will be diseased. But if the heart is sound, the entrainment will be sound. Have you guys seen what happens when sunlight passes through a magnifying glass? All the rays converge at a single point. And what happens as a result of that? heat! fire!, right? It's the same sunlight we're all exposed to during the day, but it doesn't burn. Because those rays are disunited. But when they all unite and converge at a single point, the strength and intensity of those rays are magnified and they can now penetrate through material objects, isn't it? That's the amplification and magnification that is required in our spiritual lives - where all these components of the RUH - the intellect, the soul and the body are in perfect entrainment with the heart.

# Salaat

Now how do we do that? Is there a mechanism, a practice that allows us to practice this entrainment? There is! And that practice is ⟪ar:صلوٰۃ⟫. The five daily prayers are nothing more than a practical manifestation of this entrainment of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫. If you look at the design of ⟪ar:صلوٰۃ⟫, it begins with contraction. It begins with the rejection and the abandonment of whatever you were doing. Whatever worldly need, desire, pleasure or passion is trying to become your ⟪ar:الہ⟫, you are rejecting it. You are breaking that idol with the sword of ⟪ar:لا الہ⟫, and you are turning your face in the direction of Allah. At least that's what should happen. That's the first thing we do in SALAAT. We direct the heart (the QALB) towards Allah, and we direct the body towards the QIBLA.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/51e53964-3218-4cab-9df7-fe246716f7c4.jpg}}
أُقابل الطیف بالطیف والکثیف بالکثیف

I am facing my heart towards Allah and my body towards the KAABA. It begins with the heart, and then the rest of the body follows. Every action within prayer is directed by the heart. The heart believes Allah is worthy of praise and honor, the tongue says ⟪ar:سبحان ربی العظیم و بحمدہ⟫- and the body bows in ⟪ar:رُکوع⟫as physical proof of the sincerity of that statement and belief. The heart believes that Allah is the only one worthy of prostration, the tongue affirms by saying ⟪ar:سبحان ربی الاعلی و تعالی⟫ and the body falls into prostration to prove the sincerity of that statement and belief.

What does SALAAT culminate in? It culminates in ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫. What happens in ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫? We put our head on the ground. What does the head contain? the brain, which symbolizes the spiritual intellect - our reasoning faculty. ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫is an action in which we submit our intellects before our master. It is a declaration of the fact, that Oh Allah! our intellects are incapable of understanding you; our intellects are incapable of deciphering your divine will. You are our master and we are your free slaves - and only a benevolent master like you knows what's best for his slave. And as we are in this state of ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫, as we lower our intellects before Allah, which organ do we elevate in the process?  The heart! SubhanAllah! Is that incredible or what? During our entire life, when we're standing, sitting, lying down - our intellects always remain above the heart isn't it? ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫is the only deliberate, volitional action, in which we choose to lower and submit our intellects before Allah, in order to elevate our hearts. Submission to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) is what elevates the spiritual heart - all of this beautifully captured in the act of ⟪ar:صلوٰۃ⟫.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/39/1afe32df-c347-49fe-8503-c7fd42b6e733.jpg}}

Now in the beginning of today's session I told you that the heart is designed for attachment. And it is restless for that attachment. It tries to attach itself to the false lifeless idols of this world, but it's beat doesn't end. It still remains restless. It does not find ⟪ar:اطمأنان⟫. It does not find satisfaction. When does the heart finally come to rest? When does it finally beat stopping? At the moment of death. What is death? It is the moment of finally meeting Allah. And what happens to the heart when it finally meets its beloved? It stops beating. It comes to rest - it finally finds ⟪ar:سکون⟫- it finds ⟪ar:اطمأنان⟫. It finds such an immense peace and satisfaction that it never feels the need to beat again. It never becomes restless again; because it has found the one it was searching for. It has reached its final destination - which is the beatific vision of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

<!-- section 2 (id=88, raw_sort=2): Reason vs Intellect -->

# Spirit of the intellect

The book begins by explaining the concept of ⟪ar:الروح العقل⟫  - the "spirit of the intellect".

## RUH - ⟪ar:الروح⟫

The primary linguistic meaning of "⟪ar:روح⟫" is related to notions of wind (⟪ar:رِیح⟫), breath, and life force due to its subtle nature.

Just to quickly recap, ⟪ar:روح⟫ is that primordial energy that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created in the spiritual realm. It is what witnessed its master and testified for his lordship. Allah presented himself to us in the spiritual realm and asked us this fundamental question:⟪ar:ألست بربکم؟⟫ Am I not your master? And we all replied ⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫ - We witness and we testify that indeed you are our master! So the ⟪ar:روح⟫ is that essential, primordial energy that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created in the spiritual realm. This ⟪ar:روح⟫ then joins our body in this physical realm in a dormant state; and with time, its various faculties begin to awaken in the form of the soul (⟪ar:نفس⟫) which is our consciousness or ego. Then the spiritual heart (⟪ar:قلب⟫) and the spiritual intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫) gradually develop as we mature. But these are all components of that single ⟪ar:روح⟫ that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created in the spiritual realm. The various faculties of that ⟪ar:روح⟫ have been classified with these terms of ⟪ar:نفس⟫and ⟪ar:قلب⟫and ⟪ar:عقل⟫, so that we can properly understand its capabilities.

The function of the spirit is to unify the disparate components of the body. The body is composed of various different parts - the eyes, nose, ears, hands, feet. They all have their own individual and distinct function. The spirit unifies them by putting them into action for achieving a unified goal. So if the spirit wants to read a book, the feet will walk towards the shelf, the hands will pick up the book, fingers will turn the page, eyes will read the lines, break will interpret the meanings of those words. These are all different functions coming together through different parts of the body in order to achieve a unified function - which is to read the book.

In this book, the author coins a new term that he calls ⟪ar:الروح العقل⟫  - the 'spirit of the intellect', to explain the relationship between the intellect and the spirit.  He demonstrates how the intellect is directly related to the Quranic concept of ⟪ar:روح⟫; and then he explores some of the fruits of engaging deeply with this unifying spirit in proper relation with the intellect. Now how do we understand this spirit of the intellect?

He explains that there are 3 factors that are essential to understanding the spirit of the intellect:

1. First, by understanding the distinction between the intellect and reason.

2. Second, by appreciating the centrality of revelation - that every spiritual action revolves around the teachings of Quran. In other words, intellectually understanding the essence of the principles of Quran, and not just the superficial outward actions.

3. Finally, by understanding and creating a harmony between revelation and the intellect.

Once we accomplish these 3 things - once we understand what the intellect is, center it around revelation, and harmonize it with the teachings and principles of the Quran, we generate what is called ⟪ar:الروح العقل⟫ - the spirit of the intellect.

# Intellect vs Reason

So let's begin with understanding the difference between reason and intellect. When we talk about the intellect in the general sense, we limit it to the logical and rational understanding of realities. That's how we define intellect, right? The faculty of reasoning and understanding. But he defines the intellect as a faculty, that not only "comprises" the activities of the rational mind, but "surpasses" it to include spiritual areas such as moral behavior and aesthetic sensibilities. He emphasizes the idea, that rather than looking at the intellect as an independent faculty, to look at it as a unified whole with the spirit itself - which is what I explained in my sessions as well, that it's all one energy. Intellect is simply a sub-component of that energy.

{{IMG_URL:http://localhost:786/Resources/IMAGES/88/38dcca16-5ba3-4455-98e3-0d1a11d6fe43.jpg}}

Then he explains the difference between the words "intellect" (⟪ar:العقل⟫) and "reason" (⟪ar:المنطق⟫) - because these words are generally used interchangeably in conversations. But they're not actually the same. Even in English, the word intellect comes from the Latin word *intellectus*, which means "understanding", and it has to do with an overall concept that is built upon a perception. If you look at this word "under-standing" - to stand under, right? What happens when you stand under something? That thing overshadows you! Our perception of realities overshadows our actions. Isn't that true? Our actions are performed under the shadow of our rational perceptions. Because actions are governed by how we perceive realities. You change someone's perception - you make him stand "under" a different perception - you give him a different understanding, and his actions change accordingly! And so the intellect is the tool that provides us a means to "stand under" a perception of reality, developed through reason. Intellect is the faculty that is capable of a direct contemplative vision of all the transcendent realities that the Quran speaks about. The Quran tells us about all these metaphysical realities that exist, but are hidden from our senses - realities such as Allah, angels, jinn, devils, heaven, hell, day of judgment - all these things. And so what the intellect does, is that it places us "under" the perception of these metaphysical realities, which then generates faith in our spiritual hearts and in turn governs our actions.

Now how does the intellect do that? It does it through reason.

The word reason comes from the Latin word "*ratio*".  Now what does ratio mean? It's a relation between two numbers, right? It's a comparison! So **reason** is of an indirect, discursive nature, that digresses from subject to subject. Reason is related to discourse and debates, because it functions through a binary logic - 1s and 0s - negatives and positives, do's and don'ts, pros and cons etc. So for example, you begin your thought process with an idea. That's how a thought works. It begins with an idea, a concept that conveys some intelligible meaning. Your idea has to bear some relation to the realities they express, otherwise they could turn into terrible ideas...

Hmmm... maybe I should try flying from the roof of my building! Maybe I should try banging my head against this brick wall to see if I can get through it

Those are not great ideas! Why? Because they are not aligned with the realities of gravity and physics, right? And it is our very understanding of these realities, that makes those very bad ideas. So the idea must have so intelligible meaning, and it must have some relation to the reality it expresses. So let's say you get this idea, that you should invest your money in certain stock. Or maybe you think about choosing a certain profession! The first thing you have to do is "define" that idea for yourself. What is it that I want? Right? You need a definition. Now the Arabic word for definition is ⟪ar:حد⟫ - which is the idea of creating a limitation or a boundary.  And how do you create that mental or conceptual boundary? Through a binary logic of pros and cons, dos and don'ts. You debate and dispute the idea using a series of pros and cons, in order to  "define" what you want to do. This is the process of reason - "ratio" - comparing two numbers - weighing the pros against the cons. You see?

Reason deals with the comprehension of the outward forms of things - the external factors. Reason can help us arrive, only at the mental concepts or the mental images of realities. But that's it! It cannot move any further.

Intellect however, is the internal, or spiritual aspect of that reason. The faculty that allows us to contemplate on those reasons and experience it, is the intellect. The intellect allows us to go beyond the limitations of reason and experience those realities. It is one thing to know about the presence of Allah, and it is an entirely different thing to experience the presence of Allah. You see the difference? There is knowledge and there is experience.

When you book a holiday, you go through the process of "reason" to decide where you want to go and why you want to go there; and you do that through a series of binary logic. But once you've made that decision, and you book your tickets, you start getting excited about it. You haven't reached your destination; you've not even left home yet; but the more you think about the trip, the more you contemplate over it, the more you begin to experience it and enjoy it. That experience happens through the intellect - supported by memories of previous events. So if you've experienced a beautiful sunrise on the ocean in the past, and you're planning a similar trip, that previous experience, combined with the "reason" for your next holiday, gets you all excited. Yes?

We might not remember Allah now, but we have experienced his beatific vision when we stood in his divine presence and heard him ask us - Am I not your master? We all witnessed him in his magnificent glory back then. That memory has been placed as a dormant seed in our spirit (our ⟪ar:روح⟫). Now in this world, our Messenger of Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam), through the divine revelation of Quran, provide us with the reasons or reminders to bring those seeds to life; they give us reasons to believe that he exists. They give us reason to believe that everything he has promised is true.  They give us reasons to believe that the beatific vision of his company is the most incredible experience that could possible exist. And so the more we reflect on that, the more we contemplate over it - the more we experience his divine presence in this world - even before actually meeting him in the ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫.  And the faculty that allows us to do that is the intellect.

So there is a subtle but clear distinction between "intellect" and "reason", which is very important, and it is the key to understanding the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali. So the author explains, that **reason** is the "tool" that the intellect employs to generate an experience, a perception, an understanding. I'll say that again - Reason is the "tool" , the "device of binary logic" that the intellect uses to come to know of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala); but **intellect** is the actual faculty that is capable of the experience of that beatific vision of Allah. However, that intellectual experience can only be generated through reason.
