{{IMG_URL:https://session.kashkole.com/Resources/IMAGES/88/68cecf61-129a-4993-994e-bffb605c7da5.jpg}}

This is why if you look at⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫ , it is a statement of reason - because it provides a binary logic of ⟪ar:لا الہ⟫- which is negation and ⟪ar:الا اللہ⟫ - which is affirmation. Together, they provide for us, the boundaries and definition of do's and don'ts; pros and cons, to operate within. But then, by the combination of the two, we generate the experience, benefits and pleasures of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫which is a function of the intellect. Makes sense?

So this is the first component for understanding "spirit" of the intellect - ⟪ar:روح العقل⟫ - the distinction between reason and intellect; because it is the intellect, that makes this discussion a spiritual one.

It's OK if these things feel a bit hazy in the beginning - InshaAllah it'll clear up as we move along. But as I said, you'll have to keep going back and revising this material - either using the website or your own notes, to develop a complete concept of what is being discussed.

# Centrality of Revelation

The next thing to understand the spirit of the intellect, is the central role that revelation plays for the intellect; because the intellect needs a center to revolve around. It needs certain principles, certain definitions that it can adhere to, for its proper guidance. What is that center? As Muslims we believe that center is the Quran. And  we can find a perfect demonstration of that centrality is the life example of Maulana Ali (AS). Every facet of Maulana Ali's life, and all of his teachings are centered around the Quran. In fact, the thought process, the mindset, and the life example of Maulana Ali can be seen as a kind of spiritual commentary of the Quranic text. This is why he was called the Quran in action. When he walked by, the companions would point to him and say "the Quran is walking by".  Because his life was literally seen as a dramatic unfolding of the inner meaning of the Quran - from his birth within the walls of Kaaba, to his martyrdom in ⟪ar:مسجد کوفی⟫. And this relationship between Ali and the Quran is something that was confirmed by a HADEES of the Prophet (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) in which he said:
 أنَّ علیٌ مع القرآن، و قرآن مع العلی

Indeed, Ali is with the Quran, and the Quran is with Ali. And then he said:
عَلِیُّ یُقَاتِلُ عَلیٰ تَاوِیلِہ کَما قَتَلُ عَلیَ تَنزِیلِہِ

Ali will fight for the interpretation of the Quran, as I have struggled for its revelation.  That's a really incredible statement! Ali will fight and struggle, to deliver to you the ⟪ar:تاویل⟫ of Quran, just as I have struggled and fought to bring you its ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫- its revelation. We all know about the enormous struggle that Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) had to go through, and the kinds of hardship he had to endure in order to bring this revelation to us. In the same way, if we study the short caliphate of Maulana Ali, we find that it was dominated by civil wars. There were three battles that were fought during his caliphate - ⟪ar:جمل، نحروان⟫ and ⟪ar:سفین⟫. And it can be argued, that all three battles were fought for the sake of enforcing the correct interpretation and application of the Quran. These were not political battles or power struggles as the ignorant say. All 3 battles were fought to clarify and enforce the fundamental message of ⟪ar:توحید⟫. To enforce the oneness on Allah according to the principles of Quran - at all spiritual and social levels - in thought, in speech and in action.

Now what's really interesting, is that just as the battle of ⟪ar:جمل⟫ was about to begin, a Bedouin came to Maulana Ali and said: ⟪ar:یا أمیر المومنین⟫ - What does it mean that Allah is one? Now I want you to imagine that for a second. Just imagine, I'm the leader of an army that is just about to begin fighting, and let's say you all are the soldiers in that army. The battle lines have been formed; and I'm about to give the "attack" command. And one of you kids come to me and say, "Hey Asif chacha, can you explain that session on ⟪ar:توحید⟫to me again?" How would I react? What the hell?! Are you crazy! We're about to march into battle and you want me to do the session for you right now?! Are you nuts! right? And this is exactly what the companions did. They started yelling at this bedouin - telling him that this was neither the time nor the place for such questions.

But Maulana Ali intervenes, and he says: 'Leave him alone! For indeed what the bedouin wishes to know - is what we wish for the people! This is what we are fighting for. We are fighting this battle for the proper implementation of Allah's ⟪ar:توحید⟫'. SubhanAllah!

And this is why, if we really want to understand what the centrality of revelation should mean in our lives, the best place to start, is by reflecting on the spirituality of Maulana Ali, and by considering the manner in which he responded to this bedouin. A response that **practically**demonstrates the principle of right intention taking precedence over outward actions - no matter what they may be. You see? For Maulana Ali, the most fundamental intention motivating his every action was his knowledge about the intention and the will of Allah; whether that action was religious in the general sense, or whether it was military, as in this case. He did not say, "This is not right right time to ask that question because we're in the middle of a battle!" Now if you argue this from a reason and logic point of view, then this was definitely not the right time to ask that question, isn't it? Had Maulana Ali used "reason", he would have agreed with the companions and said find a better time and place to ask this question. But you see, he went beyond the confines of reason and he used his "intellect". He saw beyond reason, to realize that the ⟪ar:توحید⟫, of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), lies at the very heart of every concerns, every motivation and every actions - whether that action is on the battlefield or on the prayer rug. Therefore there is no time, no place, no situation, no circumstance in which this question - "What do  you mean by saying Allah is one?" is irrelevant, or out of place.

And so on the very battlefield of ⟪ar:جمل⟫ right when the battle is about to begin, Maulana Ali delivers an incredible ⟪ar:خطبۃ⟫on the meaning of ⟪ar:توحید⟫- the oneness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). In that sermon he refutes the notion of Allah's oneness being in any way a 'numerical'  or 'countable' oneness. He tells the bedouin that it is not permissible to say 'Allah is one' while having any kind of a numerical concept in mind. He says, "The one who has no second cannot enter into the category of number". Because as soon as something can be qualified and quantified as "one", it automatically creates the possibility of a "two", "three", "four" etc. So the concept of Allah being "one" in the numerical sense becomes problematic in the understanding of ⟪ar:توحید⟫.  Then he explains, that the one who has no second, is not  just a concept of divinity, to which no other partner can be ascribed. Instead, **he is a reality which has no 'otherness'**. Do you get what that means? It's not that there are entities that could be partnered with Allah, but we as Muslims choose not to partner them. That's not what ⟪ar:توحید⟫is. The concept of ⟪ar:توحید⟫is the realization and the affirmation of the fact, that there is nothing in existence that has the "likeness" of Allah; there is nothing in existence that "can possibly be" partnered with him in anyway shape or form. Because he has no likeness; He has no otherness.

Now if you think about it, likeness and otherness can happen in either the "same" or "similar" species. So for example similarities exist between a parent and a child because they belong to the same species. But likeness can exist between other species as well - in shape or form. For example, a lion is a completely different specie from a dog or a goat or man - but they all share a similarity in the fact that they all have eyes and nose and mouth and limbs etc. This is why Quran refutes both types of similarities by saying:
‏لَمْ يَلِدْ وَلَمْ يُولَدْ

He begets not, nor was he begotten. He does not have a son, nor does he have a father. So he crushes the concept of likeness within the same species. And then he says:
‏وَلَمْ يَكُن لَّهُۥ كُفُوًا أَحَدٌ

And there is nothing in existence that can be compared to him in likeness or otherness; thereby eliminating the possibility of likeness with any other species. SubhanAllah!

And so Maulana Ali in his sermon explains Allah's oneness to this bedouin. I'm going to share a portion of this sermon with you - and I hope you can truly appreciate the depth and the eloquence of these statements. Because what he does is, he combines two opposing concepts to generate a third reality. Just as we use dos and don'ts to derive a conclusion, he uses the same dichotomy, by combining two entirely contradictory reasons to reveal the oneness of Allah. He says:
بسم اللہ الرحمن الرحیم
الْحَمْدُ للهِ الَّذِي بَطَنَ خَفِيَّاتِ الاْمُورِ، وَدَلَّتْ عَلَيْهِ أَعْلاَمُ الظُّهُورِ

Praise be to Allah who lies inside all hidden things, and towards whom all manifest things guide. He exists within the spirituality of meanings which are hidden; and is revealed through the physicality of words that are manifest. He exists through the metaphysical as well as the physical realities of existence.
وَامْتَنَعَ عَلَى عَيْنِ الْبَصِيرِ

He cannot be seen by the eyes of the one who can see;
فَلاَ عَيْنُ مَنْ لَمْ يَرَهُ تُنْكِرُهُ

and yet the eye which does not see him cannot deny his existence.
وَلاَ قَلْبُ مَنْ أَثْبَتَهُ يُبْصِرُهُ

and the heart that proves his existence cannot perceive Him.

You see the paradox there? He cannot be seen by the eye, but just because he cannot be seen, his existence cannot be denied. And even the heart that does see him, cannot properly perceive him. You see how he beautifully combines these opposing concepts to reveal realities about Allah? Through reason, by defining boundaries of what we can do and what we cannot do, he's creating an intellectual concept of who Allah is. This is incredible if you think about it.
سَبَقَ فِي الْعُلُوِّ فَلاَ شَيءَ أَعْلَى مِنْهُ

He is so high in his sublimity that nothing can be more sublime than him. And yet
وَقَرُبَ فِي الدُّنُوِّ فَلاَ شَيْءَ أَقْرَبُ مِنْهُ

an in closeness, he is so near that no one can be more closer than him. Watching our every single move; monitoring our every single breadth.

Qaf [50:16]
[ سورۃ ۵۰] ق

‏وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ وَنَعْلَمُ مَا تُوَسْوِسُ بِهِ نَفْسُهُۥ ۖ وَنَحْنُ أَقْرَبُ إِلَيْهِ مِنْ حَبْلِ ٱلْوَرِيدِ ‎۱۶
And indeed We have created man, and We know what his own self whispers to him. And We are closer to him than his jugular vein.

دوری کی یہ حد ہے کہ تصور بھی خطا ہے

اور قربت کا یہ عالم ہے کہ شہ رگ سے بھی نزدیک

Then he says something even more incredible:
فَلاَ اسْتِعْلاَؤُهُ بِاعَدَهُ عَنْ شَيْء مِنْ خَلْقِهِ

His sublimity does not put him at a distance from any of his creation, cause that's what happens when things become too sublime - they become inaccessible, isn't it? Once water turns into gas, it gets out of your reach. You can no longer hold it in your hand. So if Allah is so subtle and so sublime, he should be completely inaccessible - and yet...
وَلاَ قُرْبُهُ سَوَاهُمْ في المَكَانِ بِه

Nor does his closeness and nearness to creation bring them on an equal level to him. Now that is an amazing statement if you think about it. You cannot become close to someone that is completely out of reach. You cannot fall in love with someone that is not within your physical, spiritual, intellectual or conceptual reach. Allah is so sublime, so lofty, so out of reach, and yet we fall completely in love with him. He's not even in the same league as us; not even in the same playground; not even the same country; not even in the same continent; not even on the same planet; not even in the same galaxy. not even in the same universe. And yet

⟪ar:وَنَحْنُ أَقْرَبُ إِلَيْهِ مِنْ حَبْلِ ٱلْوَرِيدِ⟫ He is closer to us than our jugular vein. SubhanAllah! Then he says:
لَمْ يُطْلِعِ الْعُقُولَ عَلَى تَحْدِيدِ صِفَتِهِ

He has not revealed or disclosed the true extents of his divine qualities to the intellects. He has revealed a little something about himself to us, but not what he truly is.
ولَمْ يَحْجُبْهَا عَنْ وَاجِبِ مَعْرِفِتِهِ

And yet, he has not prevented us from securing the essential knowledge about him. SubhanAllah! He is beyond our capacity to know him, and yet he has enabled us to know about him.
 فَهُوَ الَّذِي تَشْهَدُ لَهُ أَعْلاَمُ الْوُجُودِ، عَلَى إِقْرَارِ قَلْبِ ذِي الْجُحُودِ

He is such, that all signs of existence stand witness for Him - to the extent that even the mind that denies him, is ultimately forced to testify for him.
 تَعَالَى اللهُ عَمَّا يَقولُ الْمُشَبِّهُونَ بِهِ وَالْجَاحِدُونَ لَهُ -  عُلوّاً كَبِيراً

Allah is sublime beyond what is described by those who liken him to things, or those strive against him. For he is sublime and exalted beyond comprehension.

So what he explains is that the Absolute Real (⟪ar:الحق⟫) is both transcendent and immanent at the same time. He is beyond all 'things', and is hidden from everything in existence, and yet at the same time, he is also manifested by all 'things'. He used the existence and attributes of his creation, to prove his own existence and powers. He is inaccessible and yet inalienable at the same time. Isn't this incredible? Can you appreciate the eloquence of these words? How in these very few words, he creates a shift of consciousness for us. He shifts the dogmatic notion of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫; from being this idea of one God versus many gods, to this concept of Allah being the one and only "Reality" - in the face of which  everything else becomes a mere illusion. Everything in existence, from the mole to the mountains, become "signs", they become ⟪ar:آیات⟫- they become these numerous outward phenomena calling out to us to be deciphered. Why? So that we can now retrace these outward appearances and manifestations, back to their original source - which is the literal meaning of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫.

The word ⟪ar:تاویل⟫linguistically means: Returning back to the ⟪ar:أول⟫ (the First)- ⟪ar:عَودَۃ الی الاول⟫.  To trace multiplicity back to its original source.

So the essence of this incredible sermon is to truly appreciate the dichotomy of existence. On one hand, he describes the 'Real' ⟪ar:الحق⟫ as being "the beginning and the end" of  all things (⟪ar:الاول و الآخر⟫), the apparent and the hidden (⟪ar:الظاھر و الباطن⟫)- that there is nothing "real" in existence (⟪ar:لا الہ⟫) but this one and absolute Reality (⟪ar:الا اللہ⟫), right? And on the other hand, he suggests that all apparent multiplicity and diversity in creation must be 'brought back' and 'returned' to his real and true oneness. And this is exactly what Allah tells us in the Quran:

Fusilat [41:53]
[ سورۃ ۴۱] فصلت

‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۗ أَوَلَمْ يَكْفِ بِرَبِّكَ أَنَّهُۥ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ شَهِيدٌ ‎۵۳
 We will show them Our Signs in the universe, and in their own selves, until it becomes manifest to them that this is the truth. Is it not sufficient in regard to your Lord that He is a Witness over all things?

So according to this verse everything within existence - outside us in the horizons (⟪ar:آفاق⟫) and everything within (and including) our own existence - they are all signs - they are ⟪ar:آیات⟫ that point back to ⟪ar:الحق⟫. Do you remember the linguistic meaning of the word ⟪ar:آیۃ⟫? What is an ⟪ar:آیۃ⟫?  It is something that is valuable, meaningful; and it is something that points to something or someone else. So everything that exists in the multiplicity and diversity of existence, is a valuable and meaningful sign pointing back to the absolute real. And retracing this diversity of creation, this multiplicity of hierarchies of cause and effects, back to its creator is the very concept of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫.

And this is a concept that requires deep reflection - to truly appreciate this ingenious design of our creator; that how he used multiplicity to help us understand his own unity - and then Maulana Ali (AS) goes on to crush that concept of unity by saying that it cannot be in any numerical sense. See... we could not have understood or appreciated the concept of unity without first understanding the concept of multiplicity and diversity, right? The only reason we understand what "one" means, is because we understand what "more than one" means. And this is why Allah created multiplicity and diversity for us, so that it could point towards his own unity; his own oneness. But then he further goes on to explain, that he is not one in the numerical sense - he is one in the "unique" sense.

The Unity [112:1-2]
[ سورۃ ۱۱۲] الاخلاص

‏قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ أَحَدٌ ‎۱‏ٱللَّهُ ٱلصَّمَدُ ‎۲

Say he is Allah the unique! Not one! Not ⟪ar:قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ وأَحَدٌ -⟫Say he is Allah the one; rather ⟪ar:قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ أَحَدٌ⟫Say he is Allah the unique. ⟪ar:ٱللَّهُ ٱلصَّمَدُ⟫ - Allah, the impenetrable. The word ⟪ar:صمد⟫ means impenetrable - something that has no gaps - like a boulder, or a brick of gold that is solid through and through. You cannot find a way inside. There is no way to get to know Allah in anyway, shape or form. Not physically, not scientifically, not spiritually, not intellectually, not conceptually, not mathematically, not theoretically, - there is absolutely no way of knowing him entirely - and yet knowing him is a must! SubhanAllah!

So this is the second factor that generates the spirit of the intellect. Our intellects must be anchored to revelation - in every single thought, motivation and action; just as every act of Maulana Ali was centered around the principles and teachings of Quran. And the ultimate goal of the Quran is to teach us the ⟪ar:توحید⟫ of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). Every act that the Quran teaches us to do in terms of our worship, every decision the Quran asks us to make, every sacrifice the Quran demands, they're all centralized around the ⟪ar:توحید⟫ of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala)

<!-- section 3 (id=89, raw_sort=3): Centrality Of Revelation -->

# Union Of Dichotomy

In the previous session we started looking at the 3 components that play an essential role in understanding, what the author calls the spirit of the intellect (⟪ar:روح العقل⟫). And basically he coins this term to explain the relationship between the spirit and the intellect, and how the intellect is brought to life through revelation, and the knowledge of ⟪ar:توحید⟫.

IReason Intellect
{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/89/5f7ca89a-7861-466e-9fb7-b472c7e88ba5.jpg}}

He begins by explaining the difference between "reason" and "intellect". Reason (⟪ar:منطق⟫), which comes from the Latin word "ratio", is what helps us define, or set boundaries (⟪ar:حد⟫) around ideas, through a series of negatives and positives, do's and don'ts, pros and cons, etc. Intellect (⟪ar:العقل⟫) on the other hand, combines those reasons to then generate a unified concept or a particular understanding. Reason, facilitates the process of thinking, by setting boundaries for thought through these antinomies of negatives and positives; and what emerges out of their union, is the final picture in the form of an intellectual understanding or a concept.

IXYZ
{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/89/52e00cc4-765f-4938-9aef-76f2c4851758.jpg}}

He gives the example of the 3 dimensions of length, breadth and depth to explain this concept. He compares length and breadth to the dichotomous analysis of reason; because length and breadth are on opposite planes - horizontal as opposed to vertical; and he compares depth to the intellect which is diagonal line on a graph, incorporating both, the vertical as well as the horizontal aspect of the other 2 dimensions. Then he argues that you cannot have depth, without the opposing union of length and breadth; and without depth, what you have is a flat, bilateral image that does not represent reality. Because reality exists in 3D. So he says that just as the dimension of depth can only emerge from the union of length and breadth, in the same way intellectual understanding can only result from the dichotomy of negation and affirmation of reason.

And this principle is not just limited to the process of thought. For example, even if you look at the Arabic root word for reason, which is ⟪ar:منطق⟫, it comes from the root ⟪ar:نُطق⟫. And what does ⟪ar:نُطق⟫mean? It means speech. Now speaking is an **active**act**,**that requires listening, which is **passive**in its nature. So you have those two opposites there as well; and by their union the third reality of communication emerges.

Similarly, when two opposite forces of a husband and a wife unite, their union gives birth to a third reality in the form of a child.

And if we look at our own existence, we are comprised of 2 components. We have a spirit, which is an **active**energy, and we  have a body, which is **passive**matter. We know that our spirit cannot operate in this world without the body; while the body is just dead matter without the spirit. But then together, by their union, we get an existence in this world. So we see this marvelous consistency in Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala)'s design all around us. These are all completely different examples, but they're working on the exact same principles of reason and intellect.

# Multiplicity vs Singularity

Then he parallels reason and intellect to the concepts of multiplicity and unity. He says you will always find multiplicity and diversity in reason. There will always be multiple reasons by which you can evaluate something, but the goal of all those reasons is to generate that one single concept, one idea, one decision - a unified solution to the problem.

IMultiplicity to singularity
{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/89/6b53ce72-1e16-425f-b794-cf324066ae3d.jpg}}

Because Quran, in reality, is a book of reasons! It is a book of numerous reasons. It comprises of over 6000 verses that have to do with do's and don'ts, right? But the essence, the spirit of all those verses boils down to one single statement - ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫- to signify and reveal the unity and oneness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). So he relates this phenomenon to the concepts of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫and ⟪ar:تاویل⟫based on the HADEES of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) that:
عَلِیُّ یُقَاتِلُ عَلیٰ تَاوِیلِہ کَما قَتَلُ عَلیَ تَنزِیلِہِ

Ali will fight for the⟪ar:تاویل⟫, the interpretation of the Quran, as I have struggled for its revelation, its ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫. In other words, what Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) struggled for is to bring us multiplicity. He struggled to bring us the outward forms of reason in over 6000 ⟪ar:آیات⟫of the Quran. But then he established Ali has his legatee (⟪ar:وصی⟫) in order to consolidate and unify those verses, and to reveal its one true spirit for us in meaning, by his interpretation. So you have that outward expansion in reason through ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫, followed by an inner contraction, an inner consolidation through its interpretations or ⟪ar:تاویل⟫. This is why we find that the outward form of religion (the ⟪ar:ظاھر⟫) is always diverse in nature. It has various forms of worship, numerous ⟪ar:آیات⟫of the Quran, various ranks and levels in hierarchies, countless signs in the horizons and the souls; but the ⟪ar:باطن⟫(the inner form) is always unified in its nature - one goal, one purpose, one intention; one spiritual thread running through the entire fabric of this entire physicality that we see all around us.

Now the Quran informs us that all of creation emerged from a single intention followed by a single command:

وَإِذَا قَضَىٰٓ أَمْرًۭا فَإِنَّمَا يَقُولُ لَهُۥ كُن فَيَكُونُ
When He decrees something,  He only says to it : Be!" - and it is.

So all of creation emerged, and came into existence from the single command of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) - ⟪ar:کن⟫. And then he says:

QThe Family of Imran [3:83]

The Family of Imran [3:83]
[ سورۃ ۳] آل عمران

‏أَفَغَيْرَ دِينِ ٱللَّهِ يَبْغُونَ وَلَهُۥٓ أَسْلَمَ مَن فِى ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ طَوْعًۭا وَكَرْهًۭا وَإِلَيْهِ يُرْجَعُونَ ‎۸۳
Do they seek other than the religion of Allah, while to Him submitted all creatures in the heavens and the earth, willingly or unwillingly. And to Him shall they all be returned.

So the multiplicity of the heavens and the earth came from a single command of Allah, and everything will eventually return back to Allah - ⟪ar:عودۃ الی الاول⟫,  they will all return back to the original source - and that is the very definition of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫. In other words the true goal of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫is ⟪ar:تاویل⟫.

WTanzeel Taveel

The word ⟪ar:نتزیل⟫comes from ⟪ar:نزل⟫which means to come down or descend; but as a noun, the word ⟪ar:نزل⟫is used in Arabic for a lodge or a tavern which is a temporary place right? And as I explained earlier, the word ⟪ar:تاویل⟫comes from the root word ⟪ar:اول⟫which means first, or the original source. So ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫is to descend, or lodge somewhere in a hostel or an inn temporarily, but then one has to return back! Return where? To the original source! And that journey back to the original source is what? It's ⟪ar:تاویل⟫. So he illustrates this pattern that everything comes from a singularity, stays for a short period of time, before returning back to the original source. In other words multiplicity came from singularity, and therefore it must return back to singularity, which is the definition of, guess what? ⟪ar:توحید⟫.

WTawheed

The word ⟪ar:توحید⟫literally means "to make one", "to make singular". Now what's really fascinating, is that he draws this parallel between the universe and the Quran itself. If you look at the pattern of this universe, it is exactly identical. We know that this universe also came from a singularity. A singularity, that was an extremely dense matter; which exploded out with a big bang, into the multiplicity of this universe that we see today. That's the big bang of Allah's command - ⟪ar:کُن⟫"BE"! right?

INoon
{{IMG_URL:https://session.kashkole.com/Resources/IMAGES/89/44e484ed-aebc-47c1-969f-faf9c6b687c9.jpg}}

Now ⟪ar:نون⟫is a reverberating sound. When you spell out the letter ⟪ar:نون⟫, it begins with a ⟪ar:ن⟫and it ends with a ⟪ar:ن⟫, creating that reverberation. And we know that this universe is constantly expanding as well.

IBig Bang
{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/89/a630518e-79d9-4287-bc0f-3e64147636dd.jpg}}

Now if you parallel the universe with Quran, we see that just like the universe, the Quran also came from the singularity of a command - ⟪ar:أقرأ⟫, right? That was the first command issued to Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) by ⟪ar:جبرئیل علیہ السلام⟫, which resulted in the creation of the entire Quran. So you can see that parallel there! The command ⟪ar:أقرأ⟫is like a spiritual big bang - a singularity that expanded into this massive universe of Quran. And just like this universe, the Quran is also in a state of expansion - because it's meanings never come to an end!

Now we also know that eventually this entire physical universe will reduce back to the singularity that it came from. Eventually all matter will collapse into black holes, which will then coalesce, returning back to that original singularity; which is what the process of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫ is; returning back to the origin, right? Unifying diversity! Isn't that amazing? SubhanAllah!

‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ
 We will show them Our Signs in the universe, and in their own selves, until it becomes manifest to them that this is the truth.

Now what do we find in the horizons? Multiplicity! What's in the ⟪ar:انفس⟫? Singularity! He created multiplicity in the horizons for us, which he calls his "signs". Why? To recognize the singularity of its creator - that there is one single thought, one single will, one intention, one command riding the back, of this ocean of diversity that we see in our horizons. So just as the diversity of reasons leads us to singularity that the intellect perceives, the diversity of the signs spread out in the horizons should lead our souls to the oneness and singularity of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

Why do kids put the pieces of a jigsaw puzzle together? To reveal the final picture, right? This is exactly what Allah tells us to do in the Quran. He asks us to keep piece all these distinct signs (⟪ar:آیات⟫) that he spread out for us in the horizons and the souls - ⟪ar:حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَق⟫, until the one true reality of ⟪ar:الحق⟫ emerges from it.

So, what I wanted to show you, is this remarkable consistency that exists in the design of our creator - in physicality, spirituality, religion, Quran everywhere! And consistence is the proof of authenticity. How do you know someone or something is for real? Through consistency, right? So I hope you can really appreciate and marvel over the genius of Allah's design. Look how perfect it is! This is why he says:

QThe Sovereignty [67:3]

The Sovereignty [67:3]
[ سورۃ ۶۷] الملک

‏ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ سَبْعَ سَمَوَتٍۢ طِبَاقًۭا ۖ **مَّا تَرَىٰ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلرَّحْمَنِ مِن تَفَوُتٍۢ** ۖ فَٱرْجِعِ ٱلْبَصَرَ هَلْ تَرَىٰ مِن فُطُورٍۢ ‎۳
[And] who created seven heavens in layers. You do not see in the creation of the Most Merciful any inconsistency. So extend your vision as far as you can see; do you see any breaks? any gaps? any holes?

# Centrality of Revelation

After making the distinction between reason and intellect; multiplicity and singularity, and explaining that the goal of creating multiplicity is to trace it back to its original source, to make it one! ⟪ar:توحید⟫is the ultimate goal. After explaining that, he moves on to the centrality of revelation. He gives the example of the bedouin, who just as about the battle of ⟪ar:جمل⟫was about to begin, asked Maulana Ali (AS), "What do you say about the oneness of Allah?", and Maulana Ali actually chooses to respond to his question; and in doing so he highlighted a very fundamental truth for us - that the teachings of Quran (which is centered around revealing the oneness of Allah) goes beyond the limitations and confines of what's reasonable in this world.

Because if you look at this event from the perspective of worldly "reason", it was not only unreasonable for the bedouin to ask the question, but even more unreasonable for Maulana Ali (AS) to respond to his question - because they're about to go to battle. But within the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS), the ⟪ar:توحید⟫of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) took precedence over all forms of external actions; just as intentions always take precedence over action. To him, this battle was not a fight, it was worship! He was about to enter a form of worship through this battle, to defend and clarify the  ⟪ar:توحید⟫of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). And therefore for him, this question, that "What do you say about the oneness of Allah", was an extremely relevant question, that took precedence over the act of the battle itself. Because people should know why they must worship Allah, before they enter into the act of worship itself. So for him, the **reasons**of the Quran took precedence over the reasons of this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫.  You see?

We find this exact same pattern in the battle of Karbala. If you look at the battle of Karbala from a military point of view, it was a completely unreasonable battle. Because Imam Hussain (AS) and his men never stood a chance against Yazid's army. So if you look at the pros and cons from a worldly standpoint, 72 men, weak from hunger and thirst, standing against an army of 80,000 fully nourished men, was not, by any stretch of the imagination, a reasonable decision.

But based on the reasons that the Quran provides, it was the most perfect and the most intelligent decision ever made. So the battle of Karbala was centered around the reasons and the essence of Quran. The battle of Karbala was a spiritual decision, not a worldly one, which turned that battle into worship. These people, through their martyrdom, they set a precedent for us, and those that will come after us, till the end of time - that this is how Allah should be worshiped and obeyed. Even if you have to sacrifice every single belonging, including your loved ones, including your children, even your very life, then you do it - because Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) is worthy of that sacrifice.

And this is an extremely dangerous precedent for us if you reflect on it. Because during the battle when the time of ⟪ar:ظھر⟫arrived, Imam Hussain sent one of his companions to ⟪ar:عمر ابن سعد⟫asking him to pause the battle for a bit so that they could pray ⟪ar:ظھر⟫. But he refused, saying that your prayer are not acceptable to Allah! He says: SubhanAllah! The house (⟪ar:اھل البیت⟫) from which we received the gift of prayer, their prayer is not acceptable and yours is? So he returns and says, "Ya Imam! These people are not ready to stop the battle. Imam Hussain said, well we cannot miss our prayers! So he ordered half the men to fight while the other half prayed; and then when they completed their prayers, they went and fought while the remaining half offered their prayed.

Again, if you think about this from a worldly, or practical "reason" perspective, there were so many reasons to not pray on that day. In a matter of a few hours, they would all be standing before Allah themselves, then why did they have to go to such extremes to offer their prayers? Because for Imam Hussain, praying to his creator was above and beyond any and all worldly reason. His reasons for praying, were being motivated by the Quran! They were centered around revelation, not ⟪ar:دنیا⟫.

And the reason I say that this is an extremely scary precedent, is because on the day of judgment, when you and I are standing before Allah with Imam Hussain and his companions by his side, and when Allah asks us, "Why didn't you pray?" What am I going to say? What are you going to say? I was too tired? I was too sleepy? I overate? I had too much homework? I was at the movies? I was out with friends? I was on a holiday? What practical **reason**will you give to Allah? When here stands a man who sacrificed everything he had - his possessions, his companions, his children, even his own life - but he did not submit to any reason when it came to offering his ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫ to his creator. And this is something that we should all very seriously think about!

<!-- section 4 (id=90, raw_sort=4): Harmonizing Intellect With Revelation -->

# Internal Intellect

I3 Components{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/de4e78d9-a292-42fa-9fa1-a976822e3cb7.jpg}}

In the previous sessions we studied 2 of the 3 components that are essential for the spirit of the intellect (or ⟪ar:روح العقل⟫). The first was understanding the difference between "reason" and "intellect", which is essentially the distinction between multiplicity and unity or the concepts of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫and ⟪ar:تاویل⟫. The second component was the centrality of revelation - that the intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫) has to revolve around the principles and teachings of revelation. Quranic reasons must always take precedence over worldly reasons in order to engage this spirit of the intellect.

The third, and final component that we will study today, is the idea of establishing a harmony and agreement between revelation and the intellect. In order to explain this concept, he brings in the word - interpreter (⟪ar:ترجمان⟫). He says that the role of our intellect, is like that of an interpreter. What is an interpreter?

IInterpreter{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/4f431218-5fc7-4e80-8a08-e36820f95774.jpg}}

An interpreter is someone who understands an idea or a concept in one language or in one format, and then converts and delivers that same idea and concept into another format. Right? An interpreter bridges the gap between two disconnected parties. He says, this is exactly how the human brain (⟪ar:دماغ⟫) and the human intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫) operate. They both act as interpreters of this world. How is that?

IBrain Interpreter{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/b11d9606-d3cf-4755-9ce3-bad0b942c6f6.jpg}}

Well, in the physical realm, our brains interpret the multiplicity of the sensual world around us, and it unifies all that data into a single perception for us. Our brains receives all this "**diverse**", external data, through our 5 senses (sight, sound, smell, taste, touch - multiple inputs) and it traces all that information back to that single source as its attributes, right? So for example, we look at a tree, and our brains take in everything about that tree - its shape, size, color, smell, touch, the taste of its fruit. Our brain traces back all that information to the original source and saves that information in the form of the various attributes of the tree. Right? Now what is this process of tracing multiplicity back to unity called? It's called ⟪ar:تاویل⟫- to return back to the original source. But then it combines all that sensual data and makes it one - in the form of a single perception of a tree; it makes all that data - one! What is that process called? It's called ⟪ar:توحید⟫- to make one! In other words, our brain is constantly engaged in the process of receiving multiple inputs of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫, tracing it back to its original source which is ⟪ar:تاویل⟫, and then combining that data into a single perception, which is ⟪ar:توحید⟫.

ITTT{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/4080625e-022a-45c0-80f7-a53e63e8e4b5.jpg}}

Last time we studied that in the realm of Quran, you have ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫; which is the multiplicity of words. Then you have ⟪ar:تاویل⟫which is tracing that multiplicity back to the original source through interpretations or the meanings of those words; which then results in what? It results in the oneness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), which is ⟪ar:توحید⟫.  So we all know that we have these principles at work in the realm of revelation, but what we don't realize, is that we have those exact same patterns at work in the most physical realm as well. This is how we are naturally wired to operate. Our brains take in the multiplicity of sensory data (which is similar to ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫); it trace all that information back to its original source, as attributes of that entity  (which is parallel to ⟪ar:تاویل⟫); and the unifies all that data into a single perception (which is the definition of ⟪ar:توحید⟫).

In other words, this idea of ⟪ar:توحید⟫has been placed at the very essence of human existence, as a constant reminder! Remind us of what? That this is what we have been created for! We have been created for the ⟪ar:توحید⟫of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). SubhanAllah!

Now in the spiritual realm (within our spirit - our ⟪ar:روح⟫) the intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫) does the exact same thing! It acts as interpreter. We takes in the multiple verses of revelation, we trace them back to their original intention, in order to form a unified understanding or guidance. This is why Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said:

قال رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ و سلم
ِرَسُولُ الرَّجُل تَرجُمٰنِ عَقْلِہ
The messenger of a man is his intellect

Why messenger? Because just as a messenger is sent by God to act as in intermediary between himself and mankind; in the same way, our spirit (⟪ar:روح⟫) sends out our intellects into this world, to interpret the signs in the horizons and the soul that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created for us.

IGuidance{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/504c0af7-d656-4cb7-99cc-e3fa6ffd522e.jpg}}

In other words, our spirit uses our intellects, to act as an intermediary between itself and revelation, just as our brains interpret sensory data into a single perception for us. You understand? Let's break it down! What is the ultimate goal of Quran? Guidance! ⟪ar:ھدایۃ⟫. Guidance for who? For us! Who are we? We are spirits (⟪ar:ارواح⟫). So the goal of revelation is to guide our spirit. What tool is required for that interpretation? for that unification? The intellect (⟪ar:العقل⟫). The intellect is the interpreter that converts the words and meanings of Quran, into guidance for us.

# Interpreter of Quran

So the brain functions as an interpreter for the realities of the physical world, and the intellect functions as an interpreter for the realities of the spiritual world. Now in the realm of revelation, who do we know is responsible for the interpretation, or ⟪ar:تاویل⟫of Quran? Maulana Ali (AS)! So you see the pattern here?

This is why Maulana Ali said: **Quran is a book that is inscribed between two covers; it speaks not with a tongue; and it cannot do without an interpreter (⟪ar:لا بُدَّا مِنَ التَّرجُمٰن⟫)**. In other words, even though Quran is a book of multiple reasons, it is mute by its nature; and cannot explain itself to us. It is ⟪ar:کتاب السامت⟫- a mute book. Just like this universe is a silent book of Allah; because it cannot explain itself without the interpretation of our brains! On the exact same pattern, Maulana Ali (AS) says that the Quran is a mute book of Allah, and it therefore requires an interpreter, to explain and unify it in meaning for us. Because without an interpreter, we would be lost in the ocean of its multiplicity, you see? Just as an insane person is lost in this world without his intellect. Can you see the pattern here?

Now we all know that Maulana Ali has also called himself ⟪ar:کتاب الناطق⟫- the "speaking book of Allah". When did he use these words? During the battle of ⟪ar:سفین⟫.

During the battle of ⟪ar:سفین⟫this guy ⟪ar:عمر ابن عباس⟫created a discord in Ali's camp using the Quran. When all the chief warriors of ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫ army were killed, and Ali's army advanced to the point where ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫tent became visible, and defeat was imminent;⟪ar:عمر ابن عاس⟫ invented a trick to save his side from defeat. He ordered his soldiers to raise the Holy Quran on their spears, as if begging for peace. This created a discord in Ali's camp and they became hesitant and refused to attack. So you see what he did? He confused them through the ⟪ar:ظاھر⟫of the Quran.
Maulana Ali said, 'Numerous times I advised ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫to follow the Holy Quran before this battle began! He never agreed to that. Now because we are on the verge of victory, he is tricking us with the Quran. This is not a sincere plea, it is simply a strategic move to avoid defeat. Therefore I am not willing to stop the battle.' At that point⟪ar:عسس الکندی⟫with the Quran in his hand said, 'Ya Ali! they are Muslims requesting us to follow the Holy Quran!' To that Ali said:
⟪ar:ھذا کتاب اللہ السامت و أنا کتاب اللہ الناطق⟫
This is the silent or the mute book of Allah; and I am the speaking book of Allah! I am the interpreter of this Quran! I am telling you to advance, because we're on the verge of eliminating this ⟪ar:فتنۃ⟫from its roots, once and for all! And my decision to advance is perfectly in line with the teachings of Quran and the will of Allah! But they refused to fight.

So Maulana Ali (AS) called himself⟪ar:کتاب اللہ الناطق⟫, the speaking book of Quran. Why? Because through his interpretations, he creates a harmony, and an agreement between our intellects, and the Quran. He unifies the multiplicity of its verses into the singular concept of ⟪ar:توحید⟫for us. Now I want to share something really amazing with you! I want to talk about these two words that he used to describe himself: ⟪ar:کتاب⟫and ⟪ar:ناطق⟫.

## Book

What does the word ⟪ar:کتاب⟫ mean? It means book. What is a book? A book is a written or printed work that **unifies**and brings together multiple chapters, or multiple ideas, in **one** organized and systematic way. Yes? In other words, a book is a device that converts multiplicity into unity. And Maulana Ali (AS) symbolizes himself with a ⟪ar:کتاب-⟫Why? Because he brings together, and unifies the various modes of worship, the various forms of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫ and the teachings of Quran, in a unified, systematic way for us! How does he do that?

IAli Interpreter{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/9465744c-18dd-4b16-b05f-b893ec88bfcf.jpg}}

He does it through his speech; and through his actions. If you carefully study the intentions, speech and actions of Maulana Ali (AS), you'll see that his entire life is nothing more than a creative interpretation of Quran. His every act, demonstrates a movement from the letters of revelation, to the singularity of its spirit in meaning; and then from the spirit of revelation to the will and the command of the Revealer himself. His life, systematically unifies the concept of worship and ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫for us, and organizes these Quranic concepts into actionable items for us; just as a book, is an organized collection of chapters for us to read and understand. Isn't that incredible?

Every book has multiple chapters, but the goal of all those chapters revolve around one central idea, one essential principle, one story! Isn't that true? Similarly, if you look at Ali's life, you'll see that his is a book of multiple chapters. Just as he said that the Quran is a book between two covers, Ali's life is a life between 2 ⟪ar:مساجد⟫. His is a life between two mosques. The book of his life begins with his birth in ⟪ar:مسجد الحرام⟫ and ends with his death in ⟪ar:مسجد کوفی⟫. His book, is a life spent between two ⟪ar:مساجد⟫(two mosques) just as he said the Quran is a book between two covers. Right? And what do you do in a mosque? You worship Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).  In other words, his entire life - the main subject of the book of his life, is ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫, and worship of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

Within the book of his life we find multiple chapters - all set as example for us to follow. We find the chapter of his childhood! His example of worship as a child. Then there is a chapter of his adulthood; his example of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫as an adult; his example of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫as a husband, as a brother, as a father; his example of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫as a courageous warrior; his example of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫as a leader; as a humble slave of Allah. His chapter of his servitude and loyalty to Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam). In other words, every chapter of his life exemplifies for us, how Allah wants us to write the various chapters of our book. You see?

And what is the central theme, the essence of all of the chapters of his life? To unify (to make one) the multiplicity of all his intentions, speech, actions - which was what? Carrying out the command and will of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). In other words, ⟪ar:توحید⟫is the central idea of his life's book. This is why in the Quran, most "book" references actually refer back to him.

The Cow [2:1-2]
[ سورۃ ۲] البقرۃ

‏الٓمٓ ‎۱‏ذَلِكَ ٱلْكِتَبُ لَا رَيْبَ ۛ فِيهِ ۛ هُدًۭى لِّلْمُتَّقِينَ ‎۲
( 1 ) *Alif-Lam-Mîm.*. ( 2 ) **That**is the Book about which there can be no doubt, that it is a guidance for those who wish to be conscious of Allah

First of all why does it say "that" book? Because the reciter has the book in his hand as he's reading it. It should have been ⟪ar:ھذہ⟫⟪ar:الکتب لا ریب فیہ⟫. This is the book about which there could be no doubt. So then why "that" book? ⟪ar:ذالک⟫is what's called ⟪ar:اشارہ بعید⟫in Arabic. It is used to point to a distant object. Which means that the ⟪ar:سورۃ⟫is not talking about this mute book! It's talking about "that" speaking book of Allah, it's talking about that Maulana Ali, - ⟪ar:ذَلِكَ ٱلْكِتَبُ لَا رَيْبَ ۛ فِيهِ⟫ - about whom there can be no doubt as the interpreter of Quran! ⟪ar:هُدًۭى لِّلْمُتَّقِينَ⟫The one who is the true source of guidance for anyone who wish to become conscious of Allah. You see? Similarly, in ⟪ar:سورۃ الواقعۃ⟫Allah says:
‏إِنَّهُۥ لَقُرْآنٌۭ كَرِيمٌۭ

Indeed this is an honorable recital.
فِى كِتَبٍۢ مَّكْنُونٍۢ

In a Book that is well-guarded. Who is that well guarded book? It's ⟪ar:علی ابن ابی طالب⟫. In the life example of Maulana Ali (AS), the teachings, the meanings, the interpretations and the intentions of this book has been preserved forever.
لَّا يَمَسُّهُۥٓ إِلَّا ٱلْمُطَهَّرُونَ

Which none can touch, none can access - except the one's who have been purified. Who are these purified beings? The Imams! Because Ali is no longer among us. So now how do we gain access to the meanings of this book? Through the teachings of the Imams, passed down to us through the hierarchy of ⟪ar:دعات⟫and ⟪ar:مناصیب⟫. They are the various chapters of his book - because they all take their knowledge form him.

So just as a book is a device, that unifies the essence of multiple chapters, it tells us what the author wants us to know; in the exact same way, Ali is the one who unifies the essence of the Quran for us, and tells us exactly what Allah wants us to know. That's why he is ⟪ar:کتاب اللہ⟫ - the book of Allah, but not just any book of Allah! He is ⟪ar:کتاب اللہ الناطق⟫ - the speaking book of Allah.

## Speech

Now let's take a look at the other word ⟪ar:ناطق⟫- which comes from the root word ⟪ar:نطق⟫which means speech. What is speech? What are essentially doing when we speak? We use the multiplicity of our words to deliver a singular meaning. If you ask me to bring you a glass of water because you're feeling thirsty, you have to explain it to me using multiple words! You'll have to use several words to explain to me exactly what you want. I will listen to what you say, take in the multiplicity of your words, and trace it back to your intention, and then unify it to a single task - which is bringing you that glass of water! And if there is any ambiguity or multiplicity in my understanding, I will have to reduce it, and narrow it down to a singularity. What size of glass? Large, Medium, Small? What type? Glass, paper, plastic? Warm water or cold water? Tap water or mineral water? You see? Why am I asking you all these followup questions? To unify my understanding to exactly one particular concept of what you want! I have to make it one - ⟪ar:توحید⟫. You see that? We are ⟪ar:موحدون⟫by our very nature, in the perceptions of our brain, understandings of our intellects, and unification of words into meaning.  - We are ⟪ar:موحدون -⟫We are unifiers. Allah designed us with this intrinsic,  unavoidable, inescapable ability to make one in each and every area of our life- We are built and designed for ⟪ar:توحید⟫. SubhanAllah! If that is not perfection and consistency of design, you tell me what is?

So Maulana Ali (AS) called himself ⟪ar:کتاب الناطق⟫, the speaking book, and both of those (book and speech) or devices for unification. They're both devices for ⟪ar:توحید⟫ SubhanAllah! This is just beyond incredible!! Just take a moment to really deeply reflect on the genius of Allah's design! It's incredible how he put the process of ⟪ar:توحید⟫at the very grass root level of human existence and human nature. SubhanAllah!

So this is why Maulana Ali (AS) called himself the true interpreter of Quran (⟪ar:ترجمان القرآن⟫), because his every intention, speech and action, converts Quran from the format of mute words and meaningless two dimensional actions, to the format of 3 dimensional, meaningful actionable tasks for us. He takes the apparently disconnected modes of reason (the do's and don'ts) and unifies them into a single template of worship for us.

# Internal vs External Intellect

So now he brings the two intellects together, which he calls the **internal**intellect vs the **external**intellect, to explain the concept of harmony.
IInternal Vs External{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/43463cec-316c-413f-8f03-58a0ad9e01f5.jpg}}

He explains that the only way our spirits can achieve guidance, is if we submit our internal intellect, which is partial and imperfect in its nature, to the teachings of these external intellects that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created for us in the form of Maulana Ali (AS) and the Imams, who are complete and perfect in their nature. Because their teachings and interpretations harmonize the Quran with our intellects. We cannot understand the Quran by ourselves, using our own imperfect and partial intellects. Therefore in order to achieve guidance, we must humble ourselves, accept our imperfection, and surrender to the teachings and life examples of these external and perfect intellects. We have to interpret the Quran, using the **right**interpreters! You see? Why? Because Allah revealed himself to our intellects using these external intellects and interpreters.

Think about this word - "revelation". What does revelation mean? To reveal - To make something that was previously unknown, know to others. Now, revelation is meaningless, without its counterpart of interpretation. Think about that for a second! Had Allah created and revealed this universe, but did not create human begins to interpret that revelation, would the universe have any meaning? No! It would have been pointless, right? If he revealed beauty, but did not give us sight, would that beauty be of any use? If he revealed fragrance, but did not give us the ability to smell? If he revealed sound, but did not give us the ability to hear, would revelation be of any use?

And then reflect on this incredible consistency in his design. There is multiplicity in the number of things that can be seen. There are millions of things that we can see, but we see them using one single sense of sight. There are hundreds of sounds to be heard, all heard and interpreted using the one sense of hearing. So many tastes, all enjoyed through one tongue. So many different sensations, all experienced using one sense of touch. So many fragrances and smells, all experienced through one sense of smell. Every thing that is revealed - all forms of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫exist in multiplicity, but are interpreted, taken back to the original source using one particular sense. So all forms of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫exist in multiplicity, and all forms of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫interpret them back to the original source - resulting in one perception, one observation, one concept, one idea, one principle. The result is always "making one" - ⟪ar:توحید⟫.

I mean, can we even truly appreciating how amazing and consistent this design is? it's really important for us to appreciate this consistency in Allah's design - because consistency proves authenticity. It makes the Quranic statements falsifiable - which is such an important rule in science! For something to be accurate scientifically, it has to be falsifiable.

So for example, if I put forth a theory, that water freezes to ice at 0 degrees Celsius, then I have to consistently prove that. And if you can show, that you can freeze water at 10 degrees Celsius, then you just falsified my theory. Because you proved that my theory is not consistent.

As human beings we are constantly searching for patterns; and when we find consistency in patterns, we deem them scientific, or valid. Isn't that true?  Now Allah put forth a theory for us in the form of:

Fusilat [41:53]
[ سورۃ ۴۱] فصلت

‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ ‎۵۳
 We will show them Our Signs in the universe, and in their own selves, until it becomes manifest to them that this is the truth. 

He put this theory that there is going to be multiplicity in the ⟪ar:آفاق⟫, which will be interpreted by the ⟪ar:أنفس⟫. Every ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫ in the horizons will be interpreted in the form of ⟪ar:تاویل⟫by the ⟪ar:انفس⟫. You'll always find that pair - not just in the physical realm, but in every realm! So what I revealed to you in the horizons of this universe, will be interpreted by your senses. What I have revealed through you in the horizons of the Quran, will be interpreted by your spirit. What I have revealed to you in the horizons of your body will be interpreted by your soul. What I have revealed to you in the horizons of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) will be interpreted by Maulana Ali (AS). And the patterns will match consistently across the board. The pattern of this universe works exactly like the pattern of the Quran; which works exactly like the pattern of your existence; which works exactly like the pattern of your religion!

Now go ahead and falsify my theory! Prove me wrong! He says... ⟪ar:حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ⟫- Keep trying, keep exploring, keep challenging everything until you have no option but to recognize it as the ultimate truth - based on the consistency of my design! SubhanAllah! You will find these concept of ⟪ar:تنزیل، تاویل،⟫ ⟪ar:توحید⟫at work in every single thing that I've designed.

IRevelation{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/b547c60a-cf9c-4cf9-9102-279b19ac433a.jpg}}

This is why it's called "revelation"! To make known, what was unknown! What was unknown to us? The intentions and will of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala)! How did Allah reveal his will and intentions to us? Through this creations, through this signs in the horizons and the soul he revealed his abilities to us. Through the Quran he revealed his intentions to us. Through the relationship between our body and spirit he exemplified the process of submission to us. Look how the body submits to the commands of the spirit - instantly! without any hesitation. Then through Rasul Allah and Maulana Ali (AS), he revealed the outer forms, the body, the exterior of his religion to us, and through Maulana Ali (AS), he revealed the inner forms - the meanings, the spirit and essence of his religion to us.

ITTT2{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/90/4f70ad5f-e6cf-4144-80c7-a48a2dcd6e1b.jpg}}

Therefore: ⟪ar:‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ⟫We will show them our signs in the horizons and the souls. What are these signs? What is everything pointing to? The mechanism of ⟪ar:توحید⟫. In other words, we will show them the mechanism of ⟪ar:توحید⟫in the ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫of horizons, through the ⟪ar:تاویل⟫of the souls. It cannot get any more perfect and consistent than this!

So these are the 3 components that are essential or prerequisite for the spirit of the intellect. First is understanding the difference between reason and intellect, or multiplicity and unity, or the process and design of ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫and ⟪ar:تاویل⟫. Second is appreciating the centrality of revelation, or prioritizing the Quranic reasons over all worldly reasons. Finally submitting to the teachings and the interpretations of these external intellects (such as Maulana Ali and the Imams), which is essentially the concept of ⟪ar:ولایۃ⟫; because their interpretations create a harmony and an agreement between revelation and our intellects. Quran is ⟪ar:ذکر⟫it is a reminder and the⟪ar:اھل البیت⟫ are ⟪ar:اھل اذکر⟫- they are people of reminder - and the Quran says:

QThe Bee [16:43]

The Bee [16:43]
[ سورۃ ۱۶] النحل

‏وَمَآ أَرْسَلْنَا مِن قَبْلِكَ إِلَّا رِجَالًۭا نُّوحِىٓ إِلَيْهِمْ ۚ **فَسْـَٔلُوٓا۟ أَهْلَ ٱلذِّكْرِ إِن كُنتُمْ لَا تَعْلَمُونَ** ‎۴۳
And We sent not before you any but men, whom We inspired,. So ask of those who know the Scripture, if you know not.

Allah gave us this universe and made us interpreters of his universe. On the exact same pattern, he gave us الذکر which is the Quran and he gave us the اھل الذکر , the interpreters of the Quran. He then asked us to connect with them in order to harmonize the message with the intellect.

So once we accomplish these 3 essential components, our intellects become ready for the spirit to be breathed into it in order to bring it to life.

# Goal of religion

I want to end today's session by sharing another pattern with you that has to do with this pattern of multiplicity and unity; and that is the essential goal of religion. What is religion? If you had to reduce the purpose of religion into one single statement, what would that statement be?

One statement would be that religion is a tool that helps us move out of the multiplicity of this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫, towards the singularity of ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫. Because this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫is essentially an orientation towards multiplicity. When we talk about someone who is focused or oriented towards the world, what does that mean? Think about that for a second! What does it mean to be focused on ⟪ar:دنیا⟫? It essentially means to be deluded by, and lost in multiplicity. Because the ultimate goal of the soul is to find happiness, yes? That's the common denominator. When it's oriented towards ⟪ar:دنیا⟫, how does it know how to generate happiness? Through the pleasures and sensations of the body, right? How does it do that? Through acquisition of ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. By acquiring things! Cars, houses, money, fame, power, social validation, social distractions, sports, games, movies, music - it goes on and on and on. Exponential multiplicity! Is any of it enough?  Is it ever enough? No! We acquire what we chase, we get bored of it and we want more. Why is that? What is ⟪ar:دنیا⟫never enough? Even though we have no shortage of it?! The reason is, because there is no harmony between the spirit and this body, you see? The spirit cannot be harmonized with the ⟪ar:دنیا⟫because they're completely different entities coming from completely different sources. And so physical possessions, regardless of their shape and form, their touch and feel, they act as band-aids. Temporary relief! These possessions might temporarily address the symptoms, but never the cause itself. And so there is momentary relief, followed by a greater hunger and thirst.

This ⟪ar:دنیا⟫is like an "itch". Have you ever had a rash where your skin gets irritated and itchy? What do you do when you feel that itch? You scratch it. How does it feel? It feels great right? That relief, that sensation, that pleasure! It's incredible, isn't it? But then what happens? Does the itch disappear, or does it get worse? It gets worse! So what do you do? You itch it harder, and harder and harder. Eventually what happens to the skin? It bleeds and the itch turns into a wound! Isn't that true? That's exactly the mechanism of this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. The more we scratch it, the more it itches; the more we possess the more we want to acquire! In the end, we get destroyed by the multiplicity of our own possessions! You can see that when people lose their jobs. They go crazy. Because they can no longer support their life styles. They've developed this severe itch, but they can no longer scratch it! Can you imagine how painful that must be?!!!

And so religion and the Quran steps in as an ice pack or an ointment that doesn't just address the symptoms, it tries to cure the irritation itself. It gives the soul what it needs! And what the soul needs is the love of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

وَمَا جَعَلَهُ ٱللَّهُ إِلَّا بُشْرَىٰ لَكُمْ وَلِتَطْمَىِٕنَّ قُلُوبُكُم بِهِ
Allah made it not but as a message of glad tiding for you; and so that your hearts could feel content

‏ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ وَتَطْمَىِٕنُّ قُلُوبُهُم بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ ۗ أَلَا بِذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ تَطْمَىِٕنُّ ٱلْقُلُوبُ
Those who have believed and whose hearts are satisfied by the remembrance of Allah. 
Most certainly! Unquestionably! It is only by the remembrance of Allah that hearts are satisfied

What the soul needs is to become whole again. The only reason the soul is in pain, and unhappy is because it has been separated from Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) you see?! And so Quran gives us these ointments and creams in the form of prayers, fasting, ZAKAT, AKHLAQ, that help our souls reintegrate and become whole once again, by drawing from its source! It helps us become one again! Through the ⟪ar:توحید⟫of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), through the oneness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) we, ourselves, become one and whole again. And the most beautiful manifestation of that "becoming one again" is the gift of ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫.

It's the exact same pattern of connecting our internal and imperfect intellects to the external and perfect intellect. What's the Arabic word for connection? ⟪ar:صلۃ⟫- the very root for ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫. By our prayers, by our صلوۃ we connect our imperfect and broken selves with our complete and perfect creator. This is why the act of ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫is such an incredible gift for the one who really comes to appreciate and understand this principle of multiplicity to unity. Because ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫is that one instance in our hypocritical lives, where we completely unify the multiplicity of our existence at every single level. ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫is that one act, where our intention matches our speech, and our speech matches our action. It is that one instance where our hearts, our minds and our spirits become one. They're all unified in their act of submission to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). It's a state where we have completely rejected and abandoned the multiplicity of ⟪ar:دنیا⟫to fulfill the singular will and command of our creator - which is to fulfill the purpose of our creation. What is that purpose? ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫- servitude!

The Winnowing Winds [51:56]
[ سورۃ ۵۱] الذاریات

‏وَمَا خَلَقْتُ ٱلْجِنَّ وَٱلْإِنسَ إِلَّا لِيَعْبُدُونِ ‎۵۶
And I created not the jinn and man except they should worship Me.

<!-- section 5 (id=91, raw_sort=5): The Spirit Of The Intellect -->

## The Spirit Of The Intellect

We're studying the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS). We began with this topic which the author calls "Spirit of the intellect" or ⟪ar:روح العقل⟫. He explains there are 3 components that are essential for understanding, or rather generating this spirit of the intellect. We studied all 3 components in detail in the previous sessions.

The first is understanding the difference between "reason" and  "intellect"' or the distinction between multiplicity and unity. We looked at the linguistic definitions of ⟪ar:تنزیل, تاویل⟫ and ⟪ar:توحید⟫. The word ⟪ar:تنزیل⟫ means a descent, referring to the revelation itself; ⟪ar:تاویل⟫ means to trace that revelation back to the original source ⟪ar:عُد الی الاول⟫; and ⟪ar:توحید⟫ means to unify or make one.

The second component was understanding the centrality of revelation - that the intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫)  has to revolve around the principles and teachings of revelation in order to achieve spiritual ascension. It is the idea that Quranic reasons must always take precedence over worldly reasons in  order to engage this spirit of the intellect.

The third component was to establish a harmony between revelation and intellect, by submitting our internal imperfect intellects to the external and perfect intellects that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created for us, such as Maulana Ali (AS), the Imams, DUAT etc.

That's where we left off in the previous session. In today's session we'll look at the true purpose of why Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) sent us messengers; and how they fit into the design of spiritual guidance. We'll look at how the intellect is brought to life in the form of the spiritual heart (or ⟪ar:قلب⟫). And finally, with our understanding of these concepts, we'll look at the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS) to understand what made him the most perfectly spiritual being after Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam); and why we should model our own lives and our own spiritual existence based on his life example.

# Purpose For Sending Messengers

So after explaining the 3 essential components for generating the ⟪ar:روح العقل⟫, the author moves on to explain the real purpose of sending messengers. According to Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam):
رَسُولُ الرَّجُل تَرجُمٰنِ عَقْلِہ

A person's intellect is his messenger. Why? Because just as a messenger act as an **interpreter** between God and human beings, our intellects act as interpreters for our spirit - our ⟪ar:روح⟫. It interprets the signs that Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created for us in the horizons. This is why Allah says:
‏سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ

We will show them our Signs in the universe, and in their own souls. How does he show us his signs? Through the interpretations of our intellects. This is why Rasul Allah said, that a man's intellect is his messenger.

Now this internal and imperfect messenger, is engaged by an external and perfect intellect sent to us by Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in the form of prophets, messengers and their legatee (in our case Maulana Ali (AS). And what is the purpose of this engagement? Why are they sent to us? Allah answers that question in the Quran by saying:

QSoils of War [8:24]

‏يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ ٱسْتَجِيبُوا۟ لِلَّهِ وَلِلرَّسُولِ **إِذَا دَعَاكُمْ لِمَا يُحْيِيكُمْ**
O you who believe! Answer Allah and Messenger when he calls you to that which will give you life

He says, respond to Allah and his messengers, who call you; who invite you; who make ⟪ar:دعوۃ⟫towards you - ⟪ar:دعوۃ⟫towards what? And here in lies the secret of our existence:
 دَعَاكُمْ لِمَا يُحْيِيكُمْ

They calls you to that which will give you life! That is what messengers call us to - to "that" which will give us life. Wait a minute! I thought I'm already alive! Because if messengers are calling me towards "that" which will give me life, the assumption here is that I am dead, isn't it? So then what is being given life here? Our intellects! That is what they are bringing to life with their ⟪ar:دعوۃ⟫, our intellects!

Now this same Quranic principle is reiterated by Maulana Ali (AS) in his first sermon of ⟪ar:نھج البلاغۃ⟫in which he says:

WUnearth buried treasure

قال مولانا علی علیہ السلام
فَبَعَثَ فِيهمْ رُسُلَهُ، وَوَاتَرَ إِلَيْهِمْ أَنْبِياءَهُ لِيَسْتَأْدُوهُمْ مِيثَاقَ فِطْرَتِهِ۔ وَيُذَكِّرُوهُمْ مَنْسِيَّ نِعْمَتِهِ، وَيَحْتَجُّوا عَلَيْهِمْ بَالتَّبْلِيغِ، وَيُثِيرُوا لَهُمْ دَفَائِنَ الْعُقُولِ
Then Allah sent His Messengers and series of His prophets towards them to get them to fulfill the pledges of His creation. and to remind them of his bounties and blessings, and to exhort them by preaching, in order to unearth for them the buried treasures of the intellects.

فَبَعَثَ فِيهمْ رُسُلَهُ، وَوَاتَرَ إِلَيْهِمْ أَنْبِياءَهُ لِيَسْتَأْدُوهُمْ مِيثَاقَ فِطْرَتِهِ

Then Allah sent His Messengers and series of His prophets towards them to get them to fulfill the pledges of His creation. What is this pledge? The pledge of الست بربکم؟ Am I not your master? that's when we all pledged: بلی شھدنا - Indeed you are! So the messengers are sent to help us fulfill our pledge of عبودیۃ to Allah.
 وَيُذَكِّرُوهُمْ مَنْسِيَّ نِعْمَتِهِ

and to remind them of his bounties and blessings
وَيَحْتَجُّوا عَلَيْهِمْ بَالتَّبْلِيغِ

to exhort them by preaching - to strongly encourage and urge them through their teachings- and then he says something incredible:
 وَيُثِيرُوا لَهُمْ دَفَائِنَ الْعُقُولِ

and to unearth for them the buried treasures of the intellects.

So with these words, Maulana Ali (AS) is revealing the ultimate goal of messengers. Their goal is to unearth the **treasures** buried deep within our intellects. Treasures of what? Treasures of ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫ - treasures of our slavery and servitude to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). It's already there in all of us. But it is dormant and buried deep within our intellects. That's what the messengers are sent to unearth, and dig out through revelation. So you can see that here he is essentially highlighting this relationship (the harmony) that exists between revelation and our intellects. He's saying that the intellect is not stifled, or suffocated by revelation; rather it is enlivened by it. You see?

ICall To Intellect{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/91/0b743a90-7205-4caf-ba33-0e9a74372a00.jpg}}

So now, you can picture revelation, as a loud and clear call (a ⟪ar:دعوۃ⟫) to the spiritual depths of our intellects in order to bring it to life! Our spirit (⟪ar:روح⟫) has already witnessed Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in the spiritual realm; and it has already testified for his lordship. Our spirit has experienced Allah's beatific vision, in all of his glory and majesty. That's when those seeds of his consciousness, his awareness and his recognition got planted within our intellects - the moment we testified and said: ⟪ar:بلی شھدنا⟫- Indeed we testify. But then when we were sent into this world, that testimony was made dormant; and those **treasure**of his awareness and recognition were buried within the depths of our intellects; just as Allah made a tree dormant within the seed. Just as Allah buried gold, silver and all these precious stones within the depths of mountains. You see? And why was our testimony made dormant? To test its sincerity! ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫.

"You witness and testify that I am your master? Now prove it?"It's easy to say I am your master when you're standing right before me! Now go into a world where you cannot see me directly. Go into a world where I will reveal myself through my signs in the horizons and your souls - and there; in that realm of obscurity, recognize me, and testify once again that I am your master! Study the multiplicity of my creations in the universe and unify them to recognize **the singularity**, that is your master! That is the treasure that messengers are sent to excavate - the treasure of ⟪ar:توحید⟫.  Can you appreciate the beauty of this design?

IBuried Treasures{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/91/804d0f4c-50a6-468e-967e-2b7d30a3c180.jpg}}

And so what revelation does, is that it cultivates, and brings to fruition these dormant seeds of consciousness of our ⟪ar:توحید⟫, that are buried deep within our intellects. This is why, the author argues, that the spirit of one's intellect **cannot**be alien to the revelation that descends from Allah; because it has that initial memory, that initial imprint of its creator, buried deep within it. All the messengers are doing, is they are "reminding" us of that primordial testimony with their call; with this reminder, this ⟪ar:ذکر⟫which is the Quran. And you can only be reminded of what you already know, but have forgotten. That's the definition of a reminder. A reminder is not a new piece of information! It is previously known information. This is why he says, that the reason the human intellect is capable of perceiving, recognizing and fully comprehending revelation, is because of the fact that they are both rooted in the same ultimate  source. We came from a spiritual realm, and so did revelation - to remind us of what we had originally witnessed, but have forgotten in this world.

Have you seen these movies in which the guy loses his memory or gets amnesia? What do doctors say to the relatives of that person? They say take him into familiar surroundings. Show him things from his past to jog his memory. In other words, show him signs (⟪ar:آیات⟫)  from his past, yes? That's exactly the same idea with revelation. We have been created in this world in an amnesic state. Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) put reminders of familiar surroundings in the horizons and the souls (⟪ar:آفاق و أنفس⟫) . Prophetic revelation, simply awakens and brings to surface that primordial testimony that is buried in the depths of our intellects. Messengers are like the **farmers**who waters the seeds that are hidden under the soil, in order to awakens it and bring it to the surface. Or they are like **miners**who dig deep into the depths of the mountains to bring out gold and silver. Or they are like the deep sea **divers**, who dives into the bottom of the oceans to unearth its buried treasures. Do you see the patterns? Do you see how Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) used the exact same principles in our creation? I mean, he could have just laid these precious jewels out there in the open, couldn't he? Why didn't he do that? Why did he bury them in the depths of the earth and the depths of the oceans?

First, to establish their value. So that we can appreciate their value by the fact that they take effort to retrieve.

Second - so that only the worthy can gain access to it. Because only human beings are capable of digging out these treasures and refining them, right? No other species in the world can do that!

IBond{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/91/94fbd307-3a81-4a2b-867e-9a644456f7dc.jpg}}

So now, in the words of Maulana Ali (AS), we can see that the Quran is not some extraneous source that stands in opposition to intelligence; rather we can now see it as an outward expression of the very principles, that are deeply rooted within our intellects. So our intellects serve as the inner messenger, that is then awakened and nourished by the teachings of the external messenger.

So can you now see this bond? this relationship between revelation and the intellect? On one hand, the substance of revelation cannot be engaged without the intellect, and on the other hand, the 'buried  treasures' of the intellect cannot be extracted without revelation. So there is a co-dependency there.

# Spiritual Heart

This understanding of the intellect and revelation, and the role of a messenger in connecting the two, brings us closer to what Maulana Ali refers to in his sermons as the 'spiritual heart' (or the ⟪ar:قلب⟫). This is that inner most mode of perception that is capable of 'seeing' and witnessing Allah. He says, It is the 'heart'  that can 'see' Allah, not using eyesight, not by ⟪ar:بصارۃ⟫ , but through spiritual insight, through ⟪ar:بصیرۃ⟫which is generated by the 'true principles of faith' or ⟪ar:حقائق الایمان⟫.

---

Now the author explains how to develop the spiritual heart - the ⟪ar:قلب⟫.  Let's take it from the top. What did we learn so far? We learnt that our internal, and imperfect intellects, are the **interpreters**of the signs that have been spread out for us in the horizons of this universe; just as Maulana Ali (AS) is the complete and perfect intellect for the **interpretation of the Quranic signs**, spread out for us in the horizons of revelation. We learnt that only when we combine our internal intellects with the interpretations of this external intellects, we are able to unify, and establish a harmony between our internal intellects and external revelation. Yes?

IInfusion{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/91/5d3bc86c-ba8c-4be2-ad91-7a0da7671e97.jpg}}

The author says that once this harmony has been established between intellect and revelation, we are then able to infuse of the teachings of Quran within our spirit; and the more we infuse these teachings, the more our intellects comes to life in the form of our spiritual hearts (⟪ar:قلب⟫), which is then able to once again witness the beatific vision of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) just as it did in the primordial spiritual realm. So the spiritual heart, the ⟪ar:قلب⟫is essentially the portion of the spirit, in which the teachings of Quran have been infused. And how does that infusion take place? Using the intellect!

This is why in Maulana Ali's perspective, the intellect is seen, first and  foremost as a spiritual faculty. Because without this intellect (⟪ar:عقل⟫), the spirit gets reduced to an unconscious existence - an existence without any purpose - like an insane person. Have you ever seen an insane person? They almost always make you go, "What the hell is this guy doing?!" Why is that? Because they're acting in a manner that is not aligned with our perception of reality. Isn't that true? Now the sad thing is, that in the spiritual sense, we're not far off. I'm sure there are angels that look down at us and say the exact same thing - "What the hell are these guys doing?!"(plus or minus the word hell). Our actions must seem just as ridiculous to them, as the actions of an insane person seems ridiculous to us! Because our actions do not align with the spiritual realities that the angels are accustomed to. You see? There are so many people in this world, who go through their entire life without any understanding of what they're doing; or how their actions are affecting others; or why are they even here! Because they do not have access to that spiritual portion of their intellects. They are therefore living a spiritually unconscious existence. This is what happens when the spirit operates without the intellect.
