This is why when we study the ⟪ar:سیرۃ⟫of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) and Maulana Ali (AS), we truly come to know of Allah, because they both beautifully reflected and demonstrated the characteristics of Allah in a practical form. By their character, their demeanor, their love, their justice, their compassion, their mercy, their generosity, their forgiveness, their courage, people accepted and submitted to their creator and master. Because if the creation is so incredibly amazing, then how incredible, and how amazing must their creator be - SubhanAllah!

<!-- section 9 (id=114, raw_sort=9): Unification - Developing Virtues -->

## Unification - Developing Virtues

We're studying the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS). This is a continuation of his discourse to ⟪ar:کمیل ابن زیاد⟫on the virtues of knowledge and the people of knowledge. So far we've looked at the methodology of acquiring knowledge described by the word ⟪ar:ھجم⟫used by Maulana Ali (AS) to describe an assault on words in order to penetrate the depths of its meanings. We learnt that the purpose of knowledge is to integrate it within the soul through practice in order to create a shift in our consciousness so that spiritual transformation can be achieved. We looked at the difference between morality and ethics, and how vices pose as obstacles in the path of our spiritual transformation. We discussed the ultimate struggle of ⟪ar:جھاد الاکبر⟫  which is for the intellect to win over caprice and enlist the soul in the army of ⟪ar:الرحمن⟫. We ended the conversation on the concept of ⟪ar:توحید⟫being a noun as well as a verb, which is the idea of integrating with Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in understanding as well as in action, exemplified for us in the lives of our prophet and Maulana Ali (AS).

# {2:01} Spiritual Heart & Intellect

They both are like the spiritual heart and the intellect for the soul of humanity. In other words, if we look at all of humanity as one single entity, as one single soul, then Muhammad and Ali are its heart and intellect, created for its salvation. You see? The spiritual heart is what perceives and experiences the reality of Allah, whereas the intellect recognizes that reality as the truth through logical reasoning. The word ⟪ar:عقل⟫as we know, means to bind or restrain. Its job is to define and enclose meaning, On the other hand, the heart works based on intuition, inspiration; and its job is to discloses spiritual truth. In other words, all the realities that the heart witnesses and experiences, the intellects 'binds them together', into the organic unity of ⟪ar:توحید⟫ through reason, which is an exercise in logic - negation and affirmation; constriction and expansion.

This principle is expressed in the following statements of Maulana Ali, each of which requires deep reflection. He says:
مَنْ عَرَفَ نَفسُہُ تَجَرَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ النَّاسَ تَفَرَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ الدُّنیاَ تَزَہَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ

Before we look at these statements, we must first understand the meaning of the word ⟪ar:عرف⟫. There are two words َ⟪ar:عَلِم⟫and َ⟪ar:عَرَف⟫. The word Maulana Ali (AS) used here is not ⟪ar:عَلِم⟫but ⟪ar:عَرَف⟫. So let's first understand the difference between the two words. The word ⟪ar:عَلِم⟫means to gain knowledge or to understand the factual reality of something. The word ⟪ar:عَرَف⟫is a higher form of understanding which has to do with recognizing something's essence; or recognizing the reality of something in its entirety without any obscurity. To have a perfectly clear vision of something. علم can be partial and incomplete, but ⟪ar:عرف⟫is perfect and complete. The word ⟪ar:اعراف⟫means heights, or "high ground", and it comes from the same root word. What happens when you reach a higher ground? You gain a clear view of the sky as well as land. You gain a perfect view of the terrain. So ⟪ar:عَرَف⟫means knowledge with complete and perfect clarity.  Now with this understanding let's look at these sayings of Maulana Ali (AS).
مَنْ عَرَفَ نَفسُہُ تَجَرَّدُ

He who **truly**recognizes the essence of his soul disengages himself. The one who witnesses the ugliness, the triviality, and the flaws of ones desires, caprices and whims - runs away from it. Because it is within human nature to repel the ugly and the fowl. Then he says:
مَنْ عَرَفَ النَّاسَ تَفَرَّدُ

He who **truly**recognizes the essence of people, isolates himself. Because as Saint Augustine said: "Hell is other people". They are a never ending ocean of demands and expectation that can never be fulfilled. People are disappointments waiting to happen. We're talking about people who are a slave of their own whims and caprice. This is why the word ⟪ar:ناس⟫is used - common folk. Then he says:
مَنْ عَرَفَ الدُّنیاَ تَزَہَّدُ

He who truly knows the essence of this world withholds himself from it. He comes to recognize what a dangerous place this world is with its temptations and distractions. Why? Because it distracts us and takes us away from our creator. So the one recognizes that reality withholds himself from it. Then he says:
مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ

He who truly recognizes Allah integrates himself with his creator.

If we contemplate on these statements, we see that Maulana Ali (AS) mentions three entities of disengagement or disassociation. The self, people, and ⟪ar:دنیا⟫ . Then he mentions one entity of integration which is Allah - ⟪ar:مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ⟫ - He who truly recognizes Allah integrates himself with him; he becomes like him. The key point to understand here, is that one cannot reach the stage of integration with Allah, without first disengaging from the self; from people and from this ephemeral world. And one cannot disengage from these 3 traps without realizing their true essence.

This does not mean becoming a hermit. He's talking about a spiritual disassociation. The goal is to live in this world among everyone, but to make Allah our ultimate desire. This is function of the intellect. So you can see how the spiritual heart recognizes the reality of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), but the intellect then binds and defines it all together through the process of disassociation and association.

Now what's really interesting, is that when a person integrates himself with Allah, that very shift in his consciousness and character elevates his self, and makes him beloved to people and to this world. How hard do we all try to be loved by people and fit into this world? In society? In school? At work? The more we try, the more we are rejected by both. But according to Maulana Ali (AS), once we disengage from them, and integrate with Allah; once we become like Allah, the natural outcome of that shift in consciousness and character, is that we will be loved by both. Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) and Maulana Ali (AS) are the two perfect examples of this principle in action, who were loved by not just friends but also by their enemies; not just 1400 years ago, but throughout time to this very day! SubhanAllah!

# {12:08} Sincerity

Maulana Ali (AS) refers to this total adherence to the Real as 'sincerity', ⟪ar:صدق⟫or ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫. What does ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫mean? The term ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫generally means truthfulness or sincerity. But the root word ⟪ar:خَلُصَ⟫means to be utterly pure. It means to be true to one's essence; to be unadulterated by any extraneous substance. Maulana Ali says:
ُأَخْلَصُ للہِ العِلم وَ العَمَل وَالحُبْ وَ العَدٰوَۃ، وَالقَبْضُ و المَغَادِرَۃ، وَالنُّطْقُ وَ الصُّمْت

Make purely for Allah your action and your knowledge, your love and your hatred, your taking and your leaving, and your speech and your silence

To be totally sincere, means to make oneself, and ones desires 'purely' for Allah, without any stain of impurity. What is impurity in this context? A desire for anything that is other than Allah present in one's devotion; in ones action; in ones intention and every other sentiment. One must love and hate only in function of one's relationship with Allah. Of all the tendencies, egotism is the most harmful sentiment that insinuate itself within one's motivations and intentions. It gets in the way of our sincerity. The act of ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫or 'pure sincerity', is the active effort to **be**, **think**and **act**purely for Allah - with complete selflessness! This is why Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) placed so much stress on this holy war within the soul by calling it the 'ultimate battle' - ⟪ar:جھاد الاکبر⟫. One who succeeds in this battle attains the rank of the 'righteous martyr' - ⟪ar:الشھید⟫. The martyr who sacrifices his life in the path of Allah. What does he sacrifice in this case? He sacrifices his lower self, his ego, for the sake of Allah. Why does he do that? Because he recognizes that only with Allah; only by being integrated with Allah; only by the act of ⟪ar:توحید⟫he can attain real and eternal life. This is called true witnessing - ⟪ar:الشھادۃ⟫. The word ⟪ar:شھید⟫means a witness. A witness is someone who sees and event take place.

We have all heard and read about people who took part in revolutions and freedom movements and they give up their lives for their cause. They literally witnessed that freedom first hand, even though the actual freedom took place years after their sacrifice. But they were so certain of that outcome that they sacrificed their lives for that notion. That's ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫; That is the test of pure sincerity. That's a ⟪ar:شھید⟫. Otherwise without that level of certainty and conviction, one wouldn't sacrifice a penny, let alone their own lives for a cause, right?

So the one who can become a ⟪ar:الشھید⟫in this ultimate battle ⟪ar:جھاد الاکبر⟫ ; the one who give up his desires, his caprice and the whims of his lower self, his ego, is the one whose intellect has truly witnessed the beatific vision of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). Because the intellect is the only faculty within the human consciousness that capable of objectively looking upon one's own soul as 'the other' in relation to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). Maulana Ali (AS) says: 'Struggling against ones soul through knowledge is the mark of the intellect'. In other words, the true intellectual in this battle is the one who bears 'witness'; the ⟪ar:شاھد⟫.  By sacrificing his ego, his ⟪ar:نفس⟫; his false god, that opposes the reality of Allah, he truly bears witness to Allah! To have perfect ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫then, is to rid oneself of all hypocritical self-preoccupation, and to install in its place, a sincere theocentricity - a mode of existence that centers around theology; an existence that revolves around Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) and not ones ego.

# {18:43} Worship - Tool For Sincerity

This gift of ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫that one presents to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) is expressed in this verse that we recite everyday in our prayers:

Cattle [6:162]
[ سورۃ ۶] الانعام

‏قُلْ إِنَّ صَلَاتِى وَنُسُكِى وَمَحْيَاىَ وَمَمَاتِى لِلَّهِ رَبِّ ٱلْعَلَمِينَ ‎۱۶۲
Say, "Indeed, my prayer, my sacrifice, my life and my death are for Allah, master of the worlds.

Notice that prayer, ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫is the first thing that is mentioned in this verse. This is of particular significance for anyone who wishes to appreciate the true nature of Maulana Ali's spiritual ethos. Because worship and devotion played an indispensable role in his life. When we study his life, one of the key distinguishing features of Maulana Ali's spirituality, is found in the stress that he places upon prayer - not just ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫, but all its formal forms - worship, supplications, meditation, reflection, remembrance, contemplation. He considered all theses as tools by which one can orient the soul towards Allah and attune ones self with the Ultimate Real (⟪ar:الحق⟫).

This is why ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫ is considered such an integral aspect of ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫. Worship or ⟪ar:عبودیۃ⟫is something that naturally flows out of ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫. It sustains a fundamental dimension of the human soul. Once the soul witnesses the glory of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), his sincerity turns into a force within his soul that yearns to worship. It can now only be satisfied by the sustenance that is achieved through worship. Just as the body craves food when hungry; and it craves water when thirsty; the soul craves for its spiritual sustenance through worship. The soul reaches the point, where it now perceives worship as giving the rightful owner its due. Remember how the author said that the true intellectual perceives his own soul as "the other" in relation to Allah. So what he is emphasizing here is that in this spiritual context, if ⟪ar:عبادۃ⟫or worship is marginalized, it compromises ones sincerity. Lack of the desire to worship is a demonstration that one is not sincere with Allah; one is not being 'just' to Allah; because he is not rendering Allah his due; and if he's not rendering Allah his due, he is also not rendering his own soul its due. So what we learn from this spiritual logic of Maulana Ali, is that if one is not just with Allah, he cannot be just with ones own self; and therefore he cannot be just to others. This is why he says: "Whoever establishes well-being between himself and Allah, Allah establishes well-being between him and mankind"; because they're all connected. The Quran confirms this principle:

Al-Hijr [15:99]
[ سورۃ ۱۵] الحجر

‏وَٱعْبُدْ رَبَّكَ حَتَّىٰ يَأْتِيَكَ ٱلْيَقِينُ ‎۹۹
And worship your Master until **certainty**comes unto you

You'd think Allah would say, "Worship your master till **you**reach certainty". But no! He says "Worship your master till certainty reaches you"! Because certainty, sincerity ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫is a gift bestowed upon the one who strives for it. In other words, worship is a tool provided to the soul to achieve and earn sincerity or ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫. The more one worships, the more certainty he will be blessed with, making him more sincere with Allah; and the more sincere he becomes with Allah, the more his soul will crave to worship Allah. So what this does is, it creates a circular pattern, which inevitably ends in the beatific vision of Allah. The person reaches the rank of those who possess ⟪ar:یقین⟫- complete and unshakable certainty. They are the ones who become ⟪ar:شھید⟫- the true witness bearers of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

Now let's to bring it all together. What we've learnt so far, is that Allah created our souls with buried treasures of ⟪ar:معرفۃ⟫that require a quest for knowledge. The quest for knowledge in turn, demands a total dedication and orientation of the soul in order to unearth its inner riches. When one acquires this knowledge, it transforms ones consciousness through a total concentration on the divine Reality. That's the phenomenon! That's the mechanism! That's how this spiritual process works. But how do we accomplish that? What's the tool? the apparatus given to us to make it all happen? It's worship and specially ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫which is one of its deepest modes. As we practice this dedication through prayer, it increases in intensity; and eventually reaches a point where there remains no room in one's consciousness for anything other than the object to which one is dedicated—the **remembrance of Allah** (⟪ar:ذکر اللہ⟫). A remembrance and a focus that is so powerful, that one forgets everything and everyone else, including oneself.

However, this exercise requires a tremendous and consistent effort in order for it to be effective. It requires a presence of mind, and a serious engagement of the will. This state of perfection cannot be achieved, if one prays absent-mindedly in a mechanical fashion. This is why Maulana Ali says, 'He who prays without exertion is like someone who throws an arrow without a bow.' If you toss an arrow with your hand towards a target, what will happen? It won't even reach the target, much less penetrate it, right? So Maulana Ali says that the one who prays absent-mindedly without any exertion, his prayers do not even come close to accomplishing its intended goal of reaching Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) to achieve its divine sustenance.

# {27:56} Knowledge Makes Worship Effortless

Similarly, there is nothing passive or easy about the process of acquiring knowledge. He says, 'Knowledge calls out for action; if it is answered, it becomes beneficial; otherwise it departs.' He says: 'when you hear knowledge being imparted, cleave to it, and do not adulterate it with jest, otherwise your hearts will spew it out. In other words, knowledge that is not taken seriously will be of no benefit. It remains 'undigested' and refuses to be assimilated by the 'heart'. The spiritual heart has to properly digest that knowledge in order to gain the 'reality of insight', enabling it 'see and witness' Allah.

Then he says something very profound. He says, it is not the individual who assimilates knowledge; but rather it is knowledge that assimilates the individual. What that means is that in the beginning, the individual seeks knowledge, and through that knowledge he cultivates his consciousness; he starts becoming "alive" in the real sense of the word. But then as he continues on this quest, there comes a point, where life now 'seeks' the individual. He begins to fully realizes his potential. You see? In the beginning, the individual makes an active effort to 'integrate' knowledge within his being; but at a certain point, this activity is transformed into passive receptivity; where he is now constantly and effortlessly receiving his divine sustenance from his creator. knowledge, which is true life, now integrates and assimilates the individual within itself. In other words, there is no more strive; there is no more effort. The entire process becomes effortless. Habit turns into instinct, you see?

This is why if we examine that statement of Maulana Ali to ⟪ar:کمیل⟫; he said
هَجَمَ بِهِمُ الْعِلْمُ عَلَى حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ

'Through them **knowledge**penetrates the reality of insight.' He did not say, "The scholar penetrates the reality of insight". Because it is not the scholars, but knowledge itself that penetrates the reality of insight. However, it does it through them; because these scholars have completely effaced themselves within their knowledge. They essentially become a shadow of their knowledge. You know how our shadow mimics our actions? Who makes our shadow move?  Who controls our shadow? We do? Does the shadow have to make an effort to move? No! We make the effort. The shadow just moves effortlessly, right? In the same way once knowledge is fully integrated into the scholars being, he relegates his control to his knowledge and let's his knowledge take control of his actions. He becomes the shadow and his knowledge becomes the driver. SubhanAllah!

# {32:23} Joy Of Integrating Knowledge

Then he states the outcome of that state, where knowledge has assimilated the scholar within itself. He says:
وَبَاشَرُوا رُوحَ الْيَقِينِ

They rejoice in their intimacy with the spirit of certainty. This sentence helps us appreciate the sheer happiness that flows from the 'life of the spirit' - ⟪ar:وبشِّرُ روح الیقین⟫ - they rejoice in the spirit of their certainty. The word ⟪ar:بَاشَرُوا⟫comes from  ⟪ar:بشِّرُ⟫means having intimate experience of a thing by coming into direct physical contact with it. This is why the act of intimacy is called ⟪ar:مباشرۃ⟫. But the usage of this word is strictly restricted to the experience of joy. So ⟪ar:بشِّرُ⟫is not just coming into contact with something; it is coming in contact with something that results in pleasure, joy and happiness. Therefore a better, and a more complete translation of ⟪ar:وَبَاشَرُوا⟫⟪ar:روح الیقین⟫would be that they 'rejoice in the experience of their intimate contact of certainty with their master'.

But we must understand this intimacy properly. To touch this 'spirit of certainty' is to come into contact with not with some cold logical fact or the conclusion of a purely mental process. The contact we're talking about means engaging deeply and intimately with that object of certainty, which is this context is Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). It's one thing to read a book about the beauty and fragrance of a rose; but it is an entirely different experience to hold a rose in our hands and appreciate and enjoy its beauty and fragrance, Right? That is ⟪ar:بشِّرُ⟫ So that is the kind of intimate experience these scholars experience who penetrate the reality of insight, and then rejoice in its spirit of certainty. This reality of the divine, this level of experiential understanding of our creator, is infinitely more beautiful and joyous than any conceivable bliss of paradise. This is why according to the Quran, the divine contentment ⟪ar:رِضوَان⟫ is 'greater' than Paradise itself.

Repentance [9:72]
[ سورۃ ۹] التوبۃ

‏وَعَدَ ٱللَّهُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ وَٱلْمُؤْمِنَتِ جَنَّتٍۢ تَجْرِى مِن تَحْتِهَا ٱلْأَنْهَرُ خَلِدِينَ فِيهَا وَمَسَكِنَ طَيِّبَةًۭ فِى جَنَّتِ عَدْنٍۢ ۚ **وَرِضْوَنٌۭ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ أَكْبَرُ** ۚ ذَلِكَ هُوَ ٱلْفَوْزُ ٱلْعَظِيمُ ‎۷۲
Allah has promised the believing men and believing women gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein they abide eternally, and pleasant dwellings in gardens of perpetual residence; but approval from Allah is greater. It is that which is the great attainment.

So what we learn here is that the soul that effectively opens itself up to the divine reality of its creator, cannot help, but experience a tremendous amount of joy. Maulana Ali (AS) said: 'Learning and reflecting upon knowledge is the true delight of scholars.' In other words, the true intellectual (⟪ar:العاقل⟫), is the most joyous being within all of humanity because he has come in contact with realities that is fundamental to his or her own being. Allah created us in his own image, therefore any knowledge of our creator is essentially the knowledge of our own potential. It's a knowledge of what we're capable of! It is a kind of self-discovery. It is like the joy of returning home from a long exile of ignorance. it is like the joy of coming to life after having experienced the stagnancy of death. This is why, the most intellectual of people are the ones who are closest to Allah and therefore the most alive. To be close to Allah is to be truly alive, because Allah is ⟪ar:الحی⟫The Ever-Living!

## The Jurist

Then the author talks about the most fundamental ingredient in leadership - ⟪ar:رحم⟫mercy! He defines a jurist in the words of Maulana Ali (AS). What is a jurist? A jurist is someone who is an expert in Islamic jurisprudence; someone who understands the applications and rulings of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫. That's a ⟪ar:فقیہ⟫. Maulana Ali (AS) says: a "true" jurist is someone who has recognized and experienced Allah's magnificence and his glory, in addition to learning the Islamic rulings. He is someone who is inspired and infused with the spirit of Allah, and is in that state of intimate joy and unshakable certainty of the divine kindness and compassion of his master. That, he says, is a true ⟪ar:فقیہ⟫. He is someone who truly understands the love of Allah behind the rulings and implementations of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫. He is someone who does not make people despair in the mercy of Allah and the gracious spirit of Allah (⟪ar:رَوح اللہ⟫). Why? Because he himself is immersed in that constant state of joy and happiness, flowing from that certainty of Allah's mercy. So now for him, there is absolutely no room for any despair. This is why it is a sign of disbelief to despair of this mercy of Allah. Allah says:

Al-Hijr [15:56]
[ سورۃ ۱۵] الحجر

‏قَالَ وَمَن يَقْنَطُ مِن رَّحْمَةِ رَبِّهِۦٓ إِلَّا ٱلضَّآلُّونَ ‎۵۶
He said, "And who despairs of the mercy of his Lord except for those astray?"

Cattle [6:54]
[ سورۃ ۶] الانعام

‏وَإِذَا جَآءَكَ ٱلَّذِينَ يُؤْمِنُونَ بِـَٔايَتِنَا فَقُلْ سَلَمٌ عَلَيْكُمْ ۖ كَتَبَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَىٰ نَفْسِهِ ٱلرَّحْمَةَ ۖ أَنَّهُۥ مَنْ عَمِلَ مِنكُمْ سُوٓءًۢا بِجَهَلَةٍۢ ثُمَّ تَابَ مِنۢ بَعْدِهِۦ وَأَصْلَحَ فَأَنَّهُۥ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ ‎۵۴
And when those come to you who believe in Our verses, say, "Peace be upon you. Your Lord has decreed upon Himself mercy: that any of you who does wrong out of ignorance and then repents after that and corrects himself - indeed, He is Forgiving and Merciful."

Look what Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) is saying:
كَتَبَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَىٰ نَفْسِهِ ٱلرَّحْمَةَ

Your master has prescribed for himself - mercy.  ⟪ar:كَتَبَ⟫ - He has written it on his throne. His mercy is set in writing! it cannot be changed. He has declared that no matter what his creation does, his response is always going to be mercy. His rewards will be out of mercy; his punishments will be out of mercy. And therefore the "true" jurist, is someone who truly understands that divine injunction and applies it to his own self in all matters of jurisprudence. SubhanAllah!

This is something we really need to reflect upon. How easily do we make people despair in the mercy of Allah! Oh you do this and Allah will throw your worship back in your face! Oh he's going to the hellfire; she's going to the hellfire. Allah will never forgive you for what you did - ⟪ar:أعوذ باللہ⟫. We should really take a moment to reflect on the kind of jurist we are to the people in our lives.

Anyway, this is the beauty of a jurist that Maulana Ali (AS) defined. Their unwavering belief in Allah's ⟪ar:رحمۃ⟫and then disseminating that mercy to others is the greatest virtue of all.

# {43:35} Beauty Of Virtues

Virtues are what the intellect finds beautiful. This appreciation of beauty is not merely a question of enjoying aesthetics; like appreciating a beautiful scenery or a painting; because that is subjective. What is beautiful to me might not be beautiful to you. The beauty Maulana Ali (AS) is referring to, is a very objective aspect of the intellect. It is a beauty that each and every soul can appreciate, which is coined by the term ⟪ar:احسان⟫. The Arabic word ⟪ar:احسان⟫even through simply translated as 'virtue' or 'excellence', actually means '**making beautiful**'. ٰIt comes from the word ⟪ar:حُسن⟫  which means 'beauty. Because virtue is something that **makes**the soul beautiful - they are called ⟪ar:احسان⟫. Maulana Ali (AS) says:
تَفُوقُ العقل، جمال الظواہر والبواطن

The excellence of the intellect, is in appreciating the beauty of outward and inward things. In other words, the outward beauty of forms and actions must be complemented by their inward beauty of meaning. Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said in a HADEES:
أنَّ اللہ جمیلٌ و یُحِبُّ الجمال

Indeed Allah is beautiful and He loves beauty. He also said:
اتخذو من اخلاق اللہ

Assume the character traits of Allah

So what we're learning is that because Allah **loves beauty**, this cultivation of 'beautiful traits' of ⟪ar:أخلاق⟫within oneself, is what makes one lovable to Allah. Now how do we do that? By emulating the the virtues of our prophet (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) who was at the pinnacle of ⟪ar:أخلاق⟫.

The Pen [68:4]
[ سورۃ ۶۸] القلم

‏وَإِنَّكَ لَعَلَىٰ خُلُقٍ عَظِيمٍۢ ‎۴
And indeed, you are on an exalted standard of character.

This is why Allah says:

The Family of Imran [3:31]
[ سورۃ ۳] آل عمران

‏قُلْ إِن كُنتُمْ تُحِبُّونَ ٱللَّهَ فَٱتَّبِعُونِى يُحْبِبْكُمُ ٱللَّهُ وَيَغْفِرْ لَكُمْ ذُنُوبَكُمْ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ ‎۳۱
Say :"If you love Allah then follow me, Allah will love you and forgive you of your sins. And Allah is Oft-Forgiving, Most Merciful."

This is how we can attract Allah towards us - by making ourselves beautiful in the truest sense; in character. Maulana Ali (AS) says:
أَوَّلُ العَقل التَّوَدُّدْ

The foundation of the intellect is the assimilation of love. The word he used here is ⟪ar:تَّوَدُّدْ⟫which means to make oneself lovable. He could have used other words for love like ⟪ar:وُد⟫or ⟪ar:مَوَدَّۃ⟫. No! He said ⟪ar:تَّوَدُّدْ⟫. He wants to draw our attention to this need of making oneself lovable to Allah. That can only be achieved through the process of first, integrating and realizing the principle of love within oneself, and then, expressing and manifesting that inner love, outwardly. Love is therefore understood as both: outward radiance and inner attraction.

So what we're learning here is by following in the footsteps of the Prophet, and cultivating the 'prophetic' virtues, we can make ourselves receptive to the divine influx of the love of Allah. Allah loves beauty; therefore we must make ourselves beautiful in our character; with qualities such as kindness, contentment, courtesy, generosity, bravery, modesty, wisdom, forbearance. These are all the moral qualities that one will find in the soul of the true intellectual. It is through the presence of these virtues, that the intellect is able to develop the dimensions of depth and breadth that we talked about earlier. Virtues that give substance and a 3 dimensional form to our actions. Without virtue, all actions would remain confined to a flat, horizontal plane of operation. Without virtue, we would still pray, but that prayer would be simply going through the motions. There is no depth of emotion; there is no love in that prayer. There is no appreciation of beauty in that prayer, you see?

In the previous session we looked at the importance of refraining from vices; because they cloud the intellect. But just refraining from vices is not enough. The intellect then needs to nourish the soul through the development of virtue; and once those virtues are touched by the graces of faith, one becomes aware of all that lies beneath the surface of his own field of activity. One comes to know the 'hidden treasures' of the intellect. One begins to understand the need to 'integrate' the entire the soul with the divine virtues; and this integration, or 'making one', is the fundamental goal of ⟪ar:توحید⟫. which is the dynamic process of **spiritual unification**.

**Truth** inspires the soul; but then **virtue**provides the intellect with the (⟪ar:خمیر⟫) , the leaven, which allows it to expand to its full dimensions. Sins and immoral qualities on the other hand create numerous blind spots or intellectual dysfunctions. The heart gets **diseased** by vices, and they blind the eye of the spiritual heart rendering it unable to 'see' the Real. Allah says in the Quran:

The Cow [2:10]
[ سورۃ ۲] البقرۃ

‏فِى قُلُوبِهِم مَّرَضٌۭ فَزَادَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ مَرَضًۭا ۖ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌۢ بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَكْذِبُونَ ‎۱۰
In their hearts is a disease and Allah has increased their disease. A painful torment is theirs because they used to tell lies.

The Pilgrimage [22:46]
[ سورۃ ۲۲] الحج

‏أَفَلَمْ يَسِيرُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَتَكُونَ لَهُمْ قُلُوبٌۭ يَعْقِلُونَ بِهَآ أَوْ آذَانٌۭ يَسْمَعُونَ بِهَا ۖ فَإِنَّهَا لَا تَعْمَى ٱلْأَبْصَرُ وَلَكِن تَعْمَى ٱلْقُلُوبُ ٱلَّتِى فِى ٱلصُّدُورِ ‎۴۶
Have they not traveled through the land, and have they hearts wherewith to understand and ears wherewith to hear? Indeed, it is not the eyes that grow blind, but it is the hearts which are in the breasts that grow blind.

<!-- section 10 (id=116, raw_sort=10): Deliverance -->

## Deliverance

We're continuing our discussion and analysis of this discourse given by Maulana Ali (AS) to ⟪ar:کمیل ابن زیاد⟫ in which he pointed out the signs of true scholars and sages. In the previous session, we discussed the concept of sincerity ⟪ar:أخلاص⟫, and how ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫or prayer in particular, and all worship in general are tools for the expression of that sincerity. We looked at how knowledge makes worship effortless, and the enormous joy that flows out of certainty. We finally looked at the beauty of virtue, and how it makes us receptive to the divine influx of the love of Allah, because Allah loves beauty. Beauty in character provides our ⟪ar:اسلام⟫with the dimensions of ⟪ar:ایمان⟫and ⟪ar:احسان⟫, giving our worship a 3 dimensional form. On the other hand vices make our soul ugly. They are like blemishes on the face our ⟪ar:اسلام⟫that makes us unattractive to Allah; and in the final analysis all our vices stem from the desires of our ego, defined by the term caprice or ⟪ar:ھوی⟫. Desires that are oriented towards the acquisition of this temporary and transient ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. To help us counter that, Allah gave us an intellect with the purpose of balancing the requirements of ⟪ar:دنیا⟫against the ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫. in order to succeed.

# Deliverance

In the words of Maulana Ali, "The one whose intellect dominates his caprice, attains deliverance - ⟪ar:فلاح⟫. The word ⟪ar:فلاح⟫in Arabic is a very comprehensive word. It includes the meaning of salvation as well as immense blessedness. Interesting the word ⟪ar:فلاح⟫was used in ancient Arabic when a bedouin returned back to his tent after travelling through the scorching heat of the desert. They understood ⟪ar:فلاح⟫as the comforting pleasure of a shade after a long journey in the heat of the sun. But what's unique about the word ⟪ar:فلاح⟫is that this deliverance is not a distant state in the future; rather it is a present state. So when Maulana Ali (AS) says, "The one whose intellect dominates his caprice, attains deliverance", what he's truly saying is that the one who gains control over his lower self, attains this state of salvation and immense blessedness in the present - in the here and now; in this worldly life. You see?This ⟪ar:فلاح⟫is not some grace granted after ones demise. This deliverance involves tasting that joy, which flows from that intimate contact with the spirit of certainty, in the present - right here in this world.

Now what is required to cultivate receptivity for this spirit of certainty? First and foremost it is obedience to the rules and regulations of the revealed Law (⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫). But then those actions have to be complemented and combined with purity of intention, and sincerity of devotion, which was expressed by the word ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫. Without this sincerity or purity of the heart, complying with the formal rules of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫, according to Maulana Ali (AS), is nothing but hypocrisy (⟪ar:نفاق⟫) and pretension (⟪ar:رِیا⟫). These two traits (⟪ar:نفاق⟫and ⟪ar:ریا⟫) are defined by Maulana Ali as the gravest of all sins. He actually considers them ⟪ar:شرک⟫, which is  the attribution of 'partners' to Allah. Because if one is observing ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫with the intention of showing off ones piety to people, he has partnered the pleasure of creation with the pleasure of the creator. His worship is no longer pure. The love of Allah is now mixed in with the love of people. This is why Maulana Ali (AS) says: "Understand that the slightest pretension, the slightest (⟪ar:ریا⟫) is polytheism. It is ⟪ar:شرک⟫". This sentence can actually be taken as a comment upon one of the most important condemnations of hypocrisy found in ⟪ar:سورۃ الماعون⟫.

Small Kindnesses [107:1-7]
[ سورۃ ۱۰۷] الماعون

‏أَرَءَيْتَ ٱلَّذِى يُكَذِّبُ بِٱلدِّينِ ‎۱
Have you seen him who willingly denies the Recompense? The word ⟪ar:يُكَذِّبُ⟫comes from ⟪ar:کذب⟫which means to lie. A lie is an intentional false statement. So have you seen the one who intentionally falsifies the notion of recompense?

‏فَذَلِكَ ٱلَّذِى يَدُعُّ ٱلْيَتِيمَ ‎۲
What is his sign? His sign, his mark is that you will see him repulse the orphan, 

‏وَلَا يَحُضُّ عَلَىٰ طَعَامِ ٱلْمِسْكِينِ ‎۳
And you will see that he does not urge others to feed the poor 

‏فَوَيْلٌۭ لِّلْمُصَلِّينَ ‎۴
So woe unto those performers of prayers. I pity the following types of worshipers. Who are they?

‏ٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ عَن صَلَاتِهِمْ سَاهُونَ ‎۵
Those who delay their ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫ from their prescribed times. This is a very scary verse. Not woe unto those who miss their ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫. Allah is saying woe unto those who delay their prayers, because they feel they have something more important to do.

‏ٱلَّذِينَ هُمْ يُرَآءُونَ ‎۶
I pity those who do good deeds only to show others

‏وَيَمْنَعُونَ ٱلْمَاعُونَ ‎۷
And I pity those who refuse or withhold simple assistance from others

This short ⟪ar:سورۃ⟫makes it clear that to be 'religious' in one's formal acts of devotion, has absolutely no value whatsoever - unless those acts are integrated into a life of virtue; and unless those actions are carried out with devotion, and a sincere intention of pleasing Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). In other words, actions are useless without ⟪ar:اخلاص⟫. Without these dimensions of ⟪ar:ایمان⟫and ⟪ar:احسان⟫, the outward form of ⟪ar:اسلام⟫ is meaningless. Maulana Ali (AS) says:

"There is no religion for the one who has no intellect. Action without knowledge is error, for what counts is not the action, but the sincere intention motivating that action. For action — all action, is dust, except what is purified with sincerity."

So what we're learning here is that the focus of Maulana Ali, found in his spiritual ethos, is essentially a detachment from action, and an orientation towards an inner state of being; an inner state of existence, that is sincerely and completely integrated with Allah. A state from which good actions cannot help but emerge. You understand? For those of us who have developed some level of spirituality, we push ourselves, or we disciple ourselves to focus on the outward formal actions. We get punctual with ⟪ar:صوم⟫ and ⟪ar:صلوۃ⟫.  But what we find in the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS), is a focus, not on action itself, but rather on the inner essence, the spirit and purpose of those actions, achieved through a self-effacement within the knowledge of Allah. As a result of that integration, that ⟪ar:توحید⟫, that he had with his master, his outward actions of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫ would effortlessly flow out of him. It's a very interesting concept. He sets our focus on the ⟪ar:باطن⟫of the actions; which once achieved, will naturally produce the ⟪ar:ظاھر⟫. This concept is validated by the Quran, when Allah talks about the sacrifice of animals that people perform on the day of Hajj. He says:

The Pilgrimage [22:37]
[ سورۃ ۲۲] الحج

‏لَن يَنَالَ ٱللَّهَ لُحُومُهَا وَلَا دِمَآؤُهَا **وَلَكِن يَنَالُهُ ٱلتَّقْوَىٰ مِنكُمْ** ‎۳۷
Their meat will not reach Allah, nor will their blood, but what reaches Him is your piety. 

What Allah is really interested in is the reason, the intention, the understanding and sincerity with which you sacrificed the animal; not the meat and the blood of the animal itself.

## The Kharijites

Now as for Maulana Ali (AS) statement: There is no religion for the one who has no intellect, no group better exemplified this principle than the ⟪ar:خارجی⟫ who battled Maulana Ali in the battle of ⟪ar:نحروان⟫ which took place right after the battle of ⟪ar:سفین⟫. This group comprised of numerous reciters of the Quran. These were people who possessed considerable religious prestige on account of their knowledge of the Quran, and the intensity of their worship. They would stay up all night in prayer, and they all had deep dark marks of ⟪ar:سجود⟫on their foreheads.

Their piety however, was more apparent than it was real. It was for display, it was simply show-off. This is why when someone from Maulana Ali's camp asked him:⟪ar:یا علی⟫How can we fight these people? They stay up all night in prayers and most of them know the Quran by heart". Maulana Ali (AS) replied quite sharply: "Sleeping with certainty is better than praying with doubt". Which is a very profound statement.
النوم مع اليقين أفضل من الصلاة مع الشك

Sleeping with certainty is far better than worship filled with doubt

Because certainty, as we have learnt previously, imparts tremendous peace and joy. So for the one who is immersed in what Maulana Ali calls 'the spirit of certainty', his sleep is a bodily repose that further enhances the joy that flows from a good conscience and spiritual certitude. Even the sleep of such a person becomes an act of pleasure for him. Because this spiritual certitude is understood as a kind of permanent 'wakefulness'. Even as he sleeps, this person is awake to the higher realities, you see? This is why Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said in a HADEES: "My eyes sleep, but my heart does not". Why? Because every single beat of his heart was drenched in the fragrance of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫.

So what Maulana Ali (AS) means when he says: There is no religion for the one who has no intellect, what he means is that the the true intellectual, the ⟪ar:عاقل⟫ is the one who is obedient to the divine injunction to pray. But rather than praying in a mechanical fashion and believing that the action alone suffices, he understands that such obedience, although necessary, is not a sufficient condition for achieving Allah's grace. There must be consciousness of what one is doing and why one is doing it. It is this awareness, together with inner beauty of virtue or ⟪ar:احسان⟫, that attracts the divine acceptance. This is why Maulana Ali (AS) says: 'The most worthy knowledge is that by which actions are accepted by Allah. Because if Allah doesn't accept our worship, what's the point of the effort? right? You and I would not accept insincere praise. We would not accept an insincere invitation, would we? When we don't find insincerity befitting us (as creations), how can we imagine it befitting for our creator?

# Befriending The Harsh

So now that we understand the in-depth and profound meanings of these statements to ⟪ar:حضرت کمیل⟫, let's review them again to really appreciate them. Talking about these sincere scholars who have developed this inward beauty of virtue, he said:
هَجَمَ بِهِمُ الْعِلْمُ عَلَى حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ

Through them, knowledge penetrates the reality of insight
وَبَاشَرُوا رُوحَ الْيَقِينِ

They rejoice in their intimacy with the spirit of certainty. Then he says:
وَاسْتَلاَنُوا مَا اسْتَوْعَرَهُ الْمُتْرَفُونَ

they make easy what the extravagant find harsh;
وَأَنِسُوا بِمَا اسْتَوْحَشَ مِنْهُ الْجَاهِلُونَ

they befriend that by which the ignorant are estranged.

This next statement now talks to us about the impact made upon the soul of these true scholars, by their remembrance and their constant awareness of the inevitability of death! Their realization, that death will ultimately force them to renounce this world. Because it is this renunciation, this abandonment of the pleasures of ⟪ar:دنیا⟫, that the extravagant (the ⟪ar:جاھل⟫) finds harsh. A ⟪ar:جاھل⟫is someone who has no restraint; who always reacts to a stimulus rather than respond to it. Also, the word ⟪ar:اسْتَوْحَشَ⟫used for these people comes from ⟪ar:وحش⟫which means wild. Wild animals are called ⟪ar:وحشی⟫. They are always in a reactionary mode. They function based on impulse, not intellect, you see? A ⟪ar:جاھل⟫is the exact same way. He is someone who can very easily be put in a reactionary mode. And so when such people feel a sense of abandonment, they perceive it as adversity, as difficulties, as deprivation. Now the problem for these people, is that these are all the exact circumstances, which are stated in the Quran as trials, that inevitably accompany our life in this world.

The Cow [2:155-157]
[ سورۃ ۲] البقرۃ

‏وَلَنَبْلُوَنَّكُم بِشَىْءٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلْخَوْفِ وَٱلْجُوعِ وَنَقْصٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلْأَمْوَلِ وَٱلْأَنفُسِ وَٱلثَّمَرَتِ ۗ وَبَشِّرِ ٱلصَّبِرِينَ ‎۱۵۵
And We will surely test you with something of fear and hunger and a loss of wealth and lives and fruits of labor, but give good tidings to the patient,

‏ٱلَّذِينَ إِذَآ أَصَبَتْهُم مُّصِيبَةٌۭ قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّا لِلَّهِ وَإِنَّآ إِلَيْهِ رَجِعُونَ ‎۱۵۶
Who, when disaster strikes them, say, "Indeed we belong to Allah, and indeed to Him we will return."

‏أُو۟لَٓئِكَ عَلَيْهِمْ صَلَوَتٌۭ مِّن رَّبِّهِمْ وَرَحْمَةٌۭ ۖ وَأُو۟لَٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُهْتَدُونَ ‎۱۵۷
Those are the ones upon whom are blessings from their master and his mercy. And it is those who are the [rightly] guided.

Allah says, the ⟪ar:وَأُو۟لَٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُهْتَدُونَ⟫, they are the ones who have received ⟪ar:ھدایۃ⟫. So the sign of the rightly guided is that they can actually perceive the 'blessings', that are showered down upon them while they are being patient in the face of their afflictions. Their patience comes from the perfect knowledge of the true realities, that they possess about their creator - ⟪ar:الحق⟫. They are able to remain steadfast in the face of trials, because they are firmly rooted in their awareness of their own origin and their end. Their strength comes from their conviction, that  ⟪ar:إِنَّا لِلَّهِ⟫ "Indeed we belong to him; we are his property, and the owner will take his property back - therefore ⟪ar:وَإِنَّآ إِلَيْهِ رَجِعُونَ⟫unto Him we shall return". For this reason, they can 'make easy' the trials and difficulties of life. Because if they lose money, so what? If they get burglarized, so what? Nothing ever belonged to them anyway. If they lose loved ones, they were never their property to begin with. If they lose their own lives, so what? It's only being returned to its owner. It is through this clarity in their knowledge and awareness of their own existence and their possessions, they find the trials of this life easy.

On the other hand, the 'extravagant' (the ⟪ar:جاھل⟫) experiences these exact same events as loses, and they find the experience of that loss harsh and bitter, you see? To them, its their life, their possessions, their things! So a loss; any loss; even the threat of loss; puts them in a reactionary mode. And so the ease, that permanently resides within every difficulty, remains hidden from the ignorant, but is recognized and discerned by the wise. Allah says:

Solace [94:5-6]
[ سورۃ ۹۴] الشرح

‏فَإِنَّ مَعَ ٱلْعُسْرِ يُسْرًا ‎۵
For indeed, with hardship [will be] ease. (Then he says it again)

‏إِنَّ مَعَ ٱلْعُسْرِ يُسْرًۭا ‎۶
Indeed, with hardship [will be] ease.

This is a promise being made by Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). Allah did not say "after" hardship will be ease. He said ⟪ar:مَعَ⟫"with" hardship will be ease. So what hardship does for the wise, is that it create an anticipation of "ease" for them; which they recognize as the inevitable fruit of hardship. They taste this fruit of ease as they are going through, and experiencing the hardship. SubhanAllah!

This is a perfect explanation for the events of ⟪ar:کربلا⟫. Historians have documented, that as the trials of ⟪ar:کربلا⟫increased in their intensity, signs of calmness and comfort became more evident on Imam Hussain's face. Because these sages, these scholars, in the words of Maulana Ali (AS), actually

'befriend that by which the ignorant are estranged'.

⟪ar:وَأَنِسُوا بِمَا اسْتَوْحَشَ مِنْهُ الْجَاهِلُونَ⟫SubhanAllah!

In a sermon Maulana Ali said: 'Death is more intimate to me than the breast of the mother is to the suckling baby'. Why? Because as we learnt in the previous sessions, the mention of trials and difficulties is preceded by a reminder that

The Family of Imran [3:169-170]
[ سورۃ ۳] آل عمران

‏وَلَا تَحْسَبَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ قُتِلُوا۟ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ أَمْوَتًۢا ۚ 
بَلْ أَحْيَآءٌ عِندَ رَبِّهِمْ يُرْزَقُونَ ‎۱۶۹
Do not think those who are killed in the Way of Allah are dead. 
No, they are alive with their master, and they are receiving their provision.

‏فَرِحِينَ بِمَآ آتَىٰهُمُ ٱللَّهُ مِن فَضْلِهِۦ وَيَسْتَبْشِرُونَ بِٱلَّذِينَ لَمْ يَلْحَقُوا۟ بِهِم مِّنْ خَلْفِهِمْ 
أَلَّا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ ‎۱۷۰
They rejoice in what Allah has bestowed upon them of His Bounty, rejoicing for the sake of those who have not yet joined them, 
O n them no fear shall come, nor shall they grieve.

These verses not only teach us about the affirmation of the life in the hereafter, but they also express the esoteric meaning of being 'slain' for Allah, the concept of ⟪ar:الفنا فی اللہ⟫, 'extinction in Allah'. This intimacy with death, mentioned by Maulana Ali, can be seen as this beautiful relationship with 'death' while still being alive. He is waiting to join those virtuous people who have gone before him; as much as they are waiting for him to join them.

Have you every planned a visit to someone you love with all your heart and soul, and who loves you back the same way? What stands between that meeting? Time! How badly do you wait for that time to end so you can be united with your beloved?  That's what he's talking about. Death is more intimate to me than the breast of the mother is to the suckling baby. If you've ever seen a hungry baby take its mothers breast, you can understand Maulana Ali's anticipation and excitement for death to be reunited with these people. MashaAllah!

In another sermon he says: 'I am the death of the dead', which is a very profound saying. Because life in this world itself, is a kind of death. Why? Because it is a limitation. The things that are essential for human existence, such as food, water, warmth - they are always scarce. We are constantly searching for these things and trying to preserve them when we find them; because we fear we will run out of them, right? On the other hand paradise is a realm of infinite abundance. So life in this world is a kind of death; it is a kind of limitation. By the same logic, death in this world is tantamount to true life in the next; because it opens up the door to infinite abundance. As the Quran says:

The Spider [29:64]
[ سورۃ ۲۹] العنکبوت

‏وَمَا هَذِهِ ٱلْحَيَوٰةُ ٱلدُّنْيَآ إِلَّا لَهْوٌۭ وَلَعِبٌۭ ۚ 
وَإِنَّ ٱلدَّارَ ٱلْآخِرَةَ لَهِىَ ٱلْحَيَوَانُ ۚ لَوْ كَانُوا۟ يَعْلَمُونَ ‎۶۴
And this life of the world is only amusement and play! 
Verily, the home of the Hereafter, that is the true life indeed, if they but knew

So Maulana Ali (AS) says "I am the death of the dead". Follow my example if you wish to conquer your souls; Follow my path if you want your intellects to dominate your caprice. It's an incredibly eloquent way to put forth this notion. SubhanAllah!

# Piety & Abstinence

Now it's important to understand that it is not the physical world as such that is to be negated. That is not what Maulana Ali (AS) is saying. Rather what is to be negated is worldliness. You understand the difference? It is the egotistic **attachments** to this world that must be overcome. In this vein, one of the key principles of Maulana Ali's perspective, stressed and repeated throughout ⟪ar:نھج البلاغۃ⟫, is the principle of ⟪ar:زُھد⟫, which cannot be translated by any single word in English. It's a very powerful composite word. It comprises several meanings. It means:
- Asceticism - a severe self-discipline and avoidance of all forms of forbidden indulgences
- Austerity - extreme simplicity in style or appearance
- Abstention, the practice of restraining oneself from indulging in something that is forbidden.
- Detachment
- Doing without
- Making little of what others consider grand in this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫

All these meanings are encapsulated in the word ⟪ar:زُھد⟫.  In one of his sermons, Maulana Ali (AS) eloquently expresses the attitude of the ⟪ar:زاھد⟫ toward this world. He says:

This world is an abode for which annihilation is ordained; and for its people departure has been decreed. It is sweet and green, and it hastens to the one who seeks it. It confounds the heart that gazes upon it. So set out from it with the best of provisions available to you, and ask not from it above what suffices; and seek not from it more than what can be attained.

Now what is this 'best of provision' (⟪ar:زاد⟫) that he mentions in this sermon? It is ⟪ar:تقوی⟫, which is another word that is very difficult to translate. The meaning of ⟪ar:تقوی⟫ includes:
- Piety
- Fear of Allah
- Awe, but most of all a constant awareness and consciousness of Allah

We generally understand ⟪ar:تقوی⟫ as fear of Allah, but that is actually ⟪ar:خوف⟫. When we fear something or someone that is harmful to us, the Arabic word for that is ⟪ar:خوف⟫. What word did Allah use for these virtuous people who have killed their ego; their desires, their caprice and whims?
أَلَّا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ

They will have no fear. Why? Because fear is for the one who worships his ego! You see? Not for the one who has ⟪ar:تقوی⟫. So rather than fear of harm and punishment, the word ⟪ar:تقوی⟫ has the meaning of a fear to offend; a fear to disappoint. Like a child who is afraid to upset his parents out of his love and respect for them. It is not "fear" of their punishment you see? ⟪ar:تقوی⟫ is the fear of disappointing the beloved.

Maulana Ali (AS) says: ⟪ar:یا عباد اللہ⟫'Oh slaves of Allah! I strongly encourage you, to attain ⟪ar:تقوی⟫, which is the true provision ⟪ar:خیر الزاد⟫". When do we need provisions? When we're starting off on a journey, right? When do you accumulate your provisions for your journey? On the way? Or before the journey begins? Before the journey begins! It's call packing!

Death is the beginning of that beautiful journey, and the provision that will sustain us through that journey, is the ⟪ar:تقوی⟫that we collected, preserved and set aside in this world. How do we attain this provision, this ⟪ar:زاد⟫? Through the measure of our detachment from the perishable aspects of the 'life of this world'. Quran states:

The Children of Israel [17:18]
[ سورۃ ۱۷] الاسرا

‏مَّن كَانَ يُرِيدُ ٱلْعَاجِلَةَ عَجَّلْنَا لَهُۥ فِيهَا مَا نَشَآءُ لِمَن نُّرِيدُ 
ثُمَّ جَعَلْنَا لَهُۥ جَهَنَّمَ يَصْلَىٰهَا مَذْمُومًۭا مَّدْحُورًۭا ‎۱۸
Whoever wishes for the quick pleasures of this world, we readily grant it to him - from what we will and for whom we choose. 
Then, afterwards, We have appointed for him Hell, he will burn therein disgraced and rejected,.

This same despicable aspect of this world is described in extremely vivid terms by Maulana Ali in his sayings. He says: (Listen to this)
- By Allah! this world of yours is more contemptible in my eyes, than the chewed-up bone of a pig in the hand of a leper
- The image of this world is that of a snake - soft to touch but filled with deadly poison
- This world is 'as worthless to me, as that which drips from the nose of a goat.

Now the real question is, what is it that made this world so insignificant, rather disgusting and distasteful to the likes of Maulana Ali (AS)? What was it that rendered the beauty of this world ugly for them? Was it hardships and sufferings? No! Because they befriend what the extravagant finds harsh. They actually enjoy hardships! So then what was it? It was the grandeur, the glory and the beauty of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) that made this ⟪ar:دنیا⟫distasteful to them, you see? It is in the light of the Absolute that the pleasures and attractions of this world appear fleeting and illusory. Maulana Ali (AS) says: 'The greatness of the Creator, will belittle creation in your view.'

Praising the ⟪ar:زاھد⟫, this pious man, that Maulana Ali says: "What made him great in my eyes was the insignificance of the world within him". This according to the Quran is the real meaning of ⟪ar:زھد⟫. The one who is great in the eyes of Allah, you will find him insignificant in this world. In ⟪ar:سورۃ یوسف⟫, the brothers of⟪ar:یوسف⟫deemed him of little value, because they sold him for a small price

Joseph [12:20]
[ سورۃ ۱۲] یوسف

‏وَشَرَوْهُ بِثَمَنٍۭ بَخْسٍۢ دَرَهِمَ مَعْدُودَةٍۢ وَكَانُوا۟ فِيهِ مِنَ ٱلزَّهِدِينَ ‎۲۰
And they sold him for a low price, - for a few Dirhams. And they were of those who regarded him insignificant.

The word used here for insignificant is ⟪ar:ٱلزَّهِدِينَ⟫. This world in Maulana Ali's perspective, is likewise deemed of little value. Not because he considers the world as having no value, but because in relation to the offerings of the hereafter, this world becomes insignificant. In relation to eternity; in relation to the glory of ultimate reality, it is rendered all but insignificant. It is like the light of a bulb in comparison to the light of the sun. Because in comparison to the creator, all of creation gets reduced to infinitesimal, fleeting shadows, that are destined for annihilation.

The Story [28:88]
[ سورۃ ۲۸] القصص

كُلُّ شَىْءٍ هَالِكٌ إِلَّا وَجْهَهُۥ ۚ لَهُ ٱلْحُكْمُ وَإِلَيْهِ تُرْجَعُونَ ‎۸۸
Everything will perish except His essence. His is the Decision, and to Him you shall be returned.

# Purpose of the world

So then, if this world is so insignificant, then what is its purpose for us? Why are we here? Quran beautifully answers that question:

The Children of Israel [17:44]
[ سورۃ ۱۷] الاسرا

‏تُسَبِّحُ لَهُ ٱلسَّمَوَتُ ٱلسَّبْعُ وَٱلْأَرْضُ وَمَن فِيهِنَّ ۚ 
وَإِن مِّن شَىْءٍ إِلَّا يُسَبِّحُ بِحَمْدِهِۦ وَلَكِن لَّا تَفْقَهُونَ تَسْبِيحَهُمْ ۗ 
إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ حَلِيمًا غَفُورًۭا ‎۴۴
The seven heavens and the earth and all that is therein, glorify Him 
and there is not a thing but glorifies His Praise. But you understand not their glorification. 
Truly, He is Ever Forbearing, Very Forgiving.

Everything in the world, all the creatures in existence, proclaim the infinite creativity of their Creator. That's the purpose of this ⟪ar:عالم⟫, to help us imaginatively comprehend the beauty of our creator. ⟪ar:عالم⟫is different from ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. The ⟪ar:عالم⟫comprises of everything in this world that constantly reminds the ⟪ar:عالِم⟫of the ⟪ar:علیم⟫. This ⟪ar:دنیا⟫comprises the lower pleasures of this world that cater to the ego. This is why ⟪ar:حمد⟫ all praise and gratitude is to the master who created the ⟪ar:عالم⟫, not the ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. ٰIt is:

⟪ar:الحمد للہ رب العالمین⟫ , not ⟪ar:الحمد للہ رب الدنیا⟫, you see?

So what the verse is telling us is that All of creation reveals for our intellects, the mystery of this universal praise, the Quran tells us:

Light [24:41]
[ سورۃ ۲۴] النور

‏أَلَمْ تَرَ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُسَبِّحُ لَهُۥ مَن فِى ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱلطَّيْرُ صَٓفَّتٍۢ ۖ
كُلٌّۭ قَدْ عَلِمَ صَلَاتَهُۥ وَتَسْبِيحَهُۥ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌۢ بِمَا يَفْعَلُونَ ‎۴۱
Do you not see that Allah is the one who is glorified by everything in the heavens and the earth, and the birds with wings out-spread. 
Of each one indeed knows its prayer and its glorification, and Allah is All-Aware of what they do.

This is why, it is not at all contradictory for Maulana Ali, to both, despise this world, and yet express its beauty in his descriptions. What he is despising is the ⟪ar:دنیا⟫, and what he is praising is the ⟪ar:عالم⟫that reminds him of the magnificent creativity of the ⟪ar:علیم⟫. In one of his sermons he talks about the unsurpassed marvel of the peacock. In the same sermon he talks about ants, serpents and elephants. He concludes that sermon with an exciting description of Paradise, comparing the marvels of this world with the delights of the next. Because when compared to paradise, all the pleasures and marvels of this world, pale into insignificance. In this sermon he says: For once the 'eye of the heart' is directed to paradise, your soul will become averse to the marvels of this world, its passions, its pleasures and its embellished scenery'.

So on one hand these scholars rejoice in their joy of certainty, and on the other hand they 'befriend' hardships. Now it may seem that these two notions contradict each other, but in reality the latter is the fruit of the former. The befriending of hardships comes as a result of their joy of certainty. You see? For these scholars, the spiritual resources made available by faith, combined with their intellectual assimilation, they are able to endure all difficulties without exception. Because faith when intellectually assimilated, turns into gnosis (⟪ar:معرفۃ⟫). This ⟪ar:معرفۃ⟫ comprises a certainty which is so deep, and so complete that 'even if all the veils laid over this world are lifted' it cannot increase their level of certainty. This is why we have that very famous saying of Maulana Ali:
لو کشف الغطاء ما ازدت یقینا

Even if all the veils [of this world] were lifted [from my eyes], it would not increase my certainty in the least bit. ⟪ar:اللہ اکبر⟫

# Testimony

In one of his sermon Maulana Ali describes what he means by 'sincerity' and 'purity of intention'. He says: 'I bear witness that there is no deity but Allah alone. He has no partner - this is a testimony whose sincerity has been tested, and whose essence has been assimilated with conviction. The words he uses in this sermon are ⟪ar:مُعتَقَداً مُصَعصَھَا⟫. The word ⟪ar:مُصَص⟫refers to the choicest and purest part of any substance. The essence of something is ْ⟪ar:مُصَص⟫. The root of the word ⟪ar:مُصی⟫means 'to suck'. The idea here being that the liquid or juice of a fruit contains its essence; it is something that is 'sucked' out of it. So what Maulana Ali is stressing here, is the spiritual substance; the spiritual essence of his testimony of ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫, as opposed to the mere verbal affirmation, or the mental comprehension of the words. By saying ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫, he affirms a heartfelt connection with the one for which his testimony bears witness. For Maulana Ali, the words defining the testimony echo its reality. He then continues to say: 'We always cling to this testimony  for as long as we endure, storing it up as protection against the terrors that will befall us. For truly, it renders faith resolute, opens up spiritual virtue, pleases ⟪ar:الرحمن⟫(the All-Compassionate), repels ⟪ar:الشیطان⟫(the Satan).'

So returning back to our discussion of his statement of '**befriending that by which the ignorant are estranged**', in the light of this understanding, what the statement implies, is not just the trials of this life but also the inescapability of death. For the wise, this encounter with Allah, for a blissful eternity, means a release from the prison of this world. In other words, death to them is a mode of ⟪ar:توحید⟫, whereby the soul enjoys ever greater degrees of integration within higher principles. On the other, hand for the ignorant, death is perceived as a frightful disintegration from ⟪ar:دنیا⟫. It means the end of their world, rather than the beginning of an endless life.

This goes back to that earlier concept of acting dead while still going through life, that was described as '⟪ar:فنیٰ⟫', 'extinction', 'effacement', the 'perishing' of egoistic consciousness in the face of the Absolute. The ignorant is horrified at the prospect of losing what they falsely believe to be their **life**. What they do not realize is that this sacrifice of their egoistic and limited self-consciousness, is the very basis on which infinite consciousness is tasted. One can only 'taste' the ocean of the Real, by drowning in it. That is what drowning is - giving up life by ingesting the ocean; by allowing one to be consumed by the ocean; becoming one with the ocean; integrating with the ocean. This is why the ignorant despise death. This is also the very reason why the acts of pure worship with total concentration on the Real, is 'embraced' by the wise and feared by the ignorant. Because to them, this  concentration forces them to leave behind all their worldly egotistic preoccupations. Worship forces them to exclude ⟪ar:دنیا⟫; because an intense concentration upon the Absolute, burns away thoughts of the ⟪ar:دنیا⟫, you see? And because the ignorant (the extravagant) strongly relates to, and identifies himself with the pleasures of this world stemming from the caprice and desires of his ego, the concept of death becomes a very painful event. For the wise, on the other hand, this concentration on the Absolute—this unsullied 'remembrance of Allah' is a joyous contemplation of reality before which the ego is completely displaced, and all illusions are entirely dispelled. Because in the brilliance of the ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫ , the dim lights of ⟪ar:دنیا⟫ fades away. This leads us directly to the next sentence of the passage:
وَصَحِبُوا الدُّنْيَا بِأَبْدَانٍ أَرْوَاحُهَا مُعَلَّقَةٌ بِالْـمَحَلِّ الاْعْلَى

With their bodies they keep company with this world, while their spirits are tied to the transcendent realm

This sentence can be better understood in the light of another well-known expression of Maulana Ali. He describes himself as belonging to a group whose degree of spirituality is such that 'their hearts are in the Gardens [of Paradise] while their bodies are at work'. The fact of being 'tied' or 'attached to that divine realm, consisting of the abodes and stations of paradise, leading right up to the divine presence of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) himself, implies that they are already 'within' that transcendent realm. They are already living in that spiritual realm, more than they are in this earthly one.

Maulana Ali (AS) says: Allah keeps man perpetually in that which his soul desires. In other words, these scholars are people whose consciousness is essentially - not just conceptually - but essentially determined by the transcendent realities. You see? Their consciousness in this world mirrors the realities of the spiritual realm. On the other hand, those whose consciousness is determined by worldly matters, are trapped 'within' this world - in the sense of being enclosed by its limitations. Their orientation and imagination are trapped within a worldly framework. Their actions bear the mark of  'worldliness'. So they have to do what is necessary to survive! Do unto others before they do unto you! They have to take justice in their own hands, because there is no concept of a judgment day in the future. So they're trapped by the limitations of their existence.

Whereas the actions of the wise in this world, are the fruits of their effacement in a higher reality. SubhanAllah! Then the author says something very beautiful. He says, the outward actions of these scholars in this world, disguise their inward transcendence of higher realities. So They ACT, but they are not bound by the fruits of their action. They do not expect results of their actions in this world. But then they go a step further, and they constantly judge their actions in the light of the divine acceptance, because as Maulana Ali (AS) stated earlier, they are aware that 'action - all action—is dust, except what is purified from within, and accomplished with sincerity for the sake of Allah". So their entire effort is not on the fruits of their labor, but rather on the purification and sincerity of their efforts.

# Hereafter

Then the author talks about the notion of the **hereafter** within the ethical framework of Maulana Ali's spiritual ethos. He gives a very beautiful and a unique perspective on ⟪ar:آخرۃ⟫. What is the concept of the hereafter? Is it a question of offering a heavenly carrot and threatening with a hellish stick, in order to simply 'enforce good and forbid the evil'? Absolutely not. There is a much deeper aspect, to this seemingly simplistic utilization of ethics in the form of divine warnings and promises for human action. The concept of hereafter is that it is a dynamic dimension. It is not just a mere belief or a hope for the future, rather it is an orientation within the very soul of the believer towards a destination. What the author is trying to explain is that the anticipation of the hereafter is one thing, but its realization is completely another. It is the realization (making real) that characterizes the soul of the ⟪ar:ولی اللہ⟫, the friend of Allah, who is the scholar, the saint or the sage.

Let me explain that a bit more. Think about what Maulana Ali (AS) said earlier:
لو کشف الغطاء ما ازدت یقینا

'Were the veils of this world removed from my eyes, it would not increase my certitude to smallest degree. Why? Because the anticipation, or foretaste of the hereafter, is deepened directly in proportion to one's virtue, piety and sanctity in this world. Piety and virtue are not just tools, that are useful in this world, and then become useless when one dies. If we look at this passage from the ⟪ar:نھج⟫in which Maulana Ali (AS) says: If you were to cast the eye of your heart towards what is described for you of paradise; your soul would become averse to the marvels of this world. It would become averse to its passions, its pleasures and its embellished scenery. Your soul would be mesmerized in the contemplation of those swaying trees whose roots lie hidden in dunes of musk on the banks of the rivers of gardens, and the clusters of dazzling pearls hanging down from the branches of its trees.' SubhanAllah! How beautifully he portrays this transcendent image in terms that we can grasp within our imagination.

Now it is true that the highest degree of spirituality transcends the desire for paradise and the fear of hellfire. This is why Maulana Ali (AS) said:
یا الھی ما عبدتک بشوق جنتک ولا من خوف نارک بل وجدۃ مستحقا لی العبادۃ فالعبدک

My master! I do not worship you for the desire of paradise, or out of the fear of your hellfire. Indeed I found you worthy of my worship, and for that reason alone I worship you.

So yes, pure sincerity does transcend the rewards of paradise and the fear of hellfire. However, once such high degrees of realization of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) have been attained, the state of the soul becomes something that can now be characterized as 'paradisal' - belonging to paradise. The soul reaches the state where it is already so utterly content with the beatific presence of Allah, that it is now impossible and inconceivable for it to desire anything else. This state of soul is referred to in the Quran as ⟪ar:نفس المطمئنۃ⟫.

The Dawn [89:27-29]
[ سورۃ ۸۹] الفجر

‏يَٓأَيَّتُهَا ٱلنَّفْسُ ٱلْمُطْمَئِنَّةُ ‎۲۷
:"O the one in rest and satisfaction!

‏ٱرْجِعِىٓ إِلَىٰ رَبِّكِ رَاضِيَةًۭ مَّرْضِيَّةًۭ ‎۲۸
"Come back to your Lord, Well-pleased and well-pleasing unto Him!

‏فَٱدْخُلِى فِى عِبَدِى ‎۲۹
"Enter you, then, among My honored slaves, 

The Quran also states that the 'Hereafter is better and everlasting'

The Most High [87:17]
[ سورۃ ۸۷] الاعلی

‏وَٱلْآخِرَةُ خَيْرٌۭ وَأَبْقَىٰٓ ‎۱۷

So the hereafter is not only the 'time' when divine warnings and promises will be consummated; it is also the 'space' within which all positive, angelic and divine qualities are manifested in their absolute pure nature. In other words, these divine qualities or virtues that we attain and practice during our lifetime are seen as reflections of those higher realities. Realities that encompass and penetrate the entire sphere of spiritual existence. In this vein, one such paradisal bliss in this realization of the hereafter itself. Allah says:

Sad [38:46]
[ سورۃ ۳۸] ص

‏إِنَّآ أَخْلَصْنَهُم بِخَالِصَةٍۢ ذِكْرَى ٱلدَّارِ ‎۴۶
Indeed, We did choose them by granting them the purest remembrance of their home.

In other words, the mere thought of paradise, is in itself a purification of the mind and heart. It is a means of repelling from the soul, the ever-present temptation to seek its ultimate happiness and well-being in just this world. It transforms ones actions in the light of the fact that
وَمَا تُقَدِّمُوا۟ لِأَنفُسِكُم مِّنْ خَيْرٍۢ تَجِدُوهُ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ هُوَ خَيْرًۭا وَأَعْظَمَ أَجْرًۭا

Whatever good you send before you for yourselves, you will certainly find it with Allah, better and greater in reward.

Therefore a sincere orientation towards the hereafter transforms ones worldly state into a paradisal state. He acquires these virtues and he practices ⟪ar:تقوی⟫ in this world, because these are the practices of ⟪ar:اھل الجنۃ⟫. The idea is, that if I plan to live among the people of paradise, I better adopt and practice their character traits in this world. So that when I join them, I feel right at home, you see?  That is how Maulana Ali (AS) viewed piety and virtue. Not for the reward of paradise or fear of hellfire, but rather to become like those he wishes to join. This is why he said: 'The fruit of good action is like its root'. What he means by that is that the end is already present in the means leading to it. Quran says:

The Cow [2:25]
[ سورۃ ۲] البقرۃ

‏وَبَشِّرِ ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ وَعَمِلُوا۟ ٱلصَّلِحَتِ أَنَّ لَهُمْ جَنَّتٍۢ تَجْرِى مِن تَحْتِهَا ٱلْأَنْهَرُ ۖ 
كُلَّمَا رُزِقُوا۟ مِنْهَا مِن ثَمَرَةٍۢ رِّزْقًۭا ۙ قَالُوا۟ هَذَا ٱلَّذِى رُزِقْنَا مِن قَبْلُ ۖ وَأُتُوا۟ بِهِۦ مُتَشَبِهًۭا ۖ 
وَلَهُمْ فِيهَآ أَزْوَجٌۭ مُّطَهَّرَةٌۭ ۖ وَهُمْ فِيهَا خَلِدُونَ ‎۲۵
And give good tidings to those who believe and do righteous deeds that they will have gardens [in Paradise] beneath which rivers flow. 
Whenever they are provided with a provision of fruit therefrom, they will say, "This is what we were provided with before." And it is given to them in likeness. 
And they will have therein purified spouses, and they will abide therein eternally.

These 'fruits' of جنۃ that Allah mentions in these verses, can be experienced right in this world in the form of virtue. It is experienced in the form of all goodness, beauty and truth. It is experienced in the diverse modes of happiness flowing from them. All such experiences of virtue are nothing but a foretaste of the ultimate blessings of Paradise. You want to know what the pleasure of جنۃ feels like? Perform a virtuous deed. Do something kind for someone, and see how that makes you feel. That feeling of ecstasy is nothing but a taste of what paradise will feel like. The more sincerely you perform that deed, the greater and sweeter you will be able to taste that happiness of ⟪ar:جنۃ⟫. SubhanAllah!

So what we've learnt here is that the performance of good deeds and the realization of intrinsic virtue, ⟪ar:احسان⟫, is not simply a prerequisite for future salvation, but it is already a kind of deliverance in the present. Hence the usage of the word ⟪ar:فلاح⟫. It is a deliverance from the imprisonment of sin on the outward plane, and it is a deliverance from the bondage of egocentricity on the inward plane. This theme of salvation, this concept of deliverance in the present, in the here and the now, grounded in an unshakable certitude, is fundamental to the spirit of all of Maulana Ali's teachings.

<!-- section 11 (id=202, raw_sort=11): Recap By Yasmin Palejwala -->

# Spirit Of Intellect

The soul or ⟪ar:الروح⟫is the energy that was created in the spiritual realm. This is what joins our body in the dormant state and becomes our soul in the form of consciousness. In this book, the author describes ⟪ar:الروح العقل⟫which looks at the spirit of the intellect, explaining the relationship between these two concepts.

There are three factors that need to be understood in order to understand the overarching concept of الروح العقل. The first is by understanding the distinction between intellect and reason. The second one is understanding that every spiritual action revolves around and therefore directly relates to the teachings of the Quran, which is the centrality of revelation. The third one is creating a harmony between the intellect and revelation. Once these three things are accomplished, ie. Understanding what intellect is, centering it around revelation and harmonizing it with the Qur’an and its teachings, this is the spirit of the intellect.

Firstly, reason and intellect are not the same thing. Reason is a tool used by the mind to think, understand and form judgement based on outward experience. It allows us to consider the positives and negatives of ideas and make decisions. However, intellect, using reason as a tool, is what allows us to experience the effects of these decisions or conclusions. The intellect is a unifying force, allowing us to experience the inner spiritual meanings of concepts rationalized by reason.

Next, the Quran provides the anchor for the intellect of a Muslim, such that the intellect is guided, supported and protected by its teachings. When looking for a perfect example of how this works, we look to Maulana Ali (AS) and the life that he led. Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) was in charge of the revelation, and Maulana Ali (AS) brought us it’s interpretation and inner meanings. Both endured hardships in their lives to enforce these principles so that we may understand them today. By reflecting on the spirituality of Maulana Ali (AS) we can understand how the centrality of revelation should manifest in our lives. Maulana Ali (AS) main motivation was his knowledge about the will of Allah, and this was shown in the practical example that was given in the original session.

When he answered the question from the bedouin about the oneness of Allah right before going into battle, we are able to understand that intellect superseded reason. Reasoning would have come to the outward conclusion that this was not the right time to be asking questions, but his intellect was used to see beyond this reason to realize that the توحید of Allah lies at the heart of everything, and there is never a wrong time. This is where Maulana Ali (AS) delivers his speech on the meaning of توحید and describes Allah as a ‘reality which has no otherness’, saying that even describing him as ‘one’ is incorrect, since this implies there could be a second in sequence. Instead, he should be described as unique He later describes the transcendence of Allah, how He exists outside of everything we know, but also how imminent (close) He is, as close as our jugular vein. We are able to recognise his existence and some attributes based on everything we see around us. Furthermore, everything around us provides a sign pointing back to its creator, which is the concept of تاویل.

From all of this, we understand that the second factor that generates the spirit of the intellect is this idea that every thought, motivation or action rationalized by the intellect should be centered around and anchored to revelation, in the same way that Maulana Ali (AS) centred his life around the Quran and the توحید of Allah.

# Discourse To KUMAYL

Following this, the author then looks at a conversation that was had between Maulana Ali (AS) and one of his most loyal companions, Kumail bin Ziyad, who is described as being humble, pious and someone who preserved the teachings of Maulana Ali (AS). This conversation begins with Kumail bin Ziyad asking Maulana Ali to tell him everything he knows about Allah, which leads to discourse demonstrating the ethos of the spirituality of Maulana Ali (AS) and the depths of his knowledge.

He starts by stating that knowledge is life- one is only truly alive and whole with knowledge. He then relates this to mankind by saying that there are 3 types of people- the scholar that holds the true knowledge, the student that is seeking the true knowledge, and the rest of the people, who are akin to moths, following and being influenced by every flame without seeking the true direction. The ideal place to be is in the first two categories.

He then goes on to compare knowledge to wealth, showing that wealth is the key to success in this world, just like knowledge is the key to success in the afterlife. Despite this, if there is a choice to be made between the two, since both require time and effort to obtain, you should choose the knowledge. This is because your knowledge guards you, but you have to guard your wealth. Wealth decreases when you share it, but knowledge increases when you do the same. Nobody can take our knowledge away, but it can always be increased.

He then distinguished between superficial and true knowledge, which he refers to as the reality of insight. This is demonstrated when he points to his chest, claiming that’s where the ocean of knowledge was. The chest represents the superficial knowledge center, whereas the heart lying hidden beneath is the real center.

He then describes, using the word ‘⟪ar:حجمۃ⟫’ which means assault, how in the same way as an army fights to reach the king at the center of a stronghold, one should conduct an assault on the exterior world, getting through the layers of superficial knowledge in order to reach the true reality- knowledge should penetrate the heart in the same way. This implies that there is a strive and an effort that must be undertaken to get there, ‘⟪ar:تحقیق⟫’ which translates to investigation. Investigation is the assault on words, leading to the depths of meaning. When one sees past the outer layers of this world and reaches the true depths of reality, a shift in consciousness takes place, and through learning and assimilation of these theories, actual practices manifest.

This true knowledge needs to be protected- It needs to fill the hearts of those who seek knowledge in the proper way, and who will use it positively in the ways that please Allah.
