For those whose hearts aren’t in a state to accept this knowledge, it could be used for personal gains and to hurt the friends of Allah.

Going back to the three categories of people, he describes our Imams as being the ever-present lordly knowers. Through their lineage, the proof of Allah and His religion will never become extinct. Despite being small in number, these hold the greatest rank and station with Allah.

Now when talking about Allah’s religion, it is important to understand that despite the formal institutions of religion changing from the time of Adam til now, the essence has always remained the same- the salvation of our souls through submission to Allah. This is achieved through acquiring knowledge, reiterating that one must penetrate the outer surface and understand the inner essential meanings of reality. An example of this being knowing how to pray and why we do it, compared to understanding the deeper meaning behind every action.

From this discourse, and the deeper look at spiritual knowledge, it should be understood that the only way to truly understand spirituality is through striving to understand the inner meanings of the outward realities we face, and work to assimilate this knowledge into our lives as everyday practice, orientating our souls towards those inner realities.

<!-- category 28: Justice -->

<!-- section 12 (id=162, raw_sort=12): Concept Of Justice -->

# Emulating The Divine Nature

If you look up the definition of the word justice in a dictionary, you'll find words like "fairness", "equality", "fair play". Generally for people, the word justice means to behave in a way that is fair, equal and balanced for everyone. This is the kind of description you'll find in a dictionary, which sounds more like a theoretical speculation on the meaning of justice. But the intent of the author in this book, as I understand, is to align the concept of justice, with the reality of the sacred One - ⟪ar:القدس⟫. He says that on one hand, the principle of justice possess a sacred spiritual dimension; and on the other, it contains an appreciation for the fact, that the roots of justice exist within the **divine nature** itself. Why is that important to appreciate? Because understanding the fact, that justice is the very essence of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala)'s nature, creates a profound awareness for the necessity of justice within our own soul and within the world itself.

Now what's interesting is that the author emphasizes, that justice **is** the very nature of the divine. He does not say, justice is **in**the very nature of divine; he says it **is** his nature, it **is** his essence. You see the difference? If justice was **in** his nature, then it would just be another attribute among his various attributes. But by emphasizing that justice **is** his very essence, he's pointing out that all other attributes and qualities, "essentially" stem from his justice. So that adds a completely different dimension to all his other attributes. Understanding that Justice **is** the essence of his nature makes us appreciate that his anger not only stems from his justice, his mercy is also an outcome of his justice. His love is out of justice, his wrath is out of justice. His construction is out of justice; and his destruction is out of justice. Based on this, what we're learning here, is that the spiritual perceptions of Islamic faith has to possess an ethical orientation towards justice.

To make this easy, imagine the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫having a body and a spirit; just as we all have a body and a spirit. It's body - are the various actions (prayer, fasting, paying ⟪ar:زکوۃ⟫ etc.), but the spirit of those actions are based on what? They're based on the nature of the divine - which is justice! Therefore adherence to the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫is actually a training in becoming like the divine through the principles of justice - you understand?

It's a very interesting idea. Just as we adhere to the disciplines of the physical actions of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫, we must also make a commitment to their underlying principles. Principles that surpass, and go beyond the realm of action. Principles within which, the rules and precepts of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫operate. In other words, our **actions must be complemented by contemplation on the divine**. Creating this harmony between the two principles of contemplation and action; theory and practice; idealism and reality; being and doing — it will lead us to the very heart of the Islamic message of ⟪ar:توحید⟫- which is integrating oneness. That's what ⟪ar:توحید⟫means - to integrate, to make one - to **become**one. When we make our theory and practice "one" - that's when we will become a true ⟪ar:موحد⟫.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/162/6947a78f-d7ed-429f-8afe-4c776ca19434.jpg}}

Think about the various theories out there today:
- The theory of **relativism**- that knowledge, truth, and morality are not absolute, but rather they are relative. They exist in relation to culture, society, or a historical context.
- Or the theory of **nihilism** - which believes in the rejection of all religious and moral principles - the belief that life is essentially meaningless.
- Or the Utilitarian theory - that actions are only right if they are useful or for the benefit of a majority. In other words, there is no fundamental underlying principles. Good or bad is based, simply on the result of greater benefit or loss - good is based on utility of things
- Or **opportunism** - benefitting from opportunities, as and when they arise, regardless of planning or principle. This is where you have your manipulative types. People who will try to produce an outcome through flattery, or show off, or by pretending to say or do things just in order to get what they want.

These are some of the dominant - human created - theories out there today. How did this world arrive at these theories? They arrived by completely divorcing practice with principles. Now there might be some value that comes out of these theories, but the problem is that none of these values are fundamentally rooted in the divine. So even if they mirror the Islamic values by some coincidence, they are essentially baseless. They have no spirit in them. They are dead corpses.

So what the author is arguing is that if we take any value or virtue - let's say the virtue of "truth". So if we choose to always speak the truth, without realizing the underlying **reason**for speaking "truth"- which is that "truth" is the very nature of our creator; and our goal in this world is to adopt the virtues of our creator; without that realization, speaking the truth will ultimately become situational and self-serving. It will become something that will be open to compromise when the situation warrants it, you see? I'll speak the truth when it benefits me, and I'll lie when it benefits me. But now, if truth is viewed as the nature of the divine, it becomes everlasting; it becomes eternal. The situation or the consequences of speaking the truth become irrelevant - they no longer matter. I speak the truth, because I am emulating the nature of my creator - which is ⟪ar:الحق⟫- the absolute truth. So now the virtue of truth becomes immortal, just as Allah is immortal. You understand? It eliminates the need to practice virtue on an *ad hoc*basis; because virtue practiced on an ad hoc basis is not really a virtue at all. It simply becomes a series of pragmatic adaptations to changing circumstances.

This is why when we study the life of Maulana Ali (AS), we find his life reflecting this unflinching adherence to divine virtues. His was a life that was not governed by his ⟪ar:نفس⟫. His was a life, that was entirely governed by the divine nature of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). All his incredible actions that we read and marvel over, they basically emerge as the fruit of his irresistible attraction towards the nature of ⟪ar:الحق⟫. Because his existence was perfectly oriented towards Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). His focus was never on the action itself; rather it was on the source of the principles generating that action. So what happens is, that when we focus on the beauty of the divine nature, in trying to adopt that beautiful nature, good actions naturally and inevitably emerge from us. It's that simple.

# Morality vs Ethics

See... when we generally talk about justice, we get stuck in the debates of morality and ethics. First of all, what is the difference between morality and ethics? Ethics is concerned with the concept of what is good. It deals with the soul and the spirit of "good"; the ⟪ar:باطن⟫. Morals, pertain to the rules and principles governing what is right and wrong. In other words, the discourse of ethics gives birth to morals. Ethics provides the wide framework of spiritual virtue - which is termed as ⟪ar:احسان⟫.

This is why we have 3 components of spirituality. We have ⟪ar:اسلام⟫; which provides the outer aspects of morality. Then there is ⟪ar:ایمان⟫which is the framework of ethics; and then, there is the union of the two (the combination of ⟪ar:اسلام⟫and ⟪ar:ایمان⟫), serving as their underlying principle, leading to ⟪ar:احسان⟫- which is the "good life", or the "beautiful life".

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/162/4cb43610-e5d1-4289-b08e-aaf529790ae0.jpg}}

So keep that distinction between morality and ethics

# Allah has no opposites

So now how does justice relate to ⟪ar:توحید⟫?  In Maulana Ali's ethos, we see that Allah is the ultimate reality that **has no second**. This means that he also has no moral 'opposite'. This means that the ⟪ar:خیر⟫(the goodness or the beauty) that comes from Allah, it does not have the moral opposite of evil. You understand?

This is important to understand. Because generally speaking, we tend to view this world in terms of good vs evil - Allah vs ⟪ar:شیطان⟫. We think in terms of opposites. Maulana Ali (AS) teaches us that Allah does not have any opposites.  This means that ⟪ar:شیطان⟫or ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫is not the opposite of Allah. He is not standing against Allah, because no one can stand against Allah. No one has the capability or the capacity to stand against Allah. ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫is standing against the **creation**of Allah. His only goal; his only capability, is to drive people away from Allah, by corrupting their inherent divine nature. How does he do that? By beautifying the opposite of Allah's nature. Allah's nature is modest, he beautifies arrogance. Allah's nature is decent, he beautifies lewdness and nakedness. Allah's nature is humble, he beautifies show off and boastfulness. That's essentially what ⟪ar:شیطان⟫is doing. His efforts are geared towards corrupting the divine attributes.

Allah has no opposite. The only opposite of what we perceive as ⟪ar:الحق⟫- the absolutely Reality, is unreality or nothingness. So everything that is "good" and "pure" is one with the Absolute; whereas everything that is evil, is born from the negation of his divine reality. You understand? The essence of all ⟪ar:خیر⟫ - (all goodness, everything beautiful) is identified with the nature of the Creator; whereas all of "evil" is the corrupted state of creation - corrupted by a deviation from Allah's divine nature. This deviation is strongly implied in the verse in ⟪ar:سورۃ الفلق⟫ which commands us to seek refuge in Allah from 'the evil of **that**which was created'

The Daybreak [113:1-2]
[ سورۃ ۱۱۳] الفلق

‏قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ ٱلْفَلَقِ ‎۱‏مِن شَرِّ مَا خَلَقَ ‎۲
( 1 ) Say: "I seek refuge with the Lord of the daybreak, ( 2 ) "From the evil of what He has created; 

This verse ⟪ar:مِن شَرِّ مَا خَلَقَ⟫ is often mistranslated as - from the evil which "He" created', which is wrong. It is wrong from a theologically perspective; as well as from a lexical perspective. From a lexical perspective, the statement ⟪ar:مِن شَرِّ مَا خَلَقَ⟫  refers clearly to the evil stemming from **creation**, and not as an act of the **creator**. From a theological perspective, Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) created morally free agents,  who then, through the employment of their own free will, became authors of evil in creation. In other words, when creation **chose**to use their free will to negate the divine nature of their creator - evil was born. So what the author is trying to get at, is that  good, or goodness, or beauty - lies in the essence of the creator, whereas evil is just a temporary state of deviation from that divine and pure nature. It is simply an illusion that is witnessed by the "eye of the heart".

# Justice Defined

So now with this understanding, let's understand justice as defined by the author. Once a person subscribes to this concept of "good" being at the very essence of his creator, then depending on his conviction and his accountability towards this good, he first tries to enforce it upon himself; and after having succeeded in conquering the self, he expands that notion, and applies it at the societal level. Why? Because once a person truly understands that everything good is 'real'; and all is an illusion, there is a natural inclination and motivation to restore what is real, by destroying the illusions surrounding it.

This is why in Maulana Ali's(AS) perspective, the notion of what is '**right**' and what is '**rightfully due**' are comprised within the meaning of the word ⟪ar:حق⟫. Two things - what is right; and what is rightfully due. Understanding what is right leads to one's commitment towards fulfilling one's obligations to himself. What is rightfully due is the restoration of reality by the elimination of the illusive evil. Both of these are infused with reality - that is justice.

Achieving the beautiful divine nature of the creator is the right of every single soul. It is the right of every soul to be ⟪ar:خلیفۃ اللہ⟫ - the true caliph, the true representative of Allah. It is the right of every soul to expand, and grow into the image of his creator. ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫is robbing us of that right, by creating these deviations, and distancing us from Allah's nature. His goal is to keep us small, and reduce us to our base instincts - to operate at the lowest levels of animals - through food, sex and base pleasures. You see?

In Maulana Ali's ethos, **justice**is the effort to provide what is due to one's soul and then to others.

That's where we get the concept and ideologies of justice within Islam. If you truly understand this concept, you can now appreciate all the efforts of Maulana Ali behind the civil wars. You can truly appreciate, why Imam Hussain laid down his own life, and the lives of his family members for the cause of justice. It was not because he was being arrogant, or silly - but in fact, it was an action purely based on justice. Because ⟪ar:یزید⟫ was attacking the foundation of Islam. He was the ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫ trying to rob all future generations of their divine right to transform themselves into the image of their creator. Therefore standing up against him was the only intellectual, logical, moral, ethical, and **just**option. The same applies to the efforts of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam), and every Imam and every ⟪ar:داعی⟫after him.

So Maulana Ali (AS)'s definition of justice is to give what is rightfully due to each and every soul, beginning with the self and then expanding it to the society. Hence we get his famous definition of justice:
وَضَعَ الشَّئُی حَقَّ مَحَلِّہِ

Justice is to put everything in its right place. One is 'just' only as much as one gives everything its proper due. The more one is attuned and aligned with the nature of ⟪ar:الحق⟫, the more he is able to carry out this justice with everything in existence, in a universal manner.

---

Our take away from today's session is that we have been created in God's image; and one of the purpose of that is to align ourselves with the divine nature - which is justice. Virtue is the right of each and every soul. Any virtue we adopt, has to be grounded in the realization of the divine. Adherence to⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫is the tool that allows us to achieve that divine purpose. ⟪ar:شیطان⟫ is not standing in opposition to Allah. No one can stand in opposition to him. Allah is a reality, whose only true opposite would be unreality or nothingness. The goal of ⟪ar:شیطان⟫ is to corrupt and distract our divine qualities by beautifying vices.

In the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali, justice is defined as putting things in their right place; or giving everything and everyone what is rightfully due to them. It begins at a personal level, expands to a societal level. Through the combination of the two, we are able to give Allah is due. The more we are aligned with Allah's nature, the more we will be able to carry out justice with our own selves and  everything else in existence.

<!-- section 13 (id=169, raw_sort=13): Beauty Of Justice -->

# 3 Dimensions of Soul

In the previous session we learnt that the essence of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) is justice, and all his other attributes stem from his justice. Justice, as defined by Maulana Ali is:
وَضَعَ الشَّئُی حَقَّ مَحَلِّہِ

Justice is to put everything in its proper place. In other words, one is 'just' only as much as one gives everything its proper due. The more one is attuned and aligned with the nature of ⟪ar:الحق⟫, the more he is able to carry out this justice with everything in existence, in a universal manner. Therefore within this discourse, justice is not seen as a virtue among several virtues; rather it is seen as the **perfection**or the sum of all the other virtues.

Plato defined justice as the sum of three essential virtues. He said justice is the sum of **temperance, courage and wisdom**.

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/162/ca978fe4-40cc-4b12-b8c6-e797eae916f6.jpg}}

Now why these three particular traits? Because they characterize, the three dimensions of the human soul. We have the concupiscent soul, the irascible soul and the rational soul. It is the one soul, but these are the three aspects, or dimensions of the soul, because everything is designed on the pattern of length, breadth and depth.

## The concupiscent soul ⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ بالسوء⟫

The first dimension is that of the concupiscent soul, which is the ⟪ar:النَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ⟫; or the lustful soul. This is the aspect of the soul that deals with appetites of the stomach and the genitals -⟪ar:قوۃ الشھوانیۃ⟫. This is the soul that has to do with desires.

Allah says in the Quran: ⟪ar:إِنَّ النَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ⟫ - Indeed the soul overwhelmingly commands a person to evil, or to sin. The word ⟪ar:امارۃ⟫comes from the root word ⟪ar:امر⟫which means a command. The king is called the ⟪ar:امیر⟫because he has the power to command. So this soul can have power over us; it can rule us. And we've all experienced this, when a desire, or a lust, or an appetite takes over us - and despite knowing perfectly that what we're doing is wrong or forbidden - we do it anyway. Why? Because this aspect of the soul dominates us. This is why it's called⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ⟫. The commanding soul.

So now the question is, if it has command over us, and if it dominates us, leading us to sin, then why are we held responsible for our actions? Because clearly this soul has a type of sovereignty over us, right?

This is where we need to understand this concept of ⟪ar:امر⟫. What ⟪ar:لنَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ⟫truly means, is that this aspect of our soul has sovereignty over us, only if **we** give it the power of ⟪ar:امر⟫. You understand? if we allow it to rule over us; if we allow it to subjugate us; if we subordinate ourselves to it; if we choose to listen and follow all of its dictates and commands, only **then** it can dominate us; and only then it can command us.

Now what's interesting is that in today's day and age, this aspect of the ⟪ar:نفس⟫is actually glorified - because people say ”you should do whatever makes you happy! Do whatever pleases you! Do whatever your heart desires”. YOLO (You Only Live Once), right? This YOLO concept is what has empowered this aspect at a broader societal level in these times.

## The irascible Soul ⟪ar:نفس الَّوَّامۃ⟫

The next dimension is the irascible soul or ⟪ar:نفس الوامۃ⟫. Irascible comes from "Ira", which is the Latin word for anger. We still use the word irate. If somebody is irate, it means they get very angry. So this is the faculty of anger - ⟪ar:قوۃ الغضبیۃ⟫; the faculty of wrath. Allah says in the Quran:
لَآ أُقْسِمُ بِيَوْمِ ٱلْقِيَمَةِ ‎ ‏وَلَآ أُقْسِمُ بِٱلنَّفْسِ ٱللَّوَّامَةِ

Indeed I swear by the day of judgment and I indeed I swear by the incriminating self. The word ⟪ar:لوم⟫ means to self incriminate, to self reproach, to blame oneself. What has blame got to do with anger? After we obey the command of the previous ⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ بالسوء⟫and we sin, this ⟪ar:نفس⟫self incriminates. It gets angry with itself, and experiences guilt. Guilt is actually one of the greatest blessings of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). The idea behind it, is that it should increase so much, that the person leaves those sins, and breaks the cycle. This is why it is directly tied to the day of judgment in the verse.
لَآ أُقْسِمُ بِيَوْمِ ٱلْقِيَمَةِ ‎ ‏وَلَآ أُقْسِمُ بِٱلنَّفْسِ ٱللَّوَّامَةِ

Indeed I swear by the day of judgment and I indeed I swear by the incriminating self. Why does this aspect of the soul incriminate? Because it knows that it has to stand before its master on the day of judgment, which could also be the day of wrath. So the blessing here is the capability of the نفس to get angry with itself, before the day when Allah becomes angry with it. This anger resulting in guilt is in the "seen" realm, is directly tied to the day of judgment which will take place in the unseen realm.

## The rational soul ⟪ar:نفس المطمئنۃ⟫

Finally there is the content soul - the ⟪ar:نفس المطمئنۃ⟫. Allah says to this righteous soul in the Quran:
يَا أَيَّتُهَا النَّفْسُ الْمُطْمَئِنَّةُ ارْجِعِي إِلَى رَبِّكِ رَاضِيَةً مَرْضِيَّةً

O the reassured, or content, or satisfied soul, return to your master in a state where you are well pleased with him, and he is well pleased with you.

So this is the satisfied or the content soul. What is it content with? It is content with the decree of its creator. That's the first reason it is content. The second reason is that it is no longer subject to the commands of evil. Rather it has completely taken control over the other two lower aspects of the self and subjected them to the command of their master. This is the soul that has molded; that has trained and disciplined itself in such a way, that the only thing that makes it happy; the only thing  that gives it solace are the things that its creator is pleased with.

The word ⟪ar:الْمُطْمَئِنَّةُ⟫ comes from the root word ⟪ar:طمن⟫which means tranquility or the state of serenity. So ⟪ar:النَّفْسُ الْمُطْمَئِنَّةُ⟫ is the serene soul, the content soul, the tranquil soul - that is completely at peace. This is why it rhymes with the word ⟪ar:امن⟫which literally means peace. You have ⟪ar:امن⟫and you have ⟪ar:طمن⟫, right? The word ⟪ar:امن⟫means peace, and the word means ⟪ar:طمن⟫means the state of tranquility and serenity, that is achieved when there is peace. Now what is it at peace from? It is at peace from committing sin; it is at peace from the subjugation, the tyranny, and the oppression of the other two souls. It is at peace because it has reached a level, where not only does it not sin, it no longer has the desire to sin; and when there is no sin, there is no self-incrimination; and so it is now in this state of blissful serenity. This is the soul that has perfected itself in the image of its creator.

Now the idea here is not to eliminate the previous two faculties of ⟪ar:غضب⟫and ⟪ar:شھوۃ⟫. The idea is not to eliminate desire and lust. The idea is to balance them out - which is the function of this third rational soul.

So the author says, it is necessary for these matters of ⟪ar:غضب⟫and ⟪ar:شھوۃ⟫to be balanced and moderated. If the concupiscent soul becomes subdued, if there is no desire, we will fall into this state called ⟪ar:رُخص⟫ which means leniency. We would simply stop working on ourselves completely. On the other hand, if the irascible nature, the faculty of wrath increases, we would incline towards unruliness. We would become ungovernable. We'd become arrogant and defiant. But if these two are balanced by the guidance of the rational soul, then they both will help us follow the path of guidance.

These two lower aspects can be great companions on our journey, if they are under the control of the rational mind. Otherwise, they are tyrants. Anger, if it increases and turns into wrath, it becomes very easy to strike or to kill. That is the extreme stage of the sin of anger because anger is actually forbidden. The Prophet (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said "Do not get angry!" Does this mean literally do not ever get angry? Because if it means that, then we're all doomed - even the best of us. No! That's not what it means. ⟪ar:علما⟫ said it means, Do not allow your anger to cause you to lose control. Today we see that in a lot of people. They easily lose control over their temper - people with severe anger issues. Then we have ⟪ar:قوۃ الشھوانیۃ⟫. If this one goes out of control, there's irresponsible consumption and hoarding.

Because these are like the two animal - Anger is like the dog; when crossed, it'll either bark or it'll bite. Lust or appetites are like the pig - Greed!. Have you ever seen a pig eat? It'll eat non-stop, and it does not discriminate about what it eats. That's me on amazon! Just keep getting stuff whether I need it or not; and I'm sure we all can relate to this in some way or another. Some people are emotional pigs. That lust for likes on Facebook just never gets satisfied.

So half of us are filled with rage and this desire for vengeance, and then the rest are filled with greed and appetites - concupiscent appetites. Spending too much money, wasting money on unnecessary things. It's a sickness! But this is because we are trapped in our lower selves. We have not risen above these two souls. And we are going to destroy this world because of our rage, and our lust.

Look what happened to George Floyd. Look what happened in these retaliations - In these "Black lives matter" protests. People lose control. They riot, they steal, they hurt - they turn into animals. It's because this ⟪ar:قوۃ الغضبیۃ⟫ and the ⟪ar:قوۃ الشھوانیۃ⟫completely takes over. They become extremely emotional. They lash out verbally; they lash out physically. They use whatever excuse they can find to tear everything down. So one group is doing this, the other are consuming everything out of their appetites. Perfect example is the hoarding that took place because of this COVID. So the concupiscent soul will burn this world out of anger; and the irascible soul will consume its resources until this is nothing left to consume.

So what is required to deal with this Irascible soul, or this angry soul - is "**temperance**"; which is emotional restraint, or self control - to control ones temper. Similarly what is required to deal with the desirous concupiscent soul, is to fight and battle temptations which requires "**courage**"; and both require "wisdom" that comes from the rational soul. What is wisdom? The faculty to control and apply knowledge as and when required, in exactly the right amounts as warranted by the situation. Wisdom manages temperance and courage.

This is the soul that can witness essences. This is the soul that can see first principles; that can acknowledges reality. This is the soul that recognizes beauty and harmony. That recognizes that behind all of this there is something vast  that we cannot comprehend. We can only comprehend it in our incomprehensibility of it - and that is our Lord! our master! This is true rationality. True rationality is to recognize our creator. It is irrational to believe that all of this popped into existence out of nothing.

Now when all three dimensions of the soul, with their respective virtues are in their proper place - when irascibility is under control through temperance; when concupiscence is tamed with courage, and when wisdom of the rational soul is governing both - that's when the soul can be qualified as **just**. This is why Plato defined justice as the sum of these three essential virtues. The sum of temperance, courage and wisdom.

# Tradition Of Gabriel

Now how is this justice achieved? What is the key to unlock the power of this justice? It is through **remembrance**; through ⟪ar:ذکر⟫. What is ⟪ar:ذکر⟫? It is a recollection of the ultimate reality; a recollection of the immutable principles; unchanging qualities at their various levels of application. Remembrance is not a question of learning new things from scratch. It is about remembering the divine principles and attributes that are **inherent**in the very substance of our intellects; and by which everything can be understood. These are principles that are ultimately rooted in the divine nature of our creator. This type of remembrance is regarded as the cognitive and philosophical foundation of ethical thought in Islam. This is why you can find remembrance at its best, in the Sufi traditions; which are also referred to as the tradition of ⟪ar:احسان⟫. Maulana Ali was the greatest Sufi. He was the leader of all Sufi's who perfectly demonstrated the tradition of ⟪ar:احسان⟫.

## Tradition of Gabriel

We know that this tradition of ⟪ar:احسان⟫ was highlighted to the prophet by the angel ⟪ar:جبرئیل⟫himself. We learn that through the famous HADEES of ⟪ar:جبرئیل⟫. It is narrated that the angel ⟪ar:جبرئیل⟫ approached the prophet in human form, when he was sitting with his companions, and said:
يَا مُحَمَّدُ، أخْبِرْنِي عَنْ الإسْلاَمِ

O Muhammad, tell me about submission (⟪ar:الاسلام⟫). The Prophet replied
الإِسْلاَمُ أَنْ تَشْهَدَ أَنْ لاَ إِلهَ إِلاَ اللهُ وَأَنَّ مُحَمَّداً رَسُولُ اللهِ، وَتُقِيمَ الصَّلاَةَ، وَتُؤْتِيَ الزَّكَاةَ، وَتَصُومَ رَمَضَانَ، وَتَحُجَّ الْبَيتَ إِن اسْتَطَعْتَ إِلَيْهِ سَبِيلاً

Islam is to testify that there is no god but Allah and Muhammad is his messenger. To perform the prayers, to pay the poor their due (⟪ar:زکوۃ⟫) and to fast in Ramadan, and to make the pilgrimage to the House if you are able to do so.

Gabriel said: ⟪ar:صَدَقْتَ⟫You have spoken truly! The companions, who just saw a man and didn't know he was an angel, they were amazed at his questioning the prophet and then saying that he had spoken truly.

Gabriel then asked:  ⟪ar:فَأَخْبِرْنِي عَنِ الإِيْمَانِ⟫- Tell me about faith (⟪ar:الایمان⟫).' The Prophet said,
 أَنْ تُؤْمِنَ بِاللهِ، وَمَلاَئِكَتِهِ، وَكُتُبِهِ، وَرُسُلِهِ، وَالْيَوْمِ الآخِرِ، وَتُؤْمِنَ بِالْقَدَرِ خَيْرِهِ وَشَرِّهِ

True Faith, or ⟪ar:ایمان⟫is to believe in Allah, his angels, his books, his messengers, and the Last Day, and to believe in the divine destiny, both the good and the evil. Again he said ⟪ar:صَدَقْتَ⟫, You have spoken truly.

Then he asked: ⟪ar:فَأَخْبِرْنِي عَنِ الإِحْسَانِ⟫ Tell me about virtue (⟪ar:احسان⟫). To that, Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) beautiful replied:
 أَنْ تَعْبُدَ اللهَ كَأَنَّكَ تَرَاهُ، فَإِنْ لَمْ تَكُنْ تَرَاهُ فَإِنَّهُ يَرَاكَ

It is to worship Allah as though you see Him, and if you cannot do that, then at least worship him as through he sees you. But you can't do this unless you have faith and submission, right? Just as you can't have depth, until you have length and width.

# Sufism

So this word ⟪ar:احسان⟫was literally presented to the prophet through this angelic tradition. Now what does this word ⟪ar:احسان⟫mean? The word ⟪ar:احسان⟫literally means 'doing what is beautiful'. It comes from the root word ⟪ar:حَسَنَ⟫ which means beauty. In Islamic ethical discourse, virtue and vice are often referred to  as ⟪ar:حَسَنَ⟫ and ⟪ar:قبح⟫ literally meaning beauty and ugliness. Ultimately, it is the vision of the divine beauty that inspires virtue and makes the soul beautiful; and it is the departure from the divine qualities that makes the soul ugly or ⟪ar:قبیح⟫. This is why, the overwhelming majority of the famous artists in the Islamic world—calligraphers, painters, poets, musicians, they have all been practicing Sufi's.

Now if you look at the Prophet's definition of ⟪ar:احسان⟫as '**worshiping Allah as if one could see Him**', you can recognize the crucial relationship between worship and virtue; spirituality and ethics. You can't have one without the other. Similarly the relationship between devotion to the Creator, and kindness towards his creation. You can't have one without the other. Understanding this relationship takes us to the very heart of the spiritual traditions in Islam. Conversely, it is the denial and subversion of this tradition that is ultimately responsible for the atrocities committed by Muslims today in the name of Islam. This insane idea of hurting creation to please the creator - through terrorism. And this is not a societal problem, this is also a personal problem. There are parents who easily fall in this category of terrorists. Who just terrorize their children with guilt, and Allah's punishments till they either rebel or become blind followers of religion. How can you engender the love of Allah in the hearts of children, but constantly presenting him as this monster who is just dying to burn his creation in hell. It's so insane! And yet, it happens so much!

This is why the traditions of Sufism are so amazing, because they essentially brings the ethical theories and practices together. They are rooted in a deep transformative awareness of God. This principle to behave and act as if you can see God, means to conduct oneself or generate the consciousness of being constantly in the divine presence; for even if we cannot see him, he is seeing us. This imperative of ⟪ar:احسان⟫lies at the very root of a life of virtue in the spiritual universe of Islam.

This is why when you read the sermons of Maulana Ali (AS) in ⟪ar:نھج البلاغۃ⟫, you'll see that he repeatedly draws the reader back to the 'vision' of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). He sets that vision as the true basis of a life of virtue. He also repeatedly warns us of the dangers of the opposite - the engrossing vision of the "self" which is the source of all vice.

So on one hand he tells us, 'Understand that your every action is dependent upon your prayer', In other words, your every action depends upon the way you gaze upon Allah, which is the source of all good. On the other hand, he warns us to beware of vanity, conceit and lavish praise of oneself by others - because these are ⟪ar:شیطان⟫'s most reliable opportunities to destroy the virtue of the virtuous (⟪ar:المُحسنین⟫).

In these statements, he contrasts and compares the true, objective vision of ⟪ar:الحق⟫, the Real, realized through effacement of oneself in prayer, with the distorted, subjective vision of the ego, produced by inflation of the self through pride. Because that is essentially what we are doing in prayer. We are effacing our ⟪ar:نفس⟫, our ego, through our prostration (our ⟪ar:سجدۃ⟫). Whereas what ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫wants, is for us to inflate our egos to praise, and vanity.

The first (submission to Allah through effacement in prayer) leads to justice. It puts all things in their proper place, including, and especially the ego. The second leads to all the injustices that stem from self-aggrandizement, including fanaticism and bigotry; because ⟪ar:شیطان⟫is the epitome of pride and disobedience. He is described as 'the leader of the fanatics and the forerunner of the proud (⟪ar:امام المتعصبین و سلف المستکبرین⟫).

Bigotry is an intolerance toward those who hold different opinions from oneself. Fanatical bigotry is actually an intense form of injustice; because it creates prejudice which tarnishes the very requirements of justice - which to stay objective against the discrimination and biases of the self - whether that self is defined in terms of an individual, or in terms of family, clan, tribe, nation or religion.

<!-- section 14 (id=203, raw_sort=14): Letter To MALIK -->

# Who Was MALIK?

Today's topic  is based on a letter that Maulana Ali (AS) wrote to ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫appointing him as the governor of Egypt. What's amazing is that even though a century has passed, this letter still servers as a source of inspiration for setting up an ideal constitution for Islamic governance.  The advice Maulana Ali imparts to ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫, is universally applicable - not just rulers, but also for the people being ruled (the subjects). What'll you find at the heart of it all is justice.

This letter opens up so many different dimensions of contemplation for us. It teaches rulers should behave with their subjects, and teaches us how to behave as subjects under the rule of the government; whether that government is that of a country, a state, a community, or even the self - because don't forget, our soul is the ruler of our body; and our bodies are subject to its rule. So this letter will InshaAllah open many doors of contemplation for us.

But first I'd like to begin by talking a little bit about ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫.  Who was he? ⟪ar:مالک ابن حارث الاشتر⟫was a long-standing, dedicated follower of Maulana Ali (AS), and was referred to as one of his "right-hand" men. We know ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫ was the main antagonist and nemesis of Maulana Ali (AS) in the civil wars of ⟪ar:جمل⟫ and ⟪ar:سفین⟫ that pretty much dominated his caliphate. So when Maulana Ali appointed ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫as the governor of Egypt, ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫  had him poisoned before he could take charge. Also the way he had it done was extremely treacherous.

On the way to Egypt, ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫ decided to stop at ⟪ar:القلزوم⟫. Upon his arrival, ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫ man invited Malik to lunch at his house in order to celebrate his new governorship of Egypt. Malik humbly accepted the invitation without knowing that he was ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫ man. The man placed poisoned honey in a cup on the table. Malik took a spoonful of that poisoned honey. Immediately the poison spread throughout his body. Malik realized that he had been poisoned as soon as he felt the sharp pain in his stomach. Upon realizing that he placed his hand on his stomach and said

⟪ar:بسم اللہ الرحمن الرحیم۔ انا للہ و انا الیہ راجعون⟫

In the Name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. We belong to Allah, and we shall all return to Him. That poison was so toxic that within moments after that⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫died.

When ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫ heard the news that his mission had succeeded and ⟪ar:مالک⟫had been  killed before taking up his post as governor in Egypt, he started dancing in joy. He said: Ali had two right hands. One of them was severed at ⟪ar:سفین⟫when ⟪ar:عمار یاسر⟫ was martyred; and the other one was cut today. When Maulana Ali (AS) heard about ⟪ar:مالک⟫assassination, he

⟪ar:یا مالک و ما ادراک ما المالک⟫

He said: O ⟪ar:مالک⟫and who do you think ⟪ar:مالک⟫was! By Allah, If he a mountain, he would be the only one soaring above the others; and if he were a rock, he would have been impenetrable. No horse could ascend such a mountain, and no bird could fly up to it.  SubhanAllah!

These words should give you some idea of who ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫was. I strongly encourage you to read up on him. It is said that after Ali, if there was anyone comparable to him in strength and courage, it was none other than⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫.

# Uncompromising Justice

Returning back to Maulana Ali (AS) definition of justice
وَضَعَ الشَّئُی حَقَّ مَحَلِّہِ

Justice is to put everything in its right place. The way we apply this to Allah, is by putting our relationship with him in the right place. By establishing the right relationship with Allah. Everything else is derived from this spiritual imperative. This exact same order of precedence is established by Maulana Ali (AS) in his letter to ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫ where he says: Be just with Allah and be just with people. In other words, give Allah was his due to him, and give people (the creation of Allah) what is due to them. It also means, to conform as best you can to Allah's nature, which is not only the source of justice but **is**justice.

In one of his sermons Maulana Ali (AS) says: I bear witness that Allah is Justice and he acts justly. He didn't say Allah is "just"; he said Allah is justice itself. Now we can reasonably argue, that when it comes to Allah, everything is always in its right place. But when it comes to us as human beings, all we can do is make the effort to put everything in its right place. With Allah, justice is an immutable metaphysical quality; whereas with humans, that effort to implement justice is dynamic and volatile. So that's a valid argument. But the key thing to understand here, is that our moral effort to act justly in **this** world - where things are not in their right place - is rooted in an innate spiritual predisposition to justice. We already have these qualities of justice buried deep within our intellects. So when we try to be just, we are basically expressing those same principle of justice, that are in complete alignment with the nature of the divine. So our effort for justice in this world, demonstrates a spiritual affinity with the ultimate nature of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). So basically we're trying to align our nature with the nature of our creator; we're trying to become like our creator; we're trying to perfect ourselves in the image of our creator.

Now when you broaden this concept and apply it to state and society, one of the most obvious consequence that emerges from it is piety and rectitude on the part of the rulers. Maulana Ali (AS) says in one of his sermons, "The most momentous of the reciprocal rights that Allah has made obligatory, is the right of the ruler over the subjects, and the right of the subjects over the ruler—a reciprocal obligation which Allah has ordained for them.'

Then he makes the point that is indispensable for any discourse on good governance in Islam. He says: The subjects will not be righteous except through the righteousness of the rulers, and the rulers will not be righteous except through the uprightness of the subjects. So if the subjects render to the ruler his rightful due and the ruler renders them theirs, only then will truth be honored amongst them, and the ways of religion will be upheld and the banners of justice will be erected.'

So you can see the stress he places on on the necessity of piety on the part of the ruler. It is a central aspect of his message to his governor. But you'll see it involves much more than a simple emphasis on abiding by the the formal rules of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫. The entire life of Maulana Ali (AS) can be seen as a quest for conforming to the divine nature; and particularly this quality of justice - as perfectly as possible. There are numerous examples of this during his life - but there is one defining moment during his caliphate that clearly illustrates the rigor with which he adhered to justice at all costs.

We know that ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫revolt resulted in a civil war that involved the entire Muslim empire. Everyone knew that all ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫wanted was power. He was the governor of Syria during the time of ⟪ar:عثمان⟫. When ⟪ar:عثمان⟫was killed, Maulana Ali became his greatest threat, because he knew that Ali would never let him stay in power. So what did he do? He devised this whole Civil War, disguising it under the banner of finding the murderer of ⟪ar:عثمان⟫, but in reality he wanted to eliminate his greatest threat that was Maulana Ali himself.

So when Maulana Ali assumed the throne of caliphate, he was advised by his cousin ⟪ar:ابن العباس⟫ that he should temporarily let the governors that ⟪ar:عثمان⟫had selected, stay in power. And once the new order had been consolidated, he should remove them. Maulana Ali's reply to that proposal was: I have no doubt that this would be the best strategy for the sake of reconciliation in this world; ⟪ar:لکن یلزَمَنِی من الحق⟫But there is my obligation to the truth, and my knowledge of ⟪ar:عثمان⟫governors. So by Allah, I shall never let any one of them stay at their position. So with that statement, the die was cast; and he immediately dismissed ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫along with all the other governors that ⟪ar:عثمان⟫had appointed.

But what I'd like you to note is that it was perfectly clear to him, that the solution ⟪ar:العباس⟫ proposed had every chance of success - because he knew that all ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫wanted was worldly power and would have been content with the prospect of governing Syria. There are several commentators who have referred to this decision as a sign of Ali's lack of political acumen - because it pretty much guaranteed the revolt of ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫.

But what the author is trying to highlight is - that in reality, Maulana Ali's decision was an expression of his disdain for worldly ease, if that ease was purchased at the cost of compromising essential principles. He said in one of his sermons, that 'People renounce no part of their religion for the sake of rectifying their world without Allah opening them up to something worse than it. In other words, when people compromise the principles of Allah to create ease in their life, or just to get out of a difficult situation, ٓAllah makes it worse.

And letting ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫or the other governors stay in power, knowing well that their corruption was beyond doubt and redemption - for Maulana Ali, that would have been like "renouncing" a part of his religion; because to him, a religion without justice was not religion at all. So the idea of supporting unjust governors, even for the sake of a tactical maneuver, was an inconceivable compromise on religious principles for him. This adherence to his religious principles is evident in countless actions over the period of his  lifetime.

And this is exactly what we will find in his letter to ⟪ar:مالک الاشتر⟫. In studying this letter, we will learn about the various dimensions of justice - political, ethical and spiritual. We will also learn about injustice, tyranny, oppression and corruption. We will learn that justice can operate at both a horizontal plane, as well as a vertical plane. The horizontal plane applying to creation, and the vertical plane applying to the creator. We will learn what happens when justice is conceived exclusively in terms of individual  or horizontal values - like in the case of a secular or a moral philosophy that is completely divorced from religious principles. We will also study its counterpart - the crucial role that devotion, worship, and a deep contemplation of the divine Reality plays; and how it serves as the fountainhead from which a fully integrated form of virtue naturally and endlessly flows.

The author does not include the letter in the book. He assumes we've all read it and are aware of it for this discourse. So I thought today would be a good time to just read through the letter once. I'll add the entire letter with the Arabic to the website. It's quite a lengthy letter so I'll skip over the Arabic portion and just read the translation.

# The letter
بِسمِ اللهِ الرَّحمنِ الرَّحيم

This is what Allah's servant ⟪ar:امیر المومنین علی ابن ابی طالب⟫ has ordered ⟪ar:مالک ابن حارث الاشتر⟫ in his letter of appointment for him, when he made him Governor of Egypt, for the collection of its revenues, fighting against its enemies, seeking good for its people, and making its cities prosperous.
هذَا مَا أَمَرَ بِهِ عَبْدُ اللهِ عَلِيٌ أَميِرُ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ، مَالِكَ بْنَ الْحَارِثِ الاْشْتَرَ فِي عَهْدِهِ إِلَيْهِ، حِينَ وَلاَّهُ مِصْرَ: جِبْوةَ خَرَاجِهَا، وَجِهَادَ عَدُوِّهَا، وَاسْتِصْلاَحَ أَهْلِهَا، وَعِمَارَةَ بِلاَدِهَا.

He has ordered him to fear Allah; to prefer obedience to him, and to follow what he has commanded in his Book, from his obligatory and voluntary commands. For without following these commands, one cannot achieve virtue. Neither does one become evil, except by opposing and ignoring them; and to help Allah the Glorified, with his heart, hand and tongue. For Allah assumes responsibility for helping the one who helps him, and protecting the one who supports him. He also orders him to break his heart away from passions, and to restrain it at the time of their increase, for indeed the heart commands to evil until Allah has mercy.
أَمَرَهُ بِتَقْوَى اللهِ، وَإِيثَارِ طَاعَتِهِ، وَاتِّبَاعِ مَا أَمَرَ بِهِ فِي كِتَابِهِ: مِنْ فَرَائِضِهِ وَسُنَنِهِ، الَّتِي لاَ يَسْعَدُ أَحَدٌ إِلاَّ بِاتِّبَاعِهَا، وَلاَ يَشْقَى إِلاَّ مَعَ جُحُودِهَا وَإِضَاعَتِهَا، وَأَنْ يَنْصُرَ اللهَ سُبْحَانَهُ بَيَدِهِ وَقَلْبِهِ وَلِسَانِهِ، فَإِنَّهُ، جَلَّ اسْمُهُ، قَدْ تَكَفَّلَ بِنَصْرِ مَنْ نَصَرَهُ، وَإِعْزَازِ مَنْ أَعَزَّهُ. وَأَمَرَهُ أَنْ يَكْسِرَ نَفْسَهُ عِنْدَ الشَّهَوَاتِ، وَيَزَعَهَا عِنْدَ الْجَمَحَاتِ، فَإِنَّ النَّفْسَ أَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ، إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ.

## The qualifications of a governor and his responsibilities

Then, know, O Malik, that I have sent you to an area where there have been governments before you. Governments that were both,  just, as well as oppressive. People will now view your dealings with them, as you used to watch the dealings of rulers before you. People will criticize you, just as you criticized your rulers. Indeed, the virtuous are known by the reputation that Allah spreads about them through the tongues of his creatures. Therefore your best acquisition should be the accumulation of good deeds. So control your passions, and restrain your heart from doing what is not lawful for you, because restraining the heart means detaining it just half way between what it likes and dislikes.
مَّ اعْلَمْ يَا مَالكُ، أَنِّي قدْ وَجَّهْتُكَ إِلَى بِلاَد قَدْ جَرَتْ عَلَيْهَا دُوَلٌ قَبْلَكَ، مِنْ عَدْل وَجَوْر، وَأَنَّ النَّاسَ يَنْظُرُونَ مِنْ أُمُورِكَ فِى مِثْلِ مَا كُنْتَ تَنْظُرُ فِيهِ مِنْ أُمُورِ الْوُلاَةِ قَبْلَكَ، وَيَقُولُونَ فِيكَ مَا كُنْتَ تَقُولُ فِيهِمْ،إِنَّمَا يُسْتَدَلُّ عَلَى الصَّالِحِينَ بِمَا يُجْرِي اللهُ لَهُمْ عَلَى أَلْسُنِ عِبَادِهِ. فَلْيَكُنْ أَحَبَّ الذَّخَائِرِ إِلَيْكَ ذَخِيرَةُ الْعَمَلِ الصَّالِحِ، فَامْلِكْ هَوَاكَ، وَشُحَّ بِنَفْسِكَ عَمَّا لاَ يَحِلُّ لَكَ، فَإِنَّ الشُّحَّ بِالنَّفْسِ الاْنْصَافُ مِنْهَا فَيَما أَحْبَبْتَ وَكَرِهْتَ.

Habituate your heart towards mercy, affection and kindness towards your subjects. Do not tower over them like greedy beasts who feel it is fair to devour them. People are of two kinds. Either they are your brother in religion; or they are similar to you in creation. They will slip from time to time and make mistakes. They may act wrongly, willingly or by neglect. So extend your forgiveness to them in the same way you would like Allah to extend his forgiveness to you. For you are watching over them, and your responsible Commander is watching over you, while Allah is watching over the one who has appointed you. Allah has selected you to manage their affairs and has tried you through them.
وَأَشْعِرْ قَلْبَكَ الرَّحْمَةَ لِلرَّعِيَّةِ، وَالْـمَحَبَّةَ لَهُمْ، وَاللُّطْفَ بِهِمْ، وَلاَ تَكُونَنَّ عَلَيْهِمْ سَبُعاً ضَارِياً تَغْتَنِمُ أَكْلَهُمْ، فَإِنَّهُمْ صِنْفَانِ: إِمَّا أَخٌ لَكَ فِي الدِّينِ، وَإمّا نَظِيرٌ لَكَ فِي الْخَلْقِ، يَفْرُطُ مِنْهُمُ الزَّلَلُ، وَتَعْرِضُ لَهُمُ الْعِلَلُ،يُؤْتَى عَلَى أَيْدِيهِمْ فِي الَعَمْدِ وَالْخَطَاَ، فَأَعْطِهِمْ مِنْ عَفْوِكَ وَصَفْحِكَ مِثْلَ الَّذِي تُحِبُّ أَنْ يُعْطِيَكَ اللهُ مِنْ عَفْوِهِ وَصَفْحِهِ، فَإِنَّكَ فَوْقَهُمْ، وَ وَالِي الاْمْرِ عَلَيْكَ فَوْقَكَ، وَاللهُ فَوْقَ مَنْ وَلاَّكَ! وَقَدِ اسْتَكْفَاكَ أَمْرَهُمْ، وَابْتَلاَكَ بِهِمْ.

Do not set yourself to fight Allah, because you have no power against his. Nor can you do without his forgiveness and mercy. Do not stop repenting and seeking Allah's forgiveness, and be merciful when punishing. Do not act hastily in anger if you can find a way out of it. Do not say: "I have been given authority, I should be obeyed when I command," because it engenders confusion in the hearts, weakens religion and takes one near ruin. If the authority in which you are placed produces pride or vanity in your heart, then reflect on the greatness of Allah's realm over you. Reflect on his might - the likes of which you do not even possess over your own self. This will curb your haughtiness, cure you of your high temper, and return your departed wisdom back to you.
وَلاَ تَنْصِبَنَّ نَفْسَكَ لِحَرْبِ اللهِ، فَإِنَّهْ لاَيَدَيْ لَكَ بِنِقْمَتِهِ، وَلاَ غِنَى بِكَ عَنْ عَفْوِهِ وَرَحْمَتِهِ. وَلاَ تَنْدَمَنَّ عَلَى عَفْو، وَلاَ تَبْجَحَنَّ بِعُقُوبَة، وَلاَ تُسْرِعَنَّ إِلَى بَادِرَة وَجَدْتَ مِنْهَا مَنْدُوحَةً، وَلاَ تَقُولَنَّ: إِنِّي مُؤَمَّرٌ آمُرُ فَأُطَاعُ، فَإِنَّ ذلِكَ إِدْغَالٌ فِي الْقَلْبِ، وَمَنْهَكَةٌ لِلدِّينِ، وَتَقَرُّبٌ مِنَ الْغِيَرِ. وَإِذَا أَحْدَثَ لَكَ مَا أَنْتَ فِيهِ مِنْ سُلْطَانِكَ أُبَّهَةً أَوْ مَخِيلَةً، فَانْظُرْ إِلَى عِظَمِ مُلْكِ اللهِ فَوْقَكَ، وَقُدْرَتِهِ مَنْكَ عَلَى مَا لاَ تَقْدرِ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ نَفْسِكَ، فَإِنَّ ذلِكَ يُطَامِنُ إِلَيْكَ مِنْ طِمَاحِكَ، وَيَكُفُّ عَنْكَ مِنْ غَرْبِكَ،يَفِيءُ إِلَيْكَ بِمَا عَزَبَ عَنْكَ مِنْ عَقْلِكَ! 

Beware of comparing yourself to Allah in his greatness! Or likening yourself to him in his power, for Allah humiliates every claimant of power, and disgraces every one who is arrogant.
إِيَّاكَ وَمُسَامَاةَ اللهِ فِي عَظَمَتِهِ، وَالتَّشَبُّهَ بِهِ فِي جَبَرُوتِهِ، فَإِنَّ اللهَ يُذِلُّ كُلَّ جَبَّار، وَيُهِينُ كُلَّ مُخْتَال.

Be just with Allah and be just towards his people. Be just against yourself, your loved ones, and those you have a fondness for among your subjects. If you do not do so, you will become oppressive. When a person oppresses the creatures of Allah, then Allah becomes his opponent instead of his creatures. And when Allah is the opponent of a person, he tramples his plea. Such a person shall remain in the state of war with Allah until he gives up his arrogance and repents. Nothing causes the reversal of Allah's bounty, or the hastening of his retribution more than continuance in oppression; for Allah hears the prayer of the oppressed and is on the look out for the oppressors.
أَنْصِفِ اللهَ وَأَنْصِفِ النَّاسَ مِنْ نَفْسِكَ، وَمِنْ خَاصَّةِ أَهْلِكَ، وَمَنْ لَكَ فِيهِ هَوىً مِنْ رَعِيَّتِكَ، فَإِنَّكَ إِلاَّ تَفْعَلْ تَظْلِمْ، وَمَنْ ظَلَمَ عِبَادَ اللهِ كَانَ اللهُ خَصْمَهُ دُونَ عِبَادِهِ، وَمَنْ خَاصَمَهُ اللهُ أَدْحَضَ حُجَّتَهُ، وَكَانَ لله حَرْباً حَتَّى يَنْزعَ وَيَتُوبَ. وَلَيْسَ شَيْءٌ أَدْعَى إِلَى تَغْيِيرِ نِعْمَةِ اللهِ وَتَعْجِيلِ نِقْمَتِهِ مِنْ إِقَامَة عَلَى ظُلْم، فَإِنَّ اللهَ سَميِعٌ دَعْوَةَ الْمَظْلُومِينَ، وَهُوَ لِلظَّالِمِينَ بِالْمِرْصَادِ. 

## Ruling should be in Favor of the people

Your most coveted path should be that which is the most equitable for the right; the most universal by way of justice; and the most comprehensive with regard to the agreement of your subjects. Because disagreement of the common people sweeps away the arguments of the chiefs, while the disagreement of the chiefs can be disregarded when compared with the agreement of the common people.

None among your subjects is more burdensome to the ruler in times of ease; less helpful in times of distress; more disliking of equitable treatment; more harassing in their asking favors, less thankful when granted favors, least appreciative of reasons when refused; and weaker in endurance during periods of life's discomforts *than the chiefs*. It is the common people of the community who are the pillars of religion, the power of the Muslims and the defense against enemies. Your inclination should therefore be towards them, and with them.
كُنْ أَحَبَّ الاْمُورِ إِلَيْكَ أَوْسَطُهَا فِي الْحَقِّ، وَأَعَمُّهَا فِي الْعَدْلِ، وَأَجْمَعُهَا لِرِضَى الرَّعِيَّةِ، فَإِنَّ سُخْطَ الْعَامَّةِ يُجْحِفُ بِرِضَى الْخَاصَّةِ، وَإِنَّ سُخْطَ الْخَاصَّةِ يُغْتَفَرُ مَعَ رِضَى الْعَامَّةِ. وَلَيْسَ أَحَدٌ مِنَ الرَّعِيَّةِ، أَثْقَلَ عَلَى الْوَالِي مَؤُونَةً فِي الرَّخَاءِ، وَأَقَلَّ مَعُونَةً لَهُ فِي الْبَلاَءِ، وَأَكْرَهَ لِلاْنْصَافِ، وَأَسْأَلَ بِالاْلْحَافِ، وَأَقَلَّ شُكْراً عِنْدَ الاْعْطَاءِ، وَأَبْطَأَ عُذْراً عِنْدَ الْمَنْعِ، وَأَضْعَفَ صَبْراً عِنْدَ مُلِمَّاتِ الدَّهْرِ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْخَاصَّةِ، وَإِنَّمَا عَمُودُ الدِّينِ، وَجِمَاعُ الْمُسْلِمِينَ، وَالْعُدَّةُ لِلاْعْدَاءِ، الْعَامَّةُ مِنَ الاْمَّةِ، فَلْيَكُنْ صِغْوُكَ لَهُمْ، وَمَيْلُكَ مَعَهُمْ. 

The ones among your subjects that must be furthest from you, and should be the worst in your opinion, are those who are most inquisitive of the shortcomings of people. Because people **do**have shortcomings and the ruler is the most appropriate person to cover up those shortcomings. Do not disclose whatever is hidden from you, because your obligation is to correct what is manifest to you; while Allah will deal with whatever is hidden from you. Therefore, cover up the shortcomings of your subjects as much as you can, and Allah will hide your shortcomings from them - the shortcomings that you would want to remain hidden from your subjects. Unfasten every knot of hatred in people, and cut away from your heart the cause of every enmity. Pretend ignorance from what is not clear to you. Do not hasten to support a backbiter - for a backbiter is a cheat even though he appears as a well-wisher.
وَلْيَكُنْ أَبْعَدَ رَعِيَّتِكَ مِنْكَ، وَأشْنَأَهُمْ عِنْدَكَ، أَطْلَبُهُمْ لِمَعَائِبِ النَّاسِ، فإنَّ في النَّاسِ عُيُوباً، الْوَالِي أَحَقُّ مَنْ سَتَرَهَا، فَلاَ تَكْشِفَنَّ عَمَّا غَابَ عَنْكَ مِنْهَا، فَإنَّمَا عَلَيْكَ تَطْهِيرُ مَا ظَهَرَ لَكَ، وَاللهُ يَحْكُمُ عَلَى مَا غَابَ عَنْكَ، فَاسْتُرِ الْعَوْرَةَ مَا اسْتَطَعْتَ يَسْتُرِ اللهُ مِنْكَ ما تُحِبُّ سَتْرَهُ مِنْ رَعِيَّتِكَ. أَطْلِقْ عَنِ النَّاسِ عُقْدَةَ كُلِّ حِقْد، وَاقْطَعْ عَنْكَ سَبَبَ كُلِّ وِتْر، وَتَغَابَ عَنْ كلِّ مَا لاَ يَضِحُ لَكَ، وَلاَ تَعْجَلَنَّ إِلَى تَصْدِيقِ سَاع، فَإِنَّ السَّاعِيَ غَاشٌ، وَإِنْ تَشَبَّهَ بِالنَّاصِحِينَ. 

## About Counselors

Do not include a **miser**among those you consult with. For he would hold you back from being generous, and caution you against destitution. Neither consult with a **coward**who would make you feel too weak for your affairs; nor a **greedy**person who will make the collection of wealth through evil means beautiful in your eyes. This is because miserliness, cowardice and greed, are qualities that bring about an unfavorable opinion of Allah.
وَلاَ تُدْخِلَنَّ فِي مَشُورَتِكَ بَخِيلاً يَعْدِلُ بِكَ عَنِ الْفَضْلِ، وَيَعِدُكَ الْفَقْرَ، وَلاَ جَبَاناً يُضعِّفُكَ عَنِ الاْمُورِ، وَلاَ حَرِيصاً يُزَيِّنُ لَكَ الشَّرَهَ بِالْجَوْرِ، فَإِنَّ الْبُخْلَ وَالْجُبْنَ وَالْحِرْصَ غَرَائِزُ شَتَّى يَجْمَعُهَا سُوءُ الظَّنِّ بِاللهِ.

Your worst minister is the one who has been a minister for corrupt people before you, and who joined them in their sins. Therefore he should not be your chief - because they are accomplice to sinners, and brothers of the oppressors. You can find good substitutes for them, who will be similar in their views and influence, while being dissimilar to them in their sins and vices. Find people who have never assisted an oppressor in his oppression, nor a sinner in his sin. They will give you the least trouble and the best support. They will be most considerate towards you, and the least inclined towards others. Therefore, make them your chief companions in privacy as well as in public.
شَرُّ وُزَرَائِكَ مَنْ كَانَ لِلاْشْرَارِ قَبْلَكَ وَزِيراً، وَمَنْ شَرِكَهُمْ فِي الاْثَامِ، فَلاَ يَكُونَنَّ لَكَ بِطَانَةً، فَإِنَّهُمْ أَعْوَانُ الاْثَمَةِ، وَإِخْوَانُ الظَّلَمَةِ، وَأَنْتَ وَاجِدٌ مِنْهُمْ خَيْرَ الْخَلَفِ مِمَّنْ لَهُ مِثْلُ آرَائِهِمْ وَنَفَاذِهِمْ، وَلَيْسَ عَلَيْهِ مِثْلُ آصَارِهِمْ وَأَوْزَارِهِمْ وَ آثَامِهِمْ، مِمَّنْ لَمْ يُعَاوِنْ ظَالِماً عَلَى ظُلْمِهِ، وَلاَ آثِماً عَلَى إِثْمِهِ، أُولئِكَ أَخَفُّ عَلَيْكَ مَؤُونَةً، وَأَحْسَنُ لَكَ مَعُونَةً، وَأَحْنَى عَلَيْكَ عَطْفاً، وَأَقَلُّ لِغَيْرِكَ إِلْفاً، فَاتَّخِذْ أُولئِكَ خَاصَّةً لِخَلَوَاتِكَ وَحَفَلاَتِكَ، 

The most preferable among them for you, should be those who speak bitter truth openly before you, and who support you least in your actions that Allah does not approve in his friends, even though, they may be according to your wishes. Associate yourself with God-fearing and truthful people; then educate them not to praise or please you for an act you did not perform. For excess praise produces pride, and drives one towards vanity.
ثُمَّ لْيَكُنْ آثَرُهُمْ عِنْدَكَ أَقْوَلَهُمْ بِمُرِّ الْحَقِّ لَكَ، وأَقَلَّهُمْ مُسَاعَدَةً فِيَما يَكُونُ مِنْكَ مِمَّا كَرِهَ اللهُ لاِوْلِيَائِهِ، وَاقِعاً ذلِكَ مِنْ هَوَاكَ حَيْثُ وَقَعَ. وَالْصَقْ بِأَهْلِ الْوَرَعِ وَالصِّدْقِ، ثُمَّ رُضْهُمْ عَلَى أَلاَّ يُطْرُوكَ وَلاَ يُبَجِّحُوكَ بِبَاطِل لَمْ تَفْعَلْهُ، فَإِنَّ كَثْرَةَ الاْطْرَاءِ تُحْدِثُ الزَّهْوَ، وَتُدْنِي مِنَ الْعِزَّةِ. 

The virtuous and the vicious should never be equal in your eyes. That would dissuade the virtuous from his virtue, and persuade the vicious towards his vice. Keep everyone in their proper position. You should know that the most conducive acts of a ruler in creating a good impression on his subjects, is by his good behavior with them; through the lightening of their hardships, and by not putting them through unbearable hardships.

You should therefore follow a course by which you will leave a good impression with your subjects. Such good acts will relieve you of great worries later on. Certainly, the one most entitled of having a favorable impression of you, is the one you have treated well; and the one most entitled to having a bad opinion of you is the one you have treated badly.
وَلاَ يَكُونَنَّ الْـمُحْسِنُ وَالْمُسِيءُ عِنْدَكَ بِمَنْزِلَة سَوَاء، فَإِنَّ فِي ذلِكَ تَزْهِيداً لاِهْلِ الاْحْسَانِ فِي الاْحْسَانِ،تَدْرِيباً لاِهْلِ الاْسَاءَةِ عَلَى الاْسَاءَةِ، وَأَلْزِمْ كُلاًّ مِنْهُمْ مَا أَلْزَمَ نَفْسَهُ. وَاعْلَمْ أَنَّهُ لَيْسَ شَيْءٌ بِأَدْعَى إِلَى حُسْنِ ظَنِّ وَال بِرَعِيَّتِهِ مِنْ إحْسَانِهِ إِلَيْهِمْ، وَتَخْفِيفِهِ الْمَؤُونَاتِ عَلَيْهِمْ، وَتَرْكِ اسْتِكْرَاهِهِ إِيَّاهُمْ عَلَى مَا لَيْسَ لهُ قِبَلَهُمْ، فَلْيَكُنْ مِنْكَ فِي ذلِكَ أَمْرٌ يَجَتَمِعُ لَكَ بِهِ حُسْنُ الظَّنِّ بِرَعِيَّتِكَ، فَإِنَّ حُسْنَ الظَّنِّ يَقْطَعُ عَنْكَ نَصَباً طَوِيلاً، وَإِنَّ أَحَقَّ مَنْ حَسُنَ ظَنُّكَ بِهِ لَمَنْ حَسُنَ بَلاَؤُكَ عِنْدَهُ، وَإِنَّ أَحَقَّ مَنْ سَاءَ ظَنُّكَ بِهِ لَمَنْ سَاءَ بَلاَؤُكَ عِنْدَهُ. 

Do not discontinue the good practices that the earlier people of this community established, for those practices created a general unity among  them; and through which your subjects have prospered. Do not innovate any line of action which injures those earlier practices. For in that case, the reward of those who had established those good practices will continue, but the burden for discontinuing them will fall upon you. Keep increasing your conversations with the scholars and discussions with the wise, to stabilize the prosperity of the areas under you; and continue what your predecessors have established.
وَلاَ تَنْقُضْ سُنَّةً صَالِحَةً عَمِلَ بِهَا صُدُورُ هذِهِ الاْمَّةِ، وَاجْتَمَعتْ بِهَا الاْلْفَةُ، وَصَلَحَتْ عَلَيْهَا الرَّعِيَّةُ،لاَ تُحْدِثَنَّ سُنَّةً تَضُرُّ بِشَيء مِنْ مَاضِي تِلْكَ السُّنَنِ، فَيَكُونَ الاْجْرُ بِمَنْ سَنَّهَا، وَالْوِزْرُ عَلَيْكَ بِمَا نَقَضْتَ مِنْهَا. وَأَكْثِرْ مُدَارَسَةَ الَعُلَمَاءِ، وَمُنَافَثَةَ الْحُكَمَاءِ، فِي تَثْبِيتِ مَا صَلَحَ عَلَيْهِ أَمْرُ بِلاَدِكَ، وَإِقَامَةِ مَا اسْتَقَامَ بِهِ النَّاسُ قَبْلَكَ. 

## The various classes of people

Know that people belong to various classes that only prosper together. They are not independent of one another. Among them are the armies of Allah. Then the secretarial workers of the common people and the chiefs, then the dispensers of justice, then those engaged in law and order, then the payers of head tax (⟪ar:جزیۃ⟫) and land tax (⟪ar:خَرَج⟫) from the protected disbelievers and the common Muslims. Then there are the traders and the men of industry, and finally the lowest class of the needy and the destitute. Allah has fixed the share of each one of them, and laid down his precepts about the limits of each in his Book and the ⟪ar:سنۃ⟫ of his Prophet - by way of of a settlement which is preserved with us.
وَاعْلَمْ أَنَّ الرَّعِيَّةَ طَبَقَاتٌ لاَ يَصْلُحُ بَعْضُهَا إلاَّ بِبَعْض، وَلاَ غِنَى بِبَعْضِهَا عَنْ بَعْض: فَمِنْهَا جُنُودُ اللهِ،مِنْهَا كُتَّابُ الْعَامَّةِ وَالْخَاصَّةِ، وَمِنْهَا قُضَاةُ الْعَدْلِ، وَمِنهَا عُمَّالُ الاْنْصَافِ وَالرِّفْقِ، وَمِنْهَا أَهْلُ الْجِزْيَةِ وَالْخَراجِ مِنْ أَهْلِ الذِّمَّةِ وَمُسْلِمَةِ النَّاسِ، وَمِنْهَا التُّجَّارُ وَأَهْلُ الصِّنَاعَاتِ، وَمِنهَا الطَّبَقَةُ السُّفْلَى مِنْ ذَوِي الْحَاجَةِ وَالْمَسْكَنَةِ، وَكُلٌّ قَدْ سَمَّى اللهُ سَهْمَهُ، وَوَضَعَ عَلَى حَدِّهِ وَفَرِيضَتِهِ فِي كِتَابِهِ أَوْ سُنَّةِ نَبِيِّهِ (صلى الله عليه وآله)عَهْداً مِنْهُ عِنْدَنَا مَحْفُوظاً. 

Now the army by the will of Allah, is the fortress of the subjects, the ornament of the ruler, the strength of the religion and the means of peace. Subjects cannot exist without them, while the army can be maintained only by using the funds fixed by Allah in the revenues. Through those funds, they acquire the strength to fight the enemies, and they depend on those funds for their prosperity, and fulfilling their needs. Both these classes cannot exist without the third - who are the judges, the executives, and the secretaries who pass judgement on contracts, collect revenues and are depended upon in special and general matters.
فَالْجُنُودُ، بِإِذْنِ اللهِ، حُصُونُ الرَّعِيَّةِ، وَزَيْنُ الْوُلاَةِ، وعِزُّ الدِّينِ، وَسُبُلُ الاْمْنِ، وَلَيْسَ تَقُومُ الرَّعِيَّةُ إِلاَّ بِهِمْ. ثُمَّ لاَ قِوَامَ لِلْجُنُودِ إِلاَّ بِمَا يُخْرِجُ اللهُ لَهُمْ مِنَ الْخَرَاجِ الَّذِي يَقْوَوْنَ بِهِ فِي جِهَادِ عَدُوِهِمْ، وَيَعْتَمِدُونَ عَلَيْهِ فِيَما أصْلَحهُمْ، وَيَكُونُ مِنْ وَرَاءِ حَاجَتِهِمْ. ثُمَّ لاَ قِوَامَ لِهذَيْنِ الصِّنْفَيْنِ إِلاَّ بِالصِّنْفِ الثَّالِثِ مِنَ الْقُضَاةِ وَالْعُمَّالِ وَالْكُتَّابِ، لِمَا يُحْكِمُونَ مِنَ الْمَعَاقِدِ، وَيَجْمَعُونَ مِنْ الْمَنَافِعِ، وَيُؤْتَمَنُونَ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ خَوَاصِّ الاْمُورِ وَعَوَامِّهَا. 

These mentioned classes cannot exist, except through the traders and the men of industry, who provide necessities for them, establish markets and make it possible for others to not have to do this with their own hands. Then comes the support of the lowest class that comprises of the needy and the destitute. To support and help them is an obligation, and each one of them has a share in livelihood in the name of Allah. Each one of them has a right on the ruler according to what is needed for their prosperity. The ruler cannot acquit himself of the obligations laid upon him by Allah in this matter, except by striving and seeking help from Allah, and by training himself to adhere to the just  principles, and by enduring on that account all that is easy or difficult.
وَلاَ قِوَامَ لَهُمْ جَمِيعاً إِلاَّ بِالتُّجَّارِ وَذَوِي الصِّنَاعَاتِ، فِيَما يَجْتَمِعُونَ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ مَرَافِقِهِمْ، وَيُقِيمُونَهُ مِنْ أَسْوَاقِهِمْ، وَيَكْفُونَهُمْ مِنَ التَّرَفُّقِ بِأَيْدِيهِمْ ممّا لاَ يَبْلُغُهُ رِفْقُ غَيْرِهِمْ. ثُمَّ الطَّبَقَةُ السُّفْلَى مِنْ أَهْلِ الْحَاجَةِ وَالْمَسْكَنَةِ الَّذِينَ يَحِقُّ رِفْدُهُمْ وَمَعُونَتُهُمْ. وَفِي اللهِ لِكُلّ سَعَةٌ، وَلِكُلٍّ عَلَى الْوَالِي حَقٌ بِقَدْرِ مَا يُصْلِحُهُ. وَلَيْسَ يَخْرُجُ الْوَالِي مِنْ حَقِيقَةِ مَا أَلْزَمَهُ اللهُ مِنْ ذلِكَ إِلاَّ بِالاْهْتِمامِ وَالاسْتِعَانَةِ بِاللهِ، وَتَوْطِينِ نَفْسِهِ عَلَى لُزُومِ الْحَقِّ، وَالصَّبْرِ عَلَيْهِ فِيَما خَفَّ عَلَيْهِ أَوْ ثَقُلَ. 

## Communion with Allah

The acts by which you should purify your religion for Allah should be the fulfillment of obligations that are specifically for him. Therefore, devote some of your physical activity during the night and the day to Allah. Your worship seeking nearness to Allah should be complete, and without defect or deficiency, regardless of whatever physical exertion it may involve. When you lead the prayers for people, do not scare them away by prolonging it; nor waste it by making it too short. For among them are the sick, as well as those who have needs of their own. When the Messenger of Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) sent me to Yemen, I inquired how I should offer prayers with them? He replied, "Say the prayers as the weakest of them would say, and be compassionate to the believers.
وَلْيَكُنْ فِي خَاصَّةِ مَا تُخْلِصُ لله بِهِ دِينَكَ: إِقَامَةُ فَرَائِضِهِ الَّتي هِيَ لَهُ خَاصَّةً، فَأَعْطِ اللهَ مِن بَدَنِكَ فِي لَيْلِكَ وَنَهَارِكَ، وَوَفِّ مَا تَقَرَّبْتَ بِهِ إِلَى اللهِ مِنْ ذلِكَ كَاملاً غَيْرَ مَثْلُوم وَلاَ مَنْقُوص، بَالِغاً مِنْ بَدَنِكَ مَا بَلَغَ. وَإِذَا قُمْتَ فِي صلاَتِكَ لِلنَّاسِ، فَلاَ تَكُونَنَّ مُنَفّرِاً وَلاَ مُضَيِّعاً، فَإِنَّ فِي النَّاسِ مَنْ بِهِ الْعِلَّةُ وَلَهُ الْحَاجَةُ. وَقَدْ سَأَلْتُ رَسُولَ اللهِ (صلى الله عليه وآله) حِينَ وَجَّهَنِي إِلَى الَيمنِ كَيْفَ أُصَلِّي بِهِمْ؟ فَقَالَ: "صَلِّ بِهِمْ كَصَلاَةِ أَضْعَفِهِمْ، وَكُنْ بِالْمُؤْمِنِينَ رَحِيماً". 

## On the Behavior and action of a Ruler

Do not seclude yourself from people for a long time, because the seclusion of those in authority from their subjects is a kind of narrow-sightedness and causes ignorance about their affairs. Seclusion also prevents rulers from being informed. As a result of not being informed, you will regard important matters as trivial, and trivial matters as important, good matters as bad and bad matters as good, and you will confuse truth with falsehood. After all, a governor is a human being, and cannot have knowledge of things which people keep hidden from him.
وَأَمَّا بَعْدَ هذا، فَلاَ تُطَوِّلَنَّ احْتِجَابَكَ عَنْ رَعِيَّتِكَ، فَإِنَّ احْتِجَابَ الْوُلاَةِ عَنِ الرَّعِيَّةِ شُعْبَةٌ مِنَ الضِّيقِ، وَقِلَّةُ عِلْم بِالاْمُورِ، وَالاْحْتِجَابُ مِنْهُمْ يَقْطَعُ عَنْهُمْ عِلْمَ مَا احْتَجَبُوا دوُنَهُ فَيَصْغُرُ عِندَهُمْ الْكَبِيرُ، وَيَعْظَمُ الصَّغِيرُ، وَيَقْبُحُ الْحَسَنُ، وَيَحْسُنُ الْقَبِيحُ، وَيُشَابُ الْحَقُّ بِالْبَاطِلِ، وَإِنَّمَا الْوَالِي بَشَرٌ لاَ يَعْرِفُ مَا تَوَارَى عَنْهُ النَّاسُ بِهِ مِنَ الاْمُورِ، 

There are no marks on the face of truth to differentiate its various expressions from falsehood. Then you can be one of two kinds of men. Either you will be generous in granting rights - and in that case what's the point of hiding when you are discharging your obligations and performing good acts? Or you are a victim of stinginess. In that case people will soon give up asking you, as they will lose hope of any generous treatment from you; even though they may have several needs from you.
وَلَيْسَتْ عَلَى الْحَقِّ سِمَاتٌ تُعْرَفُ بِهَا ضُرُوبُ الصِّدْقِ مِنَ الْكَذِبِ، وَإِنَّمَا أَنْتَ أَحَدُ رَجُلَيْنَ: إِمَّا امْرُؤٌ سَخَتْ نَفْسُكَ بِالْبَذْلِ فِي الْحَقِّ، فَفِيمَ احْتِجَابُكَ مِنْ وَاجِبِ حَقٍّ تُعْطِيهِ، أَوْ فِعْل كَرِيم تُسْدِيهِ، أَوْ مُبْتَلَىً بِالْمَنعِ، فَمَا أَسْرَعَ كَفَّ النَّاسِ عَنْ مَسْأَلَتِكَ إِذَا أَيِسُوا مِنْ بَذْلِكَ! مَعَ أَنَّ أَكْثَرَ حَاجَاتِ النَّاسِ إِلَيْكَ [مـ] ما لاَ مَؤُونَةَ فِيهِ عَلَيْكَ، مِنْ شَكَاةِ مَظْلِمَة، أَوْ طَلَبِ إِنْصاف فِي مُعَامَلَة. 

In addition, a governor has favorites, and people that have easy access to him. They misappropriate things; they are high-handed, and do not observe justice in matters. You should destroy the root of evil in people by cutting away the causes of these defects. Do not make any land grants to your friends or supporters. They should not expect from you  possession of land that may cause harm to adjoining people. Consider matters of irrigation or common services, whose burden people receiving the land place on others. If you do that, they will benefit and you will not, and the blame will lie on you in this world and the next.
ثُمَّ إِنَّ لِلْوَالِي خَاصَّةً وبِطَانَةً، فِيهِمُ اسْتِئْثَارٌ وَتَطَاوُلٌ، وَقِلَّةُ إِنْصَاف فِي مُعَامَلَة، فَاحْسِمْ مَادَّةَ أُولئِكَ بِقَطْعِ أَسْبَابِ تِلْكَ الاْحْوَالِ، وَلاَ تُقْطِعَنَّ لاِحَد مِنْ حَاشِيتِكَ وَحَامَّتِكَ قَطِيعةً، وَلاَ يَطْمَعَنَّ مِنْكَ فِي اعْتِقَادِ عُقْدَة، تَضُرُّ بِمَنْ يَلِيهَا مِنَ النَّاسِ، فِي شِرْب أَوْ عَمَل مُشْتَرَك، يَحْمِلُونَ مَؤُونَتَهُ عَلَى غَيْرِهِمْ، فَيَكُونَ مَهْنَأُ ذلِكَ لَهُمْ دُونَكَ، وَعَيْبُهُ عَلَيْكَ فِي الدُّنْيَا وَالاْخِرَةِ. 

Give everyone their due rights, whether they are near to you or distant from you. You should always be enduring and watchful in these matters, even though it may involve your relations and favorites. Keep in mind the reward of that which appears burdensome on you, for its reward is handsome.
وَأَلْزِمِ الْحَقَّ مَنْ لَزِمَهُ مِنَ الْقَرِيبِ وَالْبَعِيدِ، وَكُنْ فِي ذلِكَ صَابِراً مُحْتَسِباً، وَاقِعاً ذلِكَ مِنْ قَرَابَتِكَ خَاصَّتِكَ حَيْثُ وَقَعَ، وَابْتَغِ عَاقِبَتَهُ بِمَا يَثْقُلُ عَلَيْكَ مِنْهُ، فَإِنَّ مَغَبَّةَ ذلِكَ مَحْمُودَةٌ. 

If the subjects suspect you of high-handedness (using power or authority without considering the feelings of others ), explain to them your position openly, and remove their suspicion through your explanation, as it will secure your aim of keeping them firm in truth.
وَإِنْ ظَنَّتِ الرَّعِيَّةُ بِكَ حَيْفاً، فَأَصْحِرْ لَهُمْ بِعُذْرِكَ، وَاعْدِلْ عَنكَ ظُنُونَهُمْ بِإِصْحَارِكَ، فَإِنَّ فِي ذلِكَ رِيَاضَةً مِنْكَ لِنَفْسِكَ، وَرِفْقاً بِرَعِيَّتِكَ، وَ إِعْذَاراً تَبْلُغُ فِيه حَاجَتَكَ مِنْ تَقْوِيمِهِمْ عَلَى الْحَقِّ. 

Do not reject a peace offering from your enemy in which lies Allah's pleasure, because peace brings rest to your army, relief from your worries, and safety for your country. But after peace, maintain great apprehension from your enemy, because often the enemy offers peace in order to benefit from your negligence. Therefore, be cautious and do not act based on your desire in this matter.
وَلاَ تَدْفَعَنَّ صُلْحاً دَعَاكَ إِلَيْهِ عَدُوُّ كَ لله فِيهِ رِضىً، فإِنَّ فِي الصُّلْحِ دَعَةً لِجُنُودِكَ، وَرَاحَةً مِنْ هُمُومِكَ، وأَمْناً لِبِلاَدِكَ، وَلَكِنِ الْحَذَرَ كُلَّ الْحَذَرِ مِنْ عَدُوِّكِ بَعْدَ صُلْحِهِ، فَإِنَّ الْعَدُوَّ رُبَّمَا قَارَبَ لِيَتَغَفَّلَ، فَخُذْ بِالْحَزْمِ، وَاتَّهِمْ فِي ذلِكَ حُسْنَ الظَّنِّ. 

If you conclude an agreement between yourself and your enemy, or enter into a pledge with him, then fulfill your agreement and discharge your pledge faithfully. Place yourself as a shield against whatever you have pledged, because among the obligations of Allah, there is nothing on which people are more strongly united than respect for fulfilling pledges, despite the difference in their ideas and variation of their views.

Besides Muslims, even disbelievers have abide by their agreements, because they realize the dangers that would come in the wake of its violation. Therefore, do not deceive your enemy, because no one can offend Allah except the ignorant and the wicked. Allah made his agreement, and pledged the sign of security, which he has spread over his creatures through his mercy and an asylum, in which they stay under his protection and seek the benefit of his nearness. Therefore, there should be no deceit, cunning or duplicity in it.
وَإِنْ عَقَدْتَ بَيْنَكَ وَبَيْنَ عَدُوّ لَكَ عُقْدَةً، أَوْ أَلْبَسْتَهُ مِنْكَ ذِمَّةً، فَحُطْ عَهْدَكَ بِالْوَفَاءِ، وَارْعَ ذِمَّتَكَ بِالاْمَانَةِ، وَاجْعَلْ نَفْسَكَ جُنَّةً دُونَ مَا عْطَيْتَ، فَإِنَّهُ لَيْسَ مِنْ فَرَائِضِ اللهِ عزوجلّ شَيْءٌ النَّاسُ أَشدُّ عَلَيْهِ اجْتِماعاً، مَعَ تَفْرِيقِ أَهْوَائِهِمْ، وَتَشْتِيتِ آرَائِهِمْ، مِنَ تَعْظيمِ الْوَفَاءِ بِالْعُهُودِ، وَقَدْ لَزِمَ ذلِكَ الْمُشْرِكُونَ فِيَما بَيْنَهُمْ دُونَ الْمُسْلِمِينَ لِمَا اسْتَوْبَلُوا مِنْ عَوَاقِبِ الْغَدْرِ، فَلاَ تَغْدِرَنَّ بِذِمَّتِكَ، وَلاَ تَخِيسَنَّ بَعَهْدِكَ، وَلاَ تَخْتِلَنَّ عَدُوَّكَ، فَإِنَّهُ لاَ يَجْتَرِىءُ عَلَى اللهِ إِلاَّ جَاهِلٌ شَقِيٌّ. وَقَدْ جَعَلَ اللهُ عَهْدَهُ وَذِمَّتَهُ أَمْناً أَفْضَاهُ بَيْنَ الْعِبَادِ بِرَحْمَتِهِ، وَحَرِيماً يَسْكُنُونَ إِلَى مَنَعَتِهِ،يَسْتَفِيضُونَ إِلَى جِوَارِهِ، فَلاَ إِدْغَالَ وَلاَ مُدَالَسَةَ، وَلاَ خِدَاعَ فِيهِ، 

Do not enter into an agreement which may be open to different interpretations; and do not change the interpretation of vague words after its conclusion and confirmation. If an agreement of Allah puts you in hardship, do not seek its repudiation without justification, because the bearing of hardships through which you expect relief and a handsome reward, is better than a violation whose consequence you fear. Because you will then also face the fear of being called upon by Allah to account for it, and you will not be able to seek forgiveness for it in this world or the next.
وَلاَ تَعْقِدْ عَقْداً تَجُوزُ فِيهِ الْعِلَلُ، وَلاَ تُعَوِّلَنَّ عَلَى لَحْنِ القَوْل بَعْدَ التَّأْكِيدِ وَالتَّوْثِقَةِ، وَلاَ يَدْعُوَنَّكَ ضِيقُ أَمْر لَزِمَكَ فِيهِ عَهْدُ اللهِ، إِلَى طَلَبِ انْفِسَاخِهِ بِغَيْرِ الْحَقِّ، فَإنَّ صَبْرَكَ عَلَى ضِيق تَرْجُو انْفِرَاجَهُ وَفَضْلَ عَاقِبَتِهِ، خَيْرٌ مِنْ غَدْر تَخَافُ تَبِعَتَهُ، وَأَنْ تُحِيطَ بِكَ مِنَ اللهِ فِيهِ طَلِبَةٌ، لاَتَسْتَقِيلُ فِيهَا دُنْيَاكَ وَلاَ آخِرَتَكَ. 

You should avoid shedding blood without justification, because nothing is more inviting of Divine retribution, greater in evil consequences, more effective in the decline of prosperity, and cutting short of life, than the shedding of blood without justification. On the Day of Judgement Allah the Glorified, will begin his judgement with cases of bloodshed committed by them.

Therefore, do not strengthen your authority by shedding prohibited blood - for this will weaken and lower your authority. In fact it will destroy it and shift it. You cannot offer any excuse before Allah, or before me, for killing someone as there must be revenge in it. If you are involved in it by error, and you exceed in the use of your whip, sword, or hand in inflicting punishment, then the arrogance of your authority should not prevent you from paying the blood price to the successors of the person who was killed.
إِيَّاكَ وَالدَّمَاءَ وَسَفْكَهَا بِغَيْرِ حِلِّهَا، فَإِنَّهُ لَيْسَ شَيْءٌ أَدْعَى لِنِقْمَة، وَلاَ أَعْظَمَ لِتَبِعَة، وَلاَ أَحْرَى بِزَوَالِ نِعْمَة، وَانْقِطَاعِ مُدَّة، مِنْ سَفْكِ الدِّمَاءِ بِغَيْرِ حَقِّهَا، وَاللهُ سُبْحَانَهُ مُبْتَدِىءٌ بِالْحُكْمِ بَيْنَ الْعِبَادِ، فِيَما تَسَافَكُوا مِنَ الدِّمَاءِ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامةِ، فَلاَ تُقَوِّيَنَّ سُلْطَانَكَ بِسَفْكِ دَم حَرَام، فَإِنَّ ذلِكَ مِمَّا يُضْعِفُهُ وَيُوهِنُهُ، بَلْ يُزيِلُهُ وَيَنْقُلُهُ، وَلاَ عُذْرَ لَكَ عِنْدَ اللهِ وَلاَ عِنْدِي فِي قَتْلِ الْعَمدِ، لاِنَّ فِيهِ قَوَدَ الْبَدَنِ، وَإِنِ ابْتُلِيتَ بِخَطَإ وَأَفْرَطَ عَلَيْكَ سَوْطُكَ أَوْ سَيْفُكَ أَوْ يَدُكَ بِعُقُوبَة، فَإِنَّ فِي الْوَكْزَةِ فَمَا فَوْقَهَا مَقْتَلَةً، فَلاَ تَطْمَحَنَّ بِكَ نَخْوَةُ سُلْطَانِكَ عَنْ أَنْ تُؤَدِّيَ إِلَى أَوْلِيَاءِ الْمَقْتُول حَقَّهُمْ. 

You should avoid self-admiration, having reliance in what appears good in yourself, and love of exaggerated praise because this is one of the most reliable opportunities for Satan to obliterate the good deeds of the virtuous.
وَإِيَّاكَ وَالاْعْجَابَ بِنَفْسِكَ، وَالثِّقَةَ بِمَا يُعْجِبُكَ مِنْهَا، وَحُبَّ الاْطْرَاءِ، فَإِنَّ ذلِكَ مِنْ أَوْثَقِ فُرَصِ الشَّيْطَانِ فِي نَفْسِهِ، لِـيَمْحَقَ مَا يَكُونُ مِنْ إِحْسَانِ الْـمُحْسِنِينَ. 

Avoid reminding people of your good favors to them, or praising your own actions, or making promises and breaking them, because reminding people of favors destroys the good, self-praise takes away the light of truth, and breaking promises earns the hatred of Allah and of the people. Allah, the Glorified, says:

The Ranks [61:3]
[ سورۃ ۶۱] الصف

‏كَبُرَ مَقْتًا عِندَ ٱللَّهِ أَن تَقُولُوا۟ مَا لَا تَفْعَلُونَ ‎۳
Most hateful it is with Allah that you say that which you do not do.

Most hateful is it to Allah that you say what you yourselves do not do
وَإِيَّاكَ وَالْمَنَّ عَلَى رَعِيَّتِكَ بِإِحْسَانِكَ، أَوِ التَّزَيُّدَ فِيَما كَانَ مِنْ فِعْلِكَ، أَوْ أَنْ تَعِدَهُمْ فَتُتْبِعَ مَوْعِدَكَ بِخُلْفِكَ، فَإِنَّ الْمَنَّ يُبْطِلُ الاِْحْسَانَ، وَالتَّزَيُّدَ يَذْهَبُ بِنُورِ الْحَقِّ، وَالخُلْفَ يُوجِبُ الْمَقْتَ عِنْدَاللهِ وَالنَّاسِ، قَالَ اللهُ سبحانه: (كَبُرَ مَقْتاً عِنْدَ اللهِ أَنْ تَقُولُوا مَا لاَ تَفْعَلُونَ). 

Avoid haste in matters before their time. Show slowness at its proper time. Assign every matter its proper place, and do every task at its appropriate time.
