و إيَّاكَ وَالْعَجَلَةَ بِالاْمُورِ قَبْلَ أَوَانِهَا، أَوِ التَّسَاقُطَ فِيهَا عِنْدَ إِمْكَانِهَا، أَوِ الَّلجَاجَةَ فِيهَا إِذا تَنَكَّرَتْ، أَوِ الْوَهْنَ عَنْهَا إذَا اسْتَوْضَحَتْ، فَضَعْ كُلَّ أَمْر مَوْضِعَهُ، وَأَوْقِعْ كُلَّ عَمَل مَوْقِعَهُ.

Do not appropriate for yourself that, in which people have an equal share; nor disregard matters which have come to light using the excuse that you are accountable for others. Soon the veil of all matters will be raised from your eyes and you will be required to render redress to the oppressed. Have control over your sense of prestige, any outburst of anger, the might of your arm and the sharpness of your tongue. Guard against all this by avoiding haste and by delaying severe action until your anger subsides, and you regain your self-control. You cannot withhold yourself from this, unless you bear in mind that you have to return to Allah.
وَإيَّاكَ وَالاْسْتِئْثَارَ بِمَا النَّاسُ فِيهِ أُسْوَةٌ، وَالتَّغَابِيَ عَمَّا تُعْنَى بِهِ مِمَّا قَدْ وَضَحَ لِلْعُيُونِ، فَإِنَّهُ مَأْخُوذٌ مِنْكَ لِغَيْرِكَ، وَعَمَّا قَلَيل تَنْكَشِفُ عَنْكَ أَغْطِيَةُ الاُْمُورِ، وَيُنْتَصَفُ مِنْكَ لِلْمَظْلُومِ، امْلِكْ حَمِيَّةَ أَنْفِكَ، وَسَوْرَةَ حَدِّكَ، وَسَطْوَةَ يَدِكَ، وَغَرْبَ لِسَانِكَ، وَاحْتَرِسْ مِنْ كُلِّ ذلِكَ بِكَفِّ الْبَادِرَةِ، وَتَأْخِيرِ السَّطْوَةِ، حَتَّى يَسْكُنَ غَضَبُكَ فَتَمْلِكَ الاْخْتِيَارَ، وَلَنْ تَحْكُمْ ذلِكَ مِنْ نَفْسِكَ حَتَّى تُكْثِرَ هُمُومَكَ بِذِكْرِ الْمَعَادِ إِلَى رَبِّكَ. 

It is necessary for you to remember matters of your predecessors - be it a government, a great tradition, a precedent of our Prophet (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam), or the obligatory commands contained in the Book of Allah. Then you should follow them, just as you have seen us acting upon them. You must exert yourself in following what I have enjoined upon you in this document; in which I have exhausted my pleas on you, so that if your heart advances towards its passions, you may have no plea in its support.

None but Allah can protect from evil, or grant success for the good. Among matters that the Messenger of Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam), enjoined upon me in his testament, were the exhortation to prayer, the payment of ⟪ar:زکوۃ⟫ and taking care of slaves. With that, I end my document to you, and there is no strength or power but with Allah, the All-mighty.
وَالْوَاجِبُ عَلَيْكَ أَنْ تَتَذَكَّرَ مَا مَضَى لِمَنْ تَقَدَّمَكَ: مِنْ حُكُومَة عَادِلَة، أَوْ سُنَّة فَاضِلَة، أَوْ أَثَر عَنْ نَبِيِّنَا(صلى الله عليه وآله) أَوْفَرِيضَة فِي كِتَابِ اللهِ، فَتَقْتَدِيَ بِمَا شَاهَدْتَ مِمَّا عَمِلْنَا بِهِ فِيهَا، وَتَجْتَهِدَ لِنَفْسِكَ فِي اتِّبَاعِ مَا عَهِدْتُ إِلَيْكَ فِي عهْدِي هذَا، وَاسْتَوْثَقْتُ بِهِ مِنَ الْحُجَّةِ لِنَفْسِي عَلَيْكَ، لِكَيْلاَ تَكُونَ لَكَ عِلَّةٌ عِنْدَ تَسَرُّعِ نَفْسِكَ إِلَى هَوَاهَا، فَلَنْ يَعْصِمَ مِنَ السُّوءِ وَلاَ يُوَفِّقَ لِلْخَيْرِ إلاَّ اللهُ تَعَالى. وَقَدْ كَانَ فِيَما عَهدَ إليَّ رَسُولُهُ (عليه السلام) فِي وَصَايَاهُ تَحضيضاً عَلَى الصَّلاةِ وَالزَّكَاةِ وَمَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَانُكُمْ، فَبِذَلِكَ أَخْتِمُ لَكَ مَا عَهِدَ، وَلاَ قُوَّةَ إلاَّ بِاللهِ العَظِيمِ. 

I ask Allah through the vastness of his mercy, and the greatness of his power over giving a good inclination - that he may prompt me and you to advance a clear plea before him and his creatures, in a manner that may attract his pleasure, along with a handsome praise from among the people; a good result in the country; an increase in prosperity, and a heightening of honor. And that he may allow me and you to die a death of virtue and martyrdom. Surely, we have to return to him. Peace be on the Messenger of Allah. May Allah shower his blessings and plentiful salutations on him, and his pure and chaste descendants; and that is an end to the matter.
وَأَنَا أَسْأَلُ اللهَ بِسَعَةِ رَحْمَتِهِ، وَعَظِيمِ قُدْرَتِهِ عَلَى إِعْطَاءِ كُلِّ رَغْبَة، أَنْ يُوَفِّقَنِي وَإِيَّاكَ لِمَا فيهِ رِضَاهُ مِنَ الاْقَامَةِ عَلَى الْعُذْرِ الْوَاضِحِ إِلَيْهِ وَإِلَى خَلْقِهِ، مَعَ حُسْنِ الثَّنَاءِ فِي الْعِبَادِ، وَجَمِيلِ الاْثَرِ فِي الْبَلاَدِ، وَتَمَامِ النِّعْمَةِ، وَتَضْعِيفِ الْكَرَامَةِ، وَأَنْ يَخْتِمَ لِي وَلَكَ بالسَّعَادَةِ وَالشَّهَادَةِ، إِنَّا إِلَيْهِ رَاغِبُونَ، وَالسَّلاَمُ عَلَى رَسُولِ اللهِ ً. صَلَّى اللّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَ آلِهِ الطَّيِّبينَ الطّاهِرينَ وَ سَلَّمَ تَسْليماً كَثيراً. وَالسَّلامُ.

<!-- section 15 (id=201, raw_sort=15): Importance Of Humility -->

## Importance Of Humility

In the previous session we read Maulana Ali's letter addressed to one of his closest, and most trusted companions - ⟪ar:مالک ابن الاشتر النقعی⟫. We read his advise and warnings to a ruler, in regards to injustice and oppression. I want to start by asking a question. Why did he say all this to such an esteemed a personality like ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫? Who by his own words, was a mountain that no horse could climb; and no bird could soar to its heights? I think the reason is, one - to leave this letter as a guide for all people who come in power, but more importantly, to highlight the fact that no matter who the person is, no matter how righteous his initial intentions might be; no matter how formally he subjects himself to the laws of Islam, he is nonetheless always vulnerable to the temptations offered by power. This is why we have that expression, which is more of a proven truth - that  '**power corrupts**'; or, in the words of Maulana Ali, "He who rules, appropriates (⟪ar:مَن مَلَکَ أستَذرَاءَ⟫).

So this letter can be understood as addressing not only those people who wish to rule with justice, but also those who even though, might not have corrupt intentions at the outset, are nonetheless corruptible. And this is something that we find quite often in life. People usually start in positions of power with the best of intentions; but then as time passes, they get intoxicated with their power, and they become corrupt. Therefore, to this category of people, which constitutes the overwhelming majority of mankind, this letter servers as a compass pointing to the true north for anyone and everyone in a position of power. The advice in this letter is as applicable to the king of a country, as it is to the leader of the community, as it is to the manager of a company, or the head of a household. Because no one is incorruptible.

The author now looks at selected portions of the letter, and deep dives to reveal its wisdom.

# virtue and ultimate reality

The letter begins with a resounding sad reality that talks about the potential for evil in man, especially when left to his own resources, and when deprived of divine grace. Maulana Ali begins by stressing the importance of Allah's ⟪ar:واجبات⟫- which are the religious obligations imposed on every Muslim. He says:
أَمَرَهُ بِتَقْوَى اللهِ، وَإِيثَارِ طَاعَتِهِ، وَاتِّبَاعِ مَا أَمَرَ بِهِ فِي كِتَابِهِ: مِنْ فَرَائِضِهِ وَسُنَنِهِ، الَّتِي لاَ يَسْعَدُ أَحَدٌ إِلاَّ بِاتِّبَاعِهَا، وَلاَ يَشْقَى إِلاَّ مَعَ جُحُودِهَا وَإِضَاعَتِهَا

Malik is told to prefer Allah's obedience over all things. To abide by what He has commanded in His Book - for no one prospers except by following them; and no one becomes wretched, except by rejecting and and neglecting them. Then he says:
وَأَمَرَهُ أَنْ يَكْسِرَ نَفْسَهُ عِنْدَ الشَّهَوَاتِ، وَيَزَعَهَا عِنْدَ الْجَمَحَاتِ

He orders him to sever his heart off from passions, and to restrain it at the time of their increase.

The idea he is presenting here, is that obedience to Allah's divine commands is not just **legally binding**, but they are also **spiritually liberating**. Generally, we only view these obligations as legally binding. We have to obey and follow the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫because Allah has commanded us to do so. It's an obligation! But what we almost always forget to realize, is that the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫is also spiritually liberating. Allah's obedience, plays a crucial role in liberating one from the tyranny, and the oppression of ones "soul" that commands to evil - the concupiscence soul. This is why Maulana Ali immediately follows this statement by saying:
فَإِنَّ النَّفْسَ أَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ، إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ

For indeed the heart commands to evil until Allah has mercy on it. This sentence is almost identical to the words of ⟪ar:یوسف علیہ السلام⟫when he was tempted and seduced by ⟪ar:زلیخۃ⟫. According to Quran, the only way he was able to control his ⟪ar:نفس⟫was by reminding himself that:
إِنَّ النَّفْسَ أَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ، إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ ربی

Indeed the soul commands and incites to evil, except when his master has mercy on it.

This statement or this verse sets the stage to explain the relationship between the human soul and divine grace. Without Allah's grace, the soul will just naturally tends towards evil. But with Allah's assistance, it is liberated from its own base inclinations; and is guided back to its true primordial nature; its ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫. As the Quran states

The Romans [30:30]
[ سورۃ ۳۰] الروم

**‏فَأَقِمْ وَجْهَكَ لِلدِّينِ حَنِيفًۭا ۚ فِطْرَتَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلَّتِى فَطَرَ ٱلنَّاسَ عَلَيْهَا** ۚ لَا تَبْدِيلَ لِخَلْقِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ ذَلِكَ ٱلدِّينُ ٱلْقَيِّمُ وَلَكِنَّ أَكْثَرَ ٱلنَّاسِ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ ‎۳۰
So direct your face toward the religion, inclining to truth. [Adhere to] the nature of Allah upon which He has created mankind. There should be no change in the creation of Allah . That is the correct religion, but most of the people do not know.

This explains the ultimate goal and our purpose on this earth. We are all fallen souls, and the present state of our soul is corrupt. Our goal then, is to give way to the ideal state of our primordial nature. Our natural state of ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫.  That is the true substance of every human soul. But what happens, is that the temptations and the distractions of this world, cloud this natural state. But under the covers, this original state is always there, and therefore always accessible in principle. It's like the sharp blade of the sword that is always present in the steel. All the sword-smith does, is process it in order to reveal it, to bring it out. And therefore when we return to the complementary functions of the human intellect through the guidance of the divine revelation, we are able to remove the clouds covering up our natural state of ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫.

# Buried Treasure of Intellect

In the first sermon of ⟪ar:نھج البلاغہ⟫Maulana Ali says:
وَاصْطَفى سُبْحَانَهُ مِنْ وَلَدَهِ أَنْبيَاءَ أَخَذَ عَلَى الْوَحْيِ مِيثَاقَهُم، وَعَلَى تَبْليغِ الرِّسَالَةِ أَمَانَتَهُمْ

From Adam’s progeny, Allah chose prophets and took their pledge for his revelation and for carrying His message as their trust
لَمَّا بَدَّلَ أَكْثَرُ خَلْقِهِ عَهْدَ اللهِ إِلَيْهِمْ، فَجَهِلُوا حَقَّهُ، واتَّخَذُوا الاْنْدَادَ مَعَهُ، وَاجْتَالَتْهُمُ الشَّيَاطِينُ عَنْ مَعْرفَتِهِ، وَاقتَطَعَتْهُمْ عَنْ عِبَادَتِهِ

In course of time, many people perverted Allah’s trust with them and ignored His position and took partners along with Him. Satan turned them away from knowing Him and kept them aloof from His worship.
فَبَعَثَ فِيهمْ رُسُلَهُ، وَوَاتَرَ إِلَيْهِمْ أَنْبِياءَهُ، لِيَسْتَأْدُوهُمْ مِيثَاقَ فِطْرَتِهِ، وَيُذَكِّرُوهُمْ مَنْسِيَّ نِعْمَتِهِ، وَيَحْتَجُّوا عَلَيْهِمْ بَالتَّبْلِيغِ، وَيُثِيرُوا لَهُمْ دَفَائِنَ الْعُقُولِ، وَيُرَوهُمْ آيَاتِ الْمَقْدِرَةِ: مِنْ سَقْفِ فَوْقَهُمْ مَرْفُوع، وَمِهَاد تَحْتَهُمْ مَوْضُوع، وَمَعَايِشَ تُحْيِيهِمْ، وَآجَال تُفْنِيهمْ، وَأَوْصَاب تُهْرِمُهُمْ، وَأَحْدَاث تَتَابَعُ عَلَيْهِمْ 

Then Allah sent His Messengers and series of His prophets towards them to get them to fulfill the pledges of His creation; to remind them of his bounties; to call upon them by preaching, to unearth for them the buried treasures of the intellects; and show them the signs of His Omnipotence: through the sky which is raised over them, the earth that is placed beneath them, means of living that sustain them, deaths that annihilates them; ailments that turn them old and incidents that successively betake them.

The statement that we want to focus on for this discussion is his saying that Allah sent prophets in order to... ⟪ar:وَيُثِيرُوا لَهُمْ دَفَائِنَ الْعُقُولِ⟫ to unearth for them the buried treasures of the intellects. What is this buried treasure he is referring to? It is the original, God-given knowledge and awareness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) that is proper to the ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫. It is this integrated human nature, that is in complete harmony with the divine nature, from which all of its essential virtues flow - spontaneously and unhindered. This is why he says to ⟪ar:مالک⟫:
فَلْيَكُنْ أَحَبَّ الذَّخَائِرِ إِلَيْكَ ذَخِيرَةُ الْعَمَلِ الصَّالِحِ

Let your most beloved treasure, be the treasure of virtuous acts

Now for someone whose ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫ is intact, virtue would naturally be his most beloved treasure. Why? Because such a person has reversed the natural inclinations of his egotistic soul. He has established a natural orientation towards his own true nature; and in doing so, he has aligned himself with the divine reality that is the fountainhead of all virtue. Because Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) as ⟪ar:الحق⟫- the ultimate reality - is not only the foundation of virtue, he is also the true substance of every positive and beautiful quality. This is why in ⟪ar:دعا کمیل⟫ he Maulana Ali says:
وَبِعِزَّتِكَ الَّتِي لا يَقُومُ لَهَا شَيْءٌ

###### And by Your might, without which nothing can sustain
وَبِعَظَمَتِكَ الَّتِي مَلأَتْ كُلَّ شَيٍْء

###### And by Your tremulousness, which has filled all things;
وَبِسُلْطَانِكَ الَّذِي عَلاَ كُلَّ شَيٍْء

###### by Your dominion, which towers over all things;
وَبِوَجْهِكَ الْبَاقِي بَعْدَ فَنَاءِ كُلِّ شَيٍْء

###### And by Your essence, which subsists after the annihilation of all things,
وَبِأَسْمَائِكَ الَّتِي مَلأَتْ أَرْكَانَ كُلِّ شَيٍْء

###### And by Your Names, which have filled the foundation of all things;
وَبِعِلْمِكَ الَّذِي أَحَاطَ بِكُلِّ شَيٍْء

###### And by Your knowledge, which encompasses all things;
وَبِنُورِ وَجْهِكَ الَّذِي أَضَاءَ لَهُ كُلُّ شَيٍْء

###### And by the light of Your essence, through which all things are illumined!

In these statements, notice how he attributes everything ⟪ar:کل شئی⟫ back to its divine source. Sustenance to his might. Loftiness to his dominion. Existence and Subsistence to his essence. Foundation of all things to his names. Illumination to his essence. So based on this analysis every virtue on the human plane, can be considered to be the reflection of a divine attribute. These attributes are denoted as a divine 'Name' - the ⟪ar:اسماء⟫. This is why in the ⟪ar:احسانی⟫tradition or Sufism, we find the the fundamental theme expressed in the saying: 'Assume the character traits of Allah" ⟪ar:تَخَلّقُو بأخلاق اللہ⟫ .

So you can see that in Maulana Ali's perspective, the relationship between human virtue and divine reality is altogether indispensable. According to his teachings virtue does two things
1. It attracts the corresponding divine quality
2. It integrates the qualities of the divine nature within the soul - which is the very definition of ⟪ar:توحید⟫.

Virtue is the source of ⟪ar:توحید⟫and is in perfect alignment with the oneness of Allah

# Humility

So when we say ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ وحدہ لا شریک لہ⟫, There is no god except Allah, he is one and without partners, within the context of this discourse, if we think of the word ⟪ar:الہ⟫as a variable, and assign it all the positive divine qualities, we can read ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ وحدہ لا شریک لہ⟫ as - There is no positive quality, both in essence or in its manifestation, except that it pertains to Allah, the One who 'has no partner' - ⟪ar:لا شریک لہ⟫.  This means "no partner" in theological terms, and 'the one who has no second' in metaphysical terms.

There is a verse in the Quran in ⟪ar:سورۃ الانفعال⟫ when the believers were forced into battle and after they killed disbelievers, some felt an arrogance about their strength and might - that they killed so many - they probably started comparing numbers of how many disbelievers they killed. At that point this verse was revealed:

Soils of War [8:17]
[ سورۃ ۸] الانفعال

‏**فَلَمْ تَقْتُلُوهُمْ وَلَكِنَّ ٱللَّهَ قَتَلَهُمْ ۚ وَمَا رَمَيْتَ إِذْ رَمَيْتَ وَلَكِنَّ ٱللَّهَ رَمَىٰ** ۚ وَلِيُبْلِىَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ مِنْهُ بَلَآءً حَسَنًا ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌۭ ‎۱۷
And you did not kill them, but it was Allah who killed them. And you did not stone them, [O Muhammad], when you stoned them, but it was Allah who stoned them. He might test the believers with a beautiful test. Indeed, Allah is Hearing and Knowing.

So what we're learning here is that not only must we be effaced before Allah in order to be truly virtuous, but we must also embody, and participate in the very qualities that we wish Allah to manifest towards us. So if we want Allah to be merciful with us, we must be merciful with other. If we want Allah to be generous with us, we must be generous towards others. If we want Allah to love us, we must love others. And our efforts to do so will be empowered to the degree that we comprehend these divine attributes.

Now at this point, it is very important to note the relationship between intellect and humility. There is a direct relationship between the two. Our intellectual perception of the divine attributes results in humility. So when we compare our mercy to the mercy of Allah; our generosity to the generosity of Allah; our love to the love of Allah; our dominion over possessions to the vastness of Allah's dominion -  the intellect forces us to humble ourselves and submit - which in turn, leads to the effacement of our soul. And this effacement, then displaces our attention from our own ego and directs it

to the transcendent reality, as the source of all virtue and truth. So that concept of dominion changes from I have dominion, to no one has dominion except Allah. You understand? ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫ - I have no dominion, except that Allah has complete dominion. ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫I have no generosity, when compared to the generosity of Allah. ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫I have no power when compared to the power of Allah. ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫I have no strength when compared to the strength of Allah. ⟪ar:لا الہ الا اللہ⟫I have no mercy when compared to the mercy of Allah.

Now if you look at the Quranic story of ⟪ar:یوسف⟫or Joseph, in the context of his statement that the 'soul that commands to evil', it helps us understand why Maulana Ali advised ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫ using these same words. He wanted this particular concept implanted in his mind, and by extension, our minds.

So in the story of ⟪ar:یوسف⟫, the king's wife ⟪ar:زلیخۃ⟫tries to seduce him. She locks the door and approaches him with evil intent. ⟪ar:یوسف⟫even though tempted, fights his base desires and tries to escape. ⟪ar:زلیخۃ⟫grabs his shirt from behind as he tries to escape and rips it in the process. When confronted with the king, pretty much like every Bollywood movie I've seen, she shouts "rape". One of the advisors says to the king, examine his shirt. If it is torn from the front, then ⟪ar:یوسف⟫is guilty of the crime, but if it's torn from behind, then he is innocent. Sure enough, the shirt was found ripped from behind and ⟪ar:یوسف⟫is acquitted of the allegation and ⟪ar:زلیخۃ⟫is found to be the guilty party.

But when you read this Quranic story, what's interesting, is that **after**he is proven innocent, and acquitted in the presence of the king. He says

Joseph [12:53]
[ سورۃ ۱۲] یوسف

‏ وَمَآ أُبَرِّئُ نَفْسِىٓ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلنَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌۢ بِٱلسُّوٓءِ إِلَّا مَا رَحِمَ رَبِّىٓ ۚ إِنَّ رَبِّى غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ ‎۵۳
And I do not acquit myself. Indeed, the soul commands to evil, except when my Lord has mercy. Indeed, my Lord is Forgiving and Merciful.

So Allah makes it clear though this verse that when he was subjected to the seductive temptations of ⟪ar:زلیخۃ⟫his successful resistance to her devious strategy was not because of ⟪ar:یوسف⟫own moral resources, but by the grace of his master.

This is why Maulana Ali stressed on this statement to ⟪ar:مالک⟫. He wanted to highlight to him the helplessness of human beings in the face of attractive temptations, when then result in immoral gratifications. This is why even when the king acquitted ⟪ar:یوسف علیہ السلام⟫ of the crime, he did not acquit himself. He admitted that he would have been helpless in the face of that temptation, if his master had not intervened and helped him out.
وَمَآ أُبَرِّئُ نَفْسِىٓ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلنَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌۢ بِٱلسُّوٓءِ إِلَّا مَا رَحِمَ رَبِّىٓ

I do not acquit myself. For Indeed the soul commands to evil, unless Allah has mercy

{{IMG_URL:Resources/IMAGES/201/721d807d-dbe2-4faa-a828-f31ed85d798b.jpg}}

In other words, the soul that is tempted to immoral attractions, is the soul that is not yet at peace. It has not reached the state of ⟪ar:نفس المطمئنۃ⟫. There is an essential contrast highlighted in this verse. If we break this verse in two parts:  ⟪ar:إِنَّ ٱلنَّفْسَ لَأَمَّارَةٌۢ بِٱلسُّوٓءِ⟫ (indeed the soul commands to evil) and ⟪ar:إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ⟫ (except when Allah has mercy), we learn that the default nature of the soul is to be inclined towards its own base desires. To be tempted towards negative and immoral possibilities within this fleeting world of appearances. Whereas the second part  ⟪ar:إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ⟫ (except when Allah has mercy) represents the higher orientation towards the grace of Allah, that results in guiding the soul back towards the pure positive nature of the divine reality.

Can you see this acute contrast in this powerful Quranic verse? It solidifies our awareness of the soul's absolute and unconditional need of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in all circumstances. It is therefore the most fitting way to begin a letter of instruction to someone who is being vested with authority over a vast and wealthy country - where temptations are going to be in abundance; and they are going to lie in wait. This statement to ⟪ar:مالک⟫ stands as a solemn reminder of the soul's  susceptibility to temptations on all physical, and spiritual planes of existence. Temptations that can only be neutralized and overcome - not by the soul itself - but only by the mercy and grace of Allah. This is why:

Following the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫is in fact liberation of the soul.

Going back to words of ⟪ar:یوسف علیہ السلام⟫ that the soul commands to evil unless Allah has mercy, implies two things. It implies an active discernment on the vision of Allah, and then a resolute self-control as its moral consequence.  Intention focused towards Allah, and then self-control in executing that intention. But more importantly, the understanding that our effort to avoid sin expresses a kind of grace that is already bestowed, already present within the conscious soul. So this idea takes us deeper into this concept - that it's not that Allah's grace and mercy only happens when there is no sin; when we are absolutely sin-free. No! His grace and mercy is already present in the fact that we have this desire to avoid sin. The fact that we make an effort to move away from sin is also from his grace and mercy. The very capacity of the human will to strive for virtue, is in itself, a pre-existent 'mercy' embedded within the spiritual constitution of our ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫. But this grace; this gift of not wanting to sin; must be actualized, through a sincere effort to make use of this God-given power to strive for virtue. The more effort we make towards virtue, the more we will activate our powers to avoid sin.

So this is why Maulana Ali reminds ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫ of the ever-present temptation on the part of the ruler, to abuse his powers and perpetrate injustice. So he advises him to be mindful of that.

Now the question is how?! How can the holder of any political office, or any position of power, maintain fidelity to these rules? Especially when confronted with the temptations offered by power. A person can frame laws for punishing corruption, but what about the will, or the capacity to remain incorruptible in the face of the many temptations offered by authority?! That's the key here, right? It's one thing to not sin out of fear of punishment, but it's a whole different ball-game to avoid sin because it's essentially wrong. This is why Maulana Ali's exhortations take on such a negative quality in the very early part of his letter. For example, in the command to 'break the soul in the face of passionate desires', to 'dominate your inclination', to 'withhold yourself from that which is not lawful for you'. The perspective that Maulana Ali is imparting to ⟪ar:مالک⟫, or any person in position of power,  is that living according to correct principles must not take the form of just external compliance. Instead, it should be based on the moral commitment of one's inner being.

In other words, the principles of justice must be integrated, and aligned with the nature of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). It should not just be a matter of doing what's right - which is how we generally look at things. So there is a shift of consciousness here - from **doing what is right**to **being right**, or from **action**to **contemplation**. Because if this shift does not take place, the action will be weak and undetermined. It is only our orientation towards the Real, strengthens what we do in the realm of action. In other words, the formula derived from this analysis is that: **One does what is right to the degree that one is aligned with the Real**. This process of realization is expressed in the Islamic spiritual tradition as ⟪ar:تحقیق⟫, 'making real'. Our ethical conduct is 'made real' through spirituality; and our spirituality is solidified through the practice of virtue.

## Summary

So the take away from today's session is that power corrupts - any this is any applicable to any and all kinds of positions of power. Leaders, rulers, managers, parents. A position of power will always have temptations. This is why ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫creates a boundary for our soul, and this is why Allah made it obligatory on us - not to torture us, or to enslave us - but rather to free us, to liberate our souls of our base inclinations. So the first warning that Maulana Ali gave ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫ was through the reminder that
فَإِنَّ النَّفْسَ أَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ، إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ

For indeed the heart commands to evil until Allah has mercy on it. The natural inclination of the soul is to be tempted towards its base inclinations. So our efforts must be geared towards restoring our primordial state of ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫which is the true substance of every human soul. It is the buried treasure within our intellects, that prophets were sent to extract through the tools of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫. So people ask, what's the purpose of religion, what's the purpose of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫? This is the purpose! To restore our souls to their full potential. To align it with the divine nature, but learning about him. Because the more we know him, the more we recognize his vastness, superiority and glory, the more humble we will become. The more humility we engender, the more we will be able to conquer our desires, and efface it in his contemplation. The more we efface our ego, the more we will liberate ourselves from our base inclinations. The more we will disengage and disarm the concupiscent soul. And the means or the resource to accomplish this goal is by following the ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫.

<!-- section 16 (id=204, raw_sort=16): Subtle Polytheism -->

## Subtle Polytheism

In the previous session we looked at the virtue of humility and the importance of its role in our spiritual development. We looked at it in the context of the verse ⟪ar:فَإِنَّ النَّفْسَ أَمَّارَةٌ بِالسُّوءِ، إِلاَّ مَا رَحِمَ اللهُ⟫. The soul, or the ego commands to evil until the mercy of Allah accompanies the slave. We learnt that without the mercy of Allah, we are helpless abased creatures. So the only way to subdue our concupiscent soul our⟪ar:نفس الامارۃ⟫is through tapping into the buried treasures of our intellect. By activating our فترۃ and aligning it with the divine nature of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

So we looked at the virtue of humility in the previous session, today we'll look at the vice of pride and arrogance.

# Pride and Arrogance

Maulana Ali then issues the stern warning,
وَلاَ تَنْصِبَنَّ نَفْسَكَ لِحَرْبِ اللهِ

Do not set your soul up for war with Allah. What does this means? What kind of actions put the soul at war with Allah? The obvious would simply be the disobedience to Allah; or violating his religious obligations. But it also includes more subtle forms of opposition, like the attitudes that accompany outward acts of obedience, but contradict the spirit of those actions.

Take pride or ⟪ar:تکبر⟫for instance. Maulana Ali repeatedly warns ⟪ar:مالک⟫against pride. Why? Because the ruler's pride is a misappropriation of a divine attribute. Allah says:
قَالَ اللَّهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ: الْكِبْرِيَاءُ رِدَائِي، وَالْعَظَمَةُ إِزَارِي، فَمَنْ نَازَعَنِي وَاحِدًا مِنْهُمَا، قَذَفْتُهُ فِي النَّارِ

Pride is my cloak and greatness is my robe. and he who competes with me in of either of them, I shall cast him into Hell-fire

What is ⟪ar:کبر⟫?  It is a a psychic state in which a person feels a sense of superiority and behaves highhandedly with others. Its signs manifest within his actions, and it's symptoms are clearly noticeable by others. ⟪ar:کبر⟫ is not something anyone can hide. There's another word called ⟪ar:عُجُب⟫, which means to be impressed with the self. Now what's the difference between the two?

The difference is that ⟪ar:کبر⟫always require two people You have the one acting out his pride (the ⟪ar:متکبر⟫), and the the one observing his act of pride. A ⟪ar:مُعَجِّب⟫does not need a second person. He is is simply impressed with himself. The classical definition of ⟪ar:عُجُب⟫ is to exalt the blessing, and forget the source of that blessing. For example, today when someone says “You are beautiful”, we say “Thank you” as if we had anything to do with it, right? if Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) made someone beautiful, talented, then they should praise Allah, and not respond to the complement as if they made themselves beautiful. People say, Man! I worked so hard and I finally made it happen!” People believe their accomplishment are by their own efforts, completely excluding or denying Allah's assistance in it. That's ⟪ar:عجب⟫! Now ⟪ar:عجب⟫will inevitably lead to pride. Scholars say that ⟪ar:عجب⟫ is the seed, and ⟪ar:کبر⟫ is the fruit.

So the ⟪ar:متکبر⟫, is the one who snatches the cloak of pride and robe of greatness, that belongs exclusively to Allah. Allah alone has the right to the quality of ⟪ar:تکبر⟫, and He alone can be called, without insult, ⟪ar:المتکبر⟫ - 'The Proud'. Cause if you say to me, "Asif, you are very proud!", I'm not going to say, "Thank you!", right? It's an insult. But when I say Allah is the proud. He is المتکبر, I'm not insulting him, I'm glorifying him. Why? Because I'm putting pride where it belongs, you see?

So acting proudly with Allah's creation is tantamount to inwardly opposing the authority of Allah - and is therefore, an implicit denial of his unique and incomparable majesty. Now when someone who is the subject of his kingdom, openly denies and stands in opposition to the king, what is he essentially doing? He is waging war with the king, isn't he? So the denial of an attribute of Allah's sovereignty, can in turn, be seen as a kind of 'war' with him. In other words, any kind of pride, or act of arrogance or oppression on the part of a ruler towards those beneath him, stems from his enmity with the transcendent reality. He is misappropriating the divine attributes of Allah and applying it to himself. When he does that, the natural consequence is pride, arrogance, oppression and tyranny. They just naturally follow in his actions. You can clearly see this in the heads of state today. Look how these people in power behave today! Look how the leaders of a community sometimes behave! Look at how we behave today with those we have authority over. Any oppression that comes from us is directly related to our misappropriating a divine right! Taking on an attribute that belongs exclusively to Allah. This is why we have Muslims who pray 5 times a day, fast their entire lives, and yet give Islam such a bad name.

The paradox here, is that an attitude of superiority towards those who are "below"  constitutes an attitude of rebellion towards the one who is "above" us. Therefore an kind of injustice towards creation, not only brings the justice of Allah upon its perpetrator, it also manifests as an outright opposition to the Allah himself. So every vice, is in reality, comes from a spiritual disorder. The outward immorality of corruption is just the symptom of an inner spiritual sickness. What is that sickness? It is the sickness of an implicit denial of the absoluteness and the uniqueness of the one who is the source of all power and authority. We forget the question that Allah asks in the Quran:

The Believer [40:16]
[ سورۃ ۴۰] غافر

لِّمَنِ ٱلْمُلْكُ ٱلْيَوْم لِلَّهِ ٱلْوَحِدِ ٱلْقَهَّارِ ‎۱۶
To whom belongs the kingdom on this Day? It belongs to Allah - the One, the Irresistible!

So according to Maulana Ali, the truth expressed by this verse, should constantly resound in the ears of all rulers. The word ⟪ar:الیوم⟫in this verse, 'this day'. The verse is talking about the day of judgment. Allah will ask on the day of judgment, Who does all dominion belong to on this day?! But based on Maulana Ali's advise, this is meant to be understood as "every single day" of our worldly life, and not just the Day of Judgement.  So Maulana Ali is commanding ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫to ask himself this question daily. ⟪ar:لِّمَنِ ٱلْمُلْكُ ٱلْيَوْم⟫Who does this day belong to?  ⟪ar:لِلَّهِ ٱلْوَحِدِ ٱلْقَهَّارِ⟫To Allah the unavoidable. By extension, he is also commanding us to ask ourselves this same question every day: ⟪ar:لِّمَنِ ٱلْمُلْكُ ٱلْيَوْم⟫ Who does dominion belong to on this day?  Does it belong to our ⟪ar:نفس⟫? Our ego? Or does it belong to Allah?! ⟪ar:لِلَّهِ ٱلْوَحِدِ ٱلْقَهَّارِ⟫ - To Allah the unavoidable one. And if the answer in our hearts truly is that it belongs to Allah, then we should act like it. We should manifest this belief through our speech and actions.

# Covert Polytheism

In Maulana Ali's sermon known as ⟪ar:القاسیۃ⟫which means 'the Abasement', he explains the humiliation of the proud. He describes ⟪ar:ابلیس⟫as being the first to "compete" with Allah in grandeur. He therefore became the embodiment of the principles of pride and arrogance. When ordered to prostrate before Adam, Iblis refused:

Sad [38:71-74]
[ سورۃ ۳۸] ص

‏إِذْ قَالَ رَبُّكَ لِلْمَلَٓئِكَةِ إِنِّى خَلِقٌۢ بَشَرًۭا مِّن طِينٍۢ ‎۷۱‏فَإِذَا سَوَّيْتُهُۥ وَنَفَخْتُ فِيهِ مِن رُّوحِى فَقَعُوا۟ لَهُ سَجِدِينَ ‎۷۲‏فَسَجَدَ ٱلْمَلَٓئِكَةُ كُلُّهُمْ أَجْمَعُونَ ‎۷۳‏إِلَّآ إِبْلِيسَ ٱسْتَكْبَرَ وَكَانَ مِنَ ٱلْكَفِرِينَ ‎۷۴
( 71 ) when your master said to the angels: "Truly, I am going to create man from clay". ( 72 ) So when I have fashioned him and breathed into him my soul, then you must fall down and prostrate to him." ( 73 ) So the angels prostrated themselves, all of them: ( 74 ) Except Iblis. He was proud and was one of the disbelievers. 

Why did Iblis refuse to prostrate? He refused out of his 'vehement passion' that overcame him. He deemed himself superior to Adam based on the fact that he was made of fire, whereas Adam was made of clay ⟪ar:خلقتنی من نار و خلقتہ من طین⟫. He took pride in his presumed superiority, and consequently rebelling against Allah's command. Iblis is not only considered the first "enemy of Allah", he is also seen as the leader of all bigots, and the forerunner of the proud (⟪ar:امام المُتَعَصِّبِین و سلف المستکبرین⟫).

Maulana Ali warns his listeners: 'Beware! Lest he infects you with his disease through his insinuations, whispering and machinations. He urges them: (and listen to these words carefully). He says: Be resolute in placing self-abasement over your heads, casting self-glorification beneath your feet, and removing pride from your necks. And take up humility as the fortified watchtower between you and your enemy.'

Such profound words! Let's just take a moment to reflect on the beauty of this statement. Be resolute in placing self-abasement over your heads? Why over the head? Because of the mind and intellect. That's where arrogance and pride is generated. Cast self-glorification beneath your feet - trample your ego. And remove pride from your necks? Why? The neck becomes stiff with pride right? Take up humility as the fortified watchtower between yourself and your enemy. What is a watchtower? It is a tower built to create an elevated point of observation, right? From where you can see everything clearly. So examine every situation with humility. Otherwise ⟪ar:شیطان⟫will sneak in.

So his point is that humility, is the principal weapon in the war against Iblis. His ultimate goal is to inculcate pride within our hearts; because pride is not only the worst of vices, but also the poison, that infects and destroys all virtue. When pride enters the heart, all virtues manifest as vice. How scary is that?

So for instance. when you do something virtuous, like if you're being generous and feeding an entire community, but as you're doing it you're thinking, Wow! Look at me! I'm so much better than these filthy insects! So even though what you're doing is an act of virtue, that pride and arrogance in your heart turns it into a vice. Maulana Ali says, 'The sin that grieves you is better in the sight of Allah, than the virtue that makes you proud.

Also, while we're in our watchtower of humility, we should be mindful about the dangers of excessive praise and conceit. Again, it is another great opportunity for ⟪ar:شیطان⟫to completely efface and eradicate our virtues. It was these two vices - Pride and conceit, resulting in the disobedience of Allah, that led to the disgrace of Iblis. And this is extremely subtle. Even right now, as I read this, I might think this doesn't apply to me; and that arrogance in itself is a subtle ⟪ar:شرک⟫. The moment I think I'm better than that! ⟪ar:خلاص⟫That's it! ⟪ar:شیطان⟫just sneaked in. The point here is that these vices are that subtle. It is this subtle form of ⟪ar:شرک⟫that the prophet referred to as
الشِّرْكُ فِي هَذِهِ الْأُمَّةِ أَخْفَى مِنْ دَبِيبِ النَّمْلِ علی حَجَرِ الأَسوَد فِی لِیلَۃِ الظُّلْما

The creeping of shirk in my community is more hidden than the creeping of a black ant on a black stone in a dark night. It is that subtle. This is what is called ⟪ar:شرک الخفی⟫. You have ⟪ar:شرک الجلی⟫which is open and manifest ⟪ar:شرک⟫and then you have ⟪ar:شرک الخفی⟫ which is hidden and subtle ⟪ar:شرک⟫.

So ⟪ar:شرک الجلی⟫manifest as the worship of material idols; whereas ⟪ar:شرک الخفی⟫ , manifests as the worship of false gods hidden within one's own heart; within ones intentions; within ones attitudes; within ones orientations and inclinations; within the way we talk; the way we walk; what we wear; the ways in which we show-off. It includes all elements of hidden pride, vanity, ostentation, pretensions. Maulana Ali says 'Know that the slightest pretension ⟪ar:ریا⟫ is polytheism .'  You know when you call someone, and tell them how awesome you are, how kind you are, how much you do for your family or others - without saying it in those words? That's ⟪ar:ریا⟫- that's pretensions. Maulana Ali says, the even the slightest pretension is ⟪ar:شرک⟫. Why? Think about why you're doing that? You want to to be liked. You want to to appreciated. You want to be talked about in family circles. What's wrong with that? What's wrong is, that you ant that fondness, that likeness, that praise from creation, and not from the creator. That's what's wrong with it! When we act for the sake of earning the praise of others, it is an act for them and not for Allah. That creates an implicit attribution of partners to Allah. You understand?

If I'm being generous to earn your praise, that act of generosity is no longer for Allah, it is for you. In the process I have partnered you with Allah. and as Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) said: Whoever acts for other than Allah, Allah entrusts him to those he acted for on the day of judgment. On that day he will say, go get your rewards from them - not me! Because clearly your actions was for them!

So ⟪ar:عجب⟫and ⟪ar:ریا⟫will inevitably lead to disobedience of Allah just like Iblis. His disobedience was an outward moral consequence, of an inner spiritual sickness which came out of pride. Pride which now we understand is tantamount to 'hidden polytheism', and which brings in its wake, disgrace and humiliation. Iblis was disgraced because he disobeyed Allah; and he disobeyed Allah on account of his pride. So what the author is stating, is that to be proud, is in and of itself, a disgrace. It is a state of "dis" "grace". A distancing, a departure from Allah's grace. Pride is a perversion of the human state. Because the true human state should be humility. Maulana Ali asks in that sermon. "Do you not see how Allah humiliated him through his pride?". So being proud (whether manifest or subtle) is in reality already a form of humiliation. Just as we learnt earlier that wanting to move away from sin, is in and of itself a divine mercy. Similarly عجب and ریا and کبر are in and of itself a humiliation. Because self-aggrandizement will inevitably result in self-destruction.

So now returning to Maulana Ali's letter, he succinctly expresses this notion, that the pride of a ruler is set in motion, the moment he steps on this slippery slope of power, that very easily can lead towards oppression. And so he says:
وَلاَ تَقُولَنَّ: إِنِّي مُؤَمَّرٌ! آمُرُ! فَأُطَاعُ! إِنَّ ذلِكَ إِدْغَالٌ فِي الْقَلْبِ، وَمَنْهَكَةٌ لِلدِّينِ 

Do not say, "I have been given authority, I should be obeyed when I command, for this leads to corruption in the heart and the erosion of religion.

Maulana Ali is pointing out to ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫ that this self-glorification leads first to an inner, spiritual corruption—which is the corruption of the heart —and then to the destruction of religion itself. This order of priority, is highlighting the fact that the outward prescriptions, or the outward form of religion requires the correct inner attitude. Actions and intentions must be integrated, and then maintained with stability.

The author then restates Maulana Ali's idea by saying: When power goes to the head, faith departs from the heart. Conversely, for faith to properly manifest in the world, it must flow from a heart saturated with humility. This is another aspect of 'putting every thing in its proper place'.
وضع کل شئی حق محلہ

This is justice! Putting humility where it belongs - in the heart, and putting self-glorification where it belongs - beneath the feet; and placing pride where it belongs - with Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

Then as the letter continues he says:
وَإِذَا أَحْدَثَ لَكَ مَا أَنْتَ فِيهِ مِنْ سُلْطَانِكَ أُبَّهَةً أَوْ مَخِيلَةً، فَانْظُرْ إِلَى عِظَمِ مُلْكِ اللهِ فَوْقَكَ، فَإِنَّ ذلِكَ يُطَامِنُ إِلَيْكَ مِنْ طِمَاحِكَ، وَيَكُفُّ عَنْكَ مِنْ غَرْبِكَ،يَفِيءُ إِلَيْكَ بِمَا عَزَبَ عَنْكَ مِنْ عَقْلِكَ

Maulana Ali continues: 'If the authority in which you are placed produces pride or vanity in your heart, then reflect on the greatness of Allah's realm over you . This will curb your haughtiness, cure you of your high temper, and restore to you what had strayed from your intellect.

I want you to reflect on the relationship between intellect and humility that is being expressed here. The part of the intellect that 'strays' when vanity and arrogance enters the heart, is precisely the part that is conscious of **not only**the absolute reality of Allah, but also the relative, and ultimately illusory nature of everything else. What's interesting is that Maulana Ali uses the word ⟪ar:مَخِيلَةً⟫ for arrogance', which is derived from the same root as the word for imagination, ⟪ar:خیال⟫. Why? Because arrogance is intimately connected with the realm of the imagination, with the fanciful; with the realm of illusion and with everything that is unreal.

Therefore arrogance and pride, are an intense form of self-delusion, stemming from an intellectual defect. The intellectual defect, that has put things in the wrong place. Arrogance is born when we focus on the wrong things, give importance to the wrong things, glorify the wrong things, worship the wrong things. Arrogance is ultimately and intimately connected with the unreal.  When ⟪ar:خیال⟫gets attached to the unreal, it gets infused with pride, and it turns into ⟪ar:مخیلۃ⟫- arrogance. This arrogance then manifests itself as a vice in action.

What we're learning here, is that the root cause of vice goes much deeper than the level of morality. It involves not just an over-estimation of the self, it fundamentally involves an under-estimation of the Absolute. So when you see a tyrant manifesting his overt egotism, you are essentially seeing someone who has not realized the truth of Allah's sole reality; and he's engaged in the ultimate deception, and the illusion of everything else.

# Pride belongs only to Allah

Only the truly wise, assimilate the true meaning of this reality that the Quran expresses in the Quran:

The Story [28:88]
[ سورۃ ۲۸] القصص

‏وَلَا تَدْعُ مَعَ ٱللَّهِ إِلَهًا آخَرَ ۘ لَآ إِلَهَ إِلَّا هُوَ ۚ **كُلُّ شَىْءٍ هَالِكٌ إِلَّا وَجْهَهُ**ۥ ۚ لَهُ ٱلْحُكْمُ وَإِلَيْهِ تُرْجَعُونَ ‎۸۸
And do not invoke with Allah another deity. There is no deity except Him. Everything will be destroyed except His Face. His is the judgement, and to Him you will be returned.

The Beneficent [55:26-27]
[ سورۃ ۵۵] الرحمن

‏كُلُّ مَنْ عَلَيْهَا فَانٍۢ ‎۲۶
Everyone upon the earth will perish,

‏وَيَبْقَىٰ وَجْهُ رَبِّكَ ذُو ٱلْجَلَلِ وَٱلْإِكْرَامِ ‎۲۷
And there will remain the Face of your Lord, Owner of Majesty and Honor.

The Children of Israel [17:81]
[ سورۃ ۱۷] الاسرا

‏وَقُلْ جَآءَ ٱلْحَقُّ وَزَهَقَ ٱلْبَطِلُ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلْبَطِلَ كَانَ زَهُوقًۭا ‎۸۱
And say, "Truth has come, and falsehood has departed. Indeed is falsehood, [by nature], ever bound to depart."

The author emphasizes that only the **truly wise**can understand this truth - that it is humility that dissolves all egocentricity, which is the root of all pride, vanity and arrogance, which then serves as the life-blood of all tyranny and oppression.

We can now better appreciate Maulana Ali's warning, as he writes in his letter:
إِيَّاكَ وَمُسَامَةَ اللهِ فِي عَظَمَتِهِ، وَالتَّشَبُّهَ بِهِ فِي جَبَرُوتِهِ، فَإِنَّ اللهَ يُذِلُّ كُلَّ جَبَّار، وَيُهِينُ كُلَّ مُخْتَال

Beware of comparing yourself to Allah in His greatness or likening yourself to Him in His power, for Allah humiliates every claimant of power and disgraces every one who brags.

Then he says:
أَنْصِفِ اللهَ وَأَنْصِفِ النَّاسَ مِنْ نَفْسِكَ، وَمِنْ خَاصَّةِ أَهْلِكَ، وَمَنْ لَكَ فِيهِ هَوىً مِنْ رَعِيَّتِكَ، فَإِنَّكَ إِلاَّ تَفْعَلْ تَظْلِمْ، وَمَنْ ظَلَمَ عِبَادَ اللهِ كَانَ اللهُ خَصْمَهُ دُونَ عِبَادِهِ، وَمَنْ خَاصَمَهُ اللهُ أَدْحَضَ حُجَّتَهُ، وَكَانَ لله حَرْباً حَتَّى يَنْزعَ وَيَتُوبَ

Do justice for Allah and do justice towards the people. Do justice against yourself, your loved ones, and those among your subjects for whom you have a liking, because if you do not do so you will become oppressive. And when a person oppresses the creatures of Allah then, Allah himself becomes his opponent; and when Allah is the opponent of a person, he tramples his plea. Such a person will remain at war with Allah, until he gives it up and repents.

So the tyrant will eventually be forced to repent. Maybe not during his lifetime, but certainly at the moment of his death. Even if that tyrant has successfully evaded the consequences of his actions in his lifetime, he cannot escape its consequences in the hereafter.

## Summary

Our take away from today's session is that we need to be mindful in the various ways we commit ⟪ar:شرک الخفی⟫ - the subtle ways in which we assign partners with Allah. Because that will inevitably lead to pride and arrogance. We have to begin and end each day asking us the Quranic question:
⟪ar:لِّمَنِ ٱلْمُلْكُ ٱلْيَوْم⟫Who does dominion belong to on this day? Who does my today belong to? And the answer that we must manifest through our actions is ⟪ar:لَّهِ ٱلْوَحِدِ ٱلْقَهَّارِ⟫.  It belongs to Allah, the one, the unavoidable. And so if this day does not belong to me, then I cannot do whatever I want to do with it. I must use it as Allah wants me to use it; and that can only happen through humility. The way we are elevated in this ⟪ar:دین⟫is through humility - through knowledge of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) with humility - not ignorance with humility! With knowledge with humility. Cause you should just naturally be humble if you're ignorant. The danger is when you actually know something. Then you have the danger of being arrogant. So Knowledge of Allah with humility is our key to unlocking the buried treasures of ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫and achieving the desired state of ⟪ar:نفس المطمئنۃ⟫- the satisfied or content soul.

<!-- section 17 (id=205, raw_sort=17): Waging War Against Allah -->

## Waging War Against Allah

Last week we looked at the virtue of humility and the vice of arrogance. Maulana Ali describes humility as the foundation of all virtues and arrogance and pride as the foundation of all vices. So he issues a stern warning to Malik saying
وَلاَ تَنْصِبَنَّ نَفْسَكَ لِحَرْبِ اللهِ

Do not set yourself up for war with Allah. War, not just in the sense of an outward disobedience, but also in the sense of an inner resistance or an inner repulsion to the commands of Allah. He says the arrogant will ultimately turns into a tyrant. He will either oppress his own soul, or he will will oppress others. He will constantly remains in this state of war with Allah until he repents.

Now the author explains the nature of the tyrant's war. How does such a person wage war? He does so through his implicit opposition to the nature of the ultimate reality. Because such a person is violating the true nature of his being - his true ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫. This war manifests in several forms

# Persistence Of Tyranny

One form of this war manifests itself in the persistence of tyranny on the part of the tyrant. The longer his oppression continues, the more wretched his soul becomes; and the more firmly the seeds of misery get planted within his being. This is the Quranic concept of ⟪ar:استدراج⟫- the drawing out of something in degrees. The word ⟪ar:استدراج⟫comes from ⟪ar:درج⟫which means stages. Even in Urdu we have درجہ بدرجہ meaning step by step. Allah uses this word in ⟪ar:سورۃ الاعراف⟫ when referring to punishment by degrees.

The Heights [7:181-182]
[ سورۃ ۷] الاعراف

‏وَمِمَّنْ خَلَقْنَآ أُمَّةٌۭ يَهْدُونَ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَبِهِ يَعْدِلُونَ ‎۱۸۱‏وَٱلَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَتِنَا **سَنَسْتَدْرِجُهُم** مِّنْ حَيْثُ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ ‎۱۸۲
Of those We have created are people who guide with truth and dispense justice therewith. But those who reject Our signs, we will lead them on by degrees [to punishment) in ways they do not know. 

Allah uses the word ⟪ar:سَنَسْتَدْرِجُهُم⟫He says, we will lead them on by degrees towards punishment. Now what's scary, is that this punishment that Allah carries out "by degrees" can take also the form of an apparent success in worldly terms. Quran clearly states:

Repentance [9:85]
[ سورۃ ۹] التوبۃ

‏وَلَا تُعْجِبْكَ أَمْوَلُهُمْ وَأَوْلَدُهُمْ ۚ إِنَّمَا يُرِيدُ ٱللَّهُ أَن يُعَذِّبَهُم بِهَا فِى ٱلدُّنْيَا وَتَزْهَقَ أَنفُسُهُمْ وَهُمْ كَفِرُونَ ‎۸۵
And let not their wealth or their children amaze you. Allah's Plan is to punish them with these things in this world, and that their souls shall depart while they are disbelievers.

There are times we look around us, and we see these people in power oppressing the world, amassing millions at the expense of starving children; enjoying all the comforts of life while the rest suffer - and we wonder - how is this fair? Why aren't these tyrants being punished by Allah? Quran answers that by saying that they are in fact **in**a state of punishment. The intent of Allah is to punish them through their wealth. Because the more they gather, the more they amass through tyranny and oppression, the more potential punishment they accumulate - ⟪ar:سَنَسْتَدْرِجُهُم⟫. The more they gather, the more they add to their misery. On the day of judgment these people will wish they never had this power; they will wish they never had this money; because the more they gathered, the deeper they dug themselves into hellfire. Now they have to account for every single action.

This is something we can witness all around us! Look at these movie stars, pop stars - what do they end up using their money for? Alcohol, drugs, sex, non-stop partying. The more they indulge, the more miserable they become; and then they end up committing suicide. What greater oppression is there for the ⟪ar:نفس⟫?

So the first consequence of being at war with Allah, is this persistence in tyranny; being unable to stop. Increasing in their hunger and thirst of this world in degrees; and as a consequence, proportionally earning the punishments of the hereafter.

# War with Conscience

Another form of being at war with Allah is through the inner fight with one's conscience - what we call ⟪ar:ضمیر⟫in Urdu. Fighting the voice of conscience that creates the gift of guilt. What is conscience? The author beautifully defines it at the **divine infusion of spiritual discernment within the soul**. The **divine inspiration** that serves as the basis of all ethical discrimination. Allah says in ⟪ar:سورۃ الشمس⟫:

The Sun [91:7-8]
[ سورۃ ۹۱] الشمس

‏وَنَفْسٍۢ وَمَا سَوَّىٰهَا ‎۷‏فَأَلْهَمَهَا فُجُورَهَا وَتَقْوَىٰهَا ‎۸
( 7 ) I swear by the soul and and by Him Who perfected him in proportion; ( 8 ) Then He inspired him with what is wicked and what is righteous

Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) has provided this internal mechanism of correction within every human being in the form of his conscience. There are two words you might have heard - ⟪ar:فسق⟫and ⟪ar:فجور⟫; or ⟪ar:فاسق⟫ and ⟪ar:فاجر⟫. The word ⟪ar:فسق⟫literally means ‘to separate’ like Arabs say:
فَسَقَتِ الرُّطْبَةُ عَنْ قِشْرِهَا 

the date got separated from its outer coil

It also means ‘to abandon’ or ‘to withdraw’ like Arabs say:
فَسَقَ الرُّكَّابُ عَنْ قَصْدِ الطَّرِيقِ

The rider withdrew or abandoned his intended path.

In ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫terms it is used for abandoning, withdrawing or crossing the boundaries of Allah in the form of his disobedience. This term usually refers to major sins like stealing, fornication, etc. This is different from the word ⟪ar:معصية⟫which is normally used for the disobedience at a lower level.

The word ⟪ar:الفجور⟫which comes from ⟪ar:فجر⟫means to tear apart. This is why the morning prayer is called all ⟪ar:الفجر⟫because the light of the sun literally tears through the darkness of the night. Also when a dam breaks, and water bursts out, that's called ⟪ar:انفجار⟫. So ⟪ar:الفجور⟫much like ⟪ar:الفسق⟫is a word used for major evil and wickedness. The difference is, that there is still guilt and repentance at the stage of ⟪ar:فسق⟫, but once a person reaches the state of ⟪ar:فجور⟫, there is no more repentance. Not only does he commit the sin openly, he enjoys it; he is proud of it; he propagates it. He organizes rallies to support homosexuality. He doesn't just fornicate, he makes a video of it and uploads it. He doesn't just murder, he tortures the victim and tries to keep him alive as long as possible. This is ⟪ar:الفاجر⟫. He literally tears and rips though the boundaries of ⟪ar:شریعۃ⟫. Like ⟪ar:یزید⟫, like ⟪ar:شمر⟫like ⟪ar:معاویۃ⟫like ⟪ar:ابو جھل⟫.

It's good to know the difference between these terminologies:

The ⟪ar:فاسق⟫is the one who commits major sins, because he is deluded into viewing evil as beautiful. However, this person still has his conscience intact, which generates guilt within him. His inner voice keeps nagging at him.

The ⟪ar:فاجر⟫clearly knows what he's doing, and he's doing it openly. He's not even trying to hide his sin. This is the person who has completely silenced his conscience. He no longer feels guilt; because he no longer sees himself as doing any wrong.

The word ⟪ar:معصیۃ⟫is used for smaller sins that we commit in our day to day lives. The ⟪ar:عاصی⟫is a person using foul language; who gossips, back-bites, makes fun of people, etc.

Then there is another word call ⟪ar:ذنوب⟫which is the overarching term that includes everything. It comes from ⟪ar:ذنب⟫which means tail, and what does the tail cover? The butt - nothing good comes out of the butt. So ⟪ar:ذنب⟫is a term used for all sins; from the smallest sin to the greatest one.

So Allah says: ⟪ar:فَأَلْهَمَهَا فُجُورَهَا وَتَقْوَىٰهَا⟫- We have inspired the soul with the awareness of ⟪ar:فجور⟫and it's counterpart of ⟪ar:تقوی⟫which is mindfulness of God. The two extremes have been mentioned in this verse. Piety or ⟪ar:تقوی⟫being the ultimate virtue, and ⟪ar:فجور⟫being the ultimate vice. Allah says, we have inspired the human soul with both through the gift of his conscience.

Therefore the war against one's conscience, is in fact a war against Allah. This is at the superficial level. At a more deeper level, this 'war against Allah' emerges as a resistance or repulsion against the very reality that defines the soul as such. Because our soul contains this moral compass in the form of our conscience - which is an intrinsic awareness of the difference between good and evil. It is a gift that has been instilled within us by Allah. But it comes from the fact that our soul contains an innate awareness Allah's lordship over us. It subsequently recognizes its need for servitude towards him. Allah describes this consciousness (this spiritual awareness of Allah's lordship) in ⟪ar:سورۃ الاعراف⟫:

The Heights [7:172]
[ سورۃ ۷] الاعراف

‏وَإِذْ أَخَذَ رَبُّكَ مِنۢ بَنِىٓ آدَمَ مِن ظُهُورِهِمْ ذُرِّيَّتَهُمْ وَأَشْهَدَهُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ أَلَسْتُ بِرَبِّكُمْ ۖ قَالُوا۟ بَلَىٰ ۛ شَهِدْنَآ ۛ أَن تَقُولُوا۟ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَمَةِ إِنَّا كُنَّا عَنْ هَذَا غَفِلِينَ ‎۱۷۲
And [mention] when your Lord took from the children of Adam - from their loins - their descendants and made them testify of themselves, [saying to them], "Am I not your Lord?" They said, "Yes, we have testified." [This] - lest you should say on the day of Resurrection, "Indeed, we were of this unaware."

Allah describes the immanence of this spiritual consciousness within each soul in terms which emphasize its pre-existential nature. He describes it in terms of a 'moment' at the very dawn of creation. That as soon as he created us, he made us witness his glory; and then he had us testify. Why? So that we could not claim ignorance on the day of judgment.

We've now learnt, that Allah represents every thing that is perfectly good! Therefore the source of morality (knowing what is right and wrong) transcends human existence in this world. Because Allah made us witness his divine nature. When we witnessed Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) we witnessed and learnt everything that is good. We also learnt its counterparts. Everything departs from Allah's divine nature is evil. So this moment of witnessing was when when the seeds of his divine nature were sown with the soil of our intellects, in the form of our ⟪ar:فطرۃ⟫.

# Fighting the Concupiscent Soul

This is why the person who violates his own nature, is a tyrant, first and foremost to his own soul. Why? Because he is allowing it to be dominated by 'the soul which commands to evil', ⟪ar:النفس الامارۃ بالسوء⟫. This is why Maulana Ali warned ⟪ar:مالک اشتر⟫:
وَلاَ تَقُولَنَّ: إِنِّي مُؤَمَّرٌ آمُرُ فَأُطَاعُ

Do not say: "I have been given authority, I should be obeyed when I command", because the one who thinks and acts in this manner, is on his way to corrupting his soul and becoming and oppressor. Because this kind of commanding, is the nature of the concupiscent soul:  ⟪ar:النفس الامارۃ بالسوء⟫. This is how the concupiscent soul orders us around. I command you to commit this sin, you have to do it! Just Do it! Obey your thirst! The concupiscent soul commands, and its orders are obeyed by the other elements of our soul. Our intelligence, our imagination, our free will, our sentiments, our sensibilities, our emotions, and so on.

This is why Maulana Ali constantly advises us to fight against this soul - ⟪ar:مجاھدۃ النفس⟫. This necessity of struggling with ourselves, and overcoming our faults, these are an essential aspect of justice in the view of Maulana Ali. Because justice according to him, is placing every thing in its proper place. This means rectifying oneself before presuming to reform or rule over others. It means restoring the divine nature within ourselves.

Maulana Ali says, 'If your aspiration ascends to the reformation of people, begin with yourself; for your pursuit of reforming others, while your own soul is corrupt, is the greatest of all faults.

In his letter appointing ⟪ar:محمد بن ابی بکر⟫ who was Malik's predecessor as governor of Egypt, Maulana Ali makes a strong connection between the bestowal of power and the need to oppose one's soul. He writes to him Beware, O ⟪ar:محمد بن ابی بکر⟫, that I have appointed you as governor of the most immense of forces at my disposal - the people of Egypt,! So you are obliged to oppose your soul and protect your religion '

What we must note here is the relationship between the magnitude of power being placed at one's disposal, and the spiritual responsibility resulting from that power. First, it is in relation to one's own soul and its desires, and secondly, in regard to the religion that one must guard. The greater the power at ones disposal, the greater the obligation to control and restrain oneself. So people who are put in charge of communities, and granted authority - they need to really take control of their own souls. Because if such people submit to their own desires, gratifying themselves with the power at their disposal, they will end up corrupting not just their own soul, but also the religion itself which they were charged to uphold. '

The integrity of the outward form of religion, depends on the inward disposition of the soul. Without this integrity, the commanding soul will arrogate to itself the right of autonomy and dominion, as Allah describes in ⟪ar:سورۃ العلق⟫.

The Clot [96:6-7]
[ سورۃ ۹۶] العلق

‏كَلَّآ إِنَّ ٱلْإِنسَنَ لَيَطْغَىٰٓ ‎۶‏أَن رَّآهُ ٱسْتَغْنَىٰٓ ‎۷
Indeed man is rebellious, in that he deems himself independent and self-sufficient

As far as the soul deludes itself to be self-sufficient, and tries to detach itself from its total dependence upon Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), it will sets itself up as an Allah in its own right. The person will actually start believing that he is Allah himself - and therein lies the true description of a tyrant. This is the one person according to the Quran who has taken his own desire as his Allah

Crouching [45:23]
[ سورۃ ۴۵] الجاثیۃ

**‏أَفَرَءَيْتَ مَنِ ٱتَّخَذَ إِلَهَهُۥ هَوَىٰهُ** وَأَضَلَّهُ ٱللَّهُ عَلَىٰ عِلْمٍۢ وَخَتَمَ عَلَىٰ سَمْعِهِۦ وَقَلْبِهِۦ وَجَعَلَ عَلَىٰ بَصَرِهِ غِشَوَةًۭ فَمَن يَهْدِيهِ مِنۢ بَعْدِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ أَفَلَا تَذَكَّرُونَ ‎۲۳
Have you seen him who takes his own desires as his master? , and Allah knowing, left him astray, and sealed his hearing and his heart, and put a cover on his sight. Who then will guide him after Allah? Will you not then remember?

Nothing is more obvious in our times today, than this fact. Just look around you and you'll find these tyrants everywhere. You'll find them in world leaders, you'll find them in community leaders, and you'll find them in households. Every household has one of these tyrants - ⟪ar:إِنِّي مُؤَمَّرٌ آمُرُ فَأُطَاعُ⟫ - I have been given authority, I should be obeyed when I command. The real question to be asked here is - Am I this person? Are you this person? And if the honest-to-God answer is "Yes", then what can I do to humble myself? The only thing required to fix this, is that I truly need to recognize my own nothingness and insignificance before Allah. Consequently, I also need to recognize my unconditional need of Allah in my life. That is the only way I can loosen the grip of those aspects of my soul that commands me to evil. Because only that soul that already commands itself to evil, will command others to evil. Similarly, only the soul which is humble before Allah will be humble, compassionate and just in its dealings with the creation of Allah.

<!-- section 18 (id=206, raw_sort=18): Justice With Compassion -->

## Justice With Compassion

In the last session we learnt of some of the ways in which one wages war with Allah. First is through their persistence in tyranny, and second is by fighting ones conscience - the intrinsic sense of right and wrong that Allah has placed within the human soul, in the form of ⟪ar:فترۃ⟫. We learnt that once the concupiscent soul takes over, the person starts believing that he is Allah himself, and then all his actions with the group he has control or power over, they become oppressive. His focus becomes his ego, and he then demands worship from those he has control over.

# Focus On Common People

This is why Maulana Ali places such a strong emphasis on common people under the command of a ruler. He says to Malik:
كُنْ أَحَبَّ الاْمُورِ إِلَيْكَ أَوْسَطُهَا فِي الْحَقِّ، وَأَعَمُّهَا فِي الْعَدْلِ، وَأَجْمَعُهَا لِرِضَى الرَّعِيَّةِ، فَإِنَّ سُخْطَ الْعَامَّةِ يُجْحِفُ بِرِضَى الْخَاصَّةِ، وَإِنَّ سُخْطَ الْخَاصَّةِ يُغْتَفَرُ مَعَ رِضَى الْعَامَّةِ

Let the most beloved of your affairs be those that are most focused upon the truth; that are the most comprehensive in justice; and are the most inclusive of popular approval. For the disapproval of common folk undermines the approval of the elite.

This stress that he places on the importance of focusing on common people, is not only sound and practical advice for the smooth and fair administration of a state; it also reflects the spiritual imperative of realizing ones actual insignificance, and ones abasement in relation to Allah. So he's commanding Malik, to as a ruler, identify with the poor rather than the rich. This again is in accordance with the Quranic principle:

The Angels [35:15]
[ سورۃ ۳۵] فاطر

‏ يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ أَنتُمُ ٱلْفُقَرَآءُ إِلَى ٱللَّهِۖ وَٱللَّهُ هُوَ ٱلْغَنِىُّ ٱلْحَمِيدُ ‎۱۵
O mankind! it is you who stand in need of Allah, but Allah is self-sufficient, Worthy of all praise.

We know that both Maulana Ali, and Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) lived a life of poverty and simplicity. This was not because they wanted to shun the world. We learn this from the famous exchange between the ascetic ⟪ar:آثم بن زیاد⟫whose family complained to Maulana Ali that he was too abstemious. They complained that he would not indulge in anything related to the world. The narration goes that his father came to Maulana Ali and said that I have two sons. Allah has blessed both with wealth and good fortune. The older one enjoys his wealth, eats and drinks while fulfilling his religious obligations. ⟪ar:آثم⟫on the other hand, despite having the capability of living a comfortable life, he practices abstinence. Does not eat, does not drink, wears rough clothing, and when we tell him to enjoy life a bit, he gives us your example. He says I follow in the footsteps of ⟪ar:علی ابن ابی طالب⟫.

So Maulana Ali calls him over and tells him to think of his family and not to cut himself from the good things (⟪ar:الطیبات⟫) that Allah has permitted. ⟪ar:آثم⟫ says: ⟪ar:یا امیر المؤمنین⟫ you say this to me, and yet you are in your rough clothes and you eat coarse food! Maulana Ali says to him "Woe to you, I am not like you! Allah, the exalted, has made it incumbent on righteous leaders (⟪ar:أئمۃ الحق⟫) that they proportion themselves to the poorest among the common people

⟪ar:یَقَدِّرُ أنفُسَھُم بِدَفَۃِ النفس⟫Why? So that the poverty of the poor does not make them covetous.

From this example, we learn about the need for the person in power to identify themselves with the weak, the poor and the powerless; because that is the most effective way of diminishing worldliness, and enhancing spirituality in a society. So what Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam), Maulana Ali and the ⟪ar:ائمۃ⟫did, was they deliberately lowered themselves in material terms, to the level of the poorest people, they were made rulers over. In other words, **those who could have the most, **chose**to have the least**. So Maulana Ali is implying here, that if a leader is to follow the example of ⟪ar:ائمۃ الحق⟫, he must attach himself to ⟪ar:الحق⟫- because once the ruler is connected with the ultimate spiritual reality, material possessions becomes of no consequence. In that state, the most powerful and the most powerless, both have equal access to the treasures of the spiritual domain, you see? They both become equal in their spiritual sustenance from the ultimate reality, which is directly proportional to the realization of the truth expressed in the verse we just saw:
يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ أَنتُمُ ٱلْفُقَرَآءُ إِلَى ٱللَّهِۖ وَٱللَّهُ هُوَ ٱلْغَنِىُّ ٱلْحَمِيدُ

O mankind, you are destitute before Allah, and Allah is self-sufficient, the absolutely Praiseworthy. The more you accept your state; the more you manifest your need for Allah, the more he will provide. Isn't that how we operate? The more we see a person in need, the more we give?! So the same applies to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

This is why Maulana Ali insisted on rulers having a lifestyle that matches that of the most poor living under their rule. Otherwise the poor would have a valid excuse against them. They would argue that it's easy for you to talk about God and religion when you have all these worldly possessions and you don't have to work for your next meal, right? So Maulana Ali's strategy to set an example himself, and then insist that rulers align their lifestyle with the poor, was an extremely effective strategy to ensure that the inequalities in society do not generate envy among the poor.

# Justice With Compassion

Another benefit that comes out of identifying with the poor, is that it also enhances the ruler's awareness of his **own perpetual need of Allah**. It helps him depend more desperately on the mercy of Allah. How can he then expect to receive the mercy of Allah? By being merciful with his creation, right?!. So this desperate dependence on Allah, then naturally translates into an increase in mercy and compassion for one's fellow human beings.

Now the author talks about any interesting paradox. People generally think that compassion conflicts with justice, right? You have to be harsh or indifference when enacting justice, right? But according to Maulana Ali this notion is completely false. He says, the capacity to act with compassion, in no way conflicts with the demands of justice. Rather, it is an intrinsic aspect of justice, because it is aligned with the fundamental nature of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). Justice, as he defined it, is putting things in their proper place; meaning, one must place compassion whenever and wherever it is needed. Because that is the intrinsic character of ⟪ar:الحق⟫. This is why every ⟪ar:سورۃ⟫of the Quran is crowned with the verse ⟪ar:بسم اللہ الرحمن الرحیم⟫. With the name of Allah, the infinitely Compassionate, the ever merciful.

So what the author is telling us is that the capacity to act with compassion, stems not so much from a personal sentiment, but rather from an innate affinity with the very nature of the divine reality. Allah confirms his compassion in the Quran in numerous places. He says:
وَرَحْمَتِى وَسِعَتْ كُلَّ شَىْءٍۢ

My mercy encompasses all things.
رحمتی یغلِبُ غضبی

My mercy will always take precedence over my wrath.
كَتَبَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَىٰ نَفْسِهِ ٱلرَّحْمَةَ

Your master has prescribed mercy upon his own soul. The word ⟪ar:کتب⟫means to write, to document. A very interesting use of the word here. What's the wisdom in using the word ⟪ar:کتب⟫here? Once you write something, it's permanent. Words are temporary, but a written document is permanent. The rule cannot be changed, and it can be used as proof - for and against someone. So Allah says:  ⟪ar:كَتَبَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَىٰ نَفْسِهِ ٱلرَّحْمَةَ⟫ - Your master has written, he has made mercy compulsory upon himself in his dealing with creation. When he blesses, it will be out of his mercy; when he punishes it will be out of his mercy. There is not a single act he will perform that is void of his mercy - SubhanAllah!

Now keep in mind that these statements do not deny the **rigorous**side of the nature of things. This mercy does not mean that "Oh well you did 50 things wrong, I'll just punish you for 5". No! Nor do they negate the **wrathful** side of Allah. But what's being expressed here is that his rigor and wrath; they are both subordinated to his higher ontological principle of mercy. So his intrinsically beatific side that showers blessings; as well as his wrathful side that delivers punishment - both are subjected to his compassion and mercy.

Similarly, when it comes to interacting with fellow human beings, we find many sayings of Maulana Ali on the necessity of showing mercy and compassion. He said:
- The dispensing of mercy, attracts, and brings down divine mercy.
- As you grant mercy, so will you be granted mercy.
- I am astounded by the person who hopes for mercy from the one above him, while he is not merciful to those beneath him.

So what we're learning here is that the more we align our nature to Allah's nature; the more we tune-in our spiritual orientations and our moral conduct to the divine frequency of Allah's mercy, the more "real" we will become; the more righteous we become, the more "god-like" we become. The more we represent Allah as his true caliphs on this earth.

So when it comes to the concept of justice, the more a ruler extends compassion towards his subjects in this sacred manner, the more justice he will be able to deliver - not just in the physical sense, but also in the moral and spiritual sense. This is why we see that Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) began crying when the hand of the thief was chopped off. He himself ordered that punishment, why is he crying now when it's being executed? Because ordering the punishment was fulfilling his divine obligation - it was putting things in its proper place - but he had no personal vendetta against that man. His compassion for his fellow human being did not leave him for a single moment. So when the punishment was being executed his began crying for that man's pain. SubhanAllah! This is the amazing prophet we follow! (Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam)

So what we're learning here is that even though, rigorous applications of corrective punishments has its place; and at times is unavoidable - compassion should always be the underlying sentiment. This is why, in his letter, Maulana Ali goes on to instruct Malik to inflict corporal punishment upon any executive officer found guilty of misappropriation of public funds. His counsel and letters are filled with stern warnings of swift and severe retribution to his governors. And reading it, we might think where's the compassion and mercy in that?! But the thing to understand is that the severity of his warnings represent the measure of his unflinching fidelity to the requirements of fairness and honesty in the administering of public revenues. His severity is out of his compassion for the oppressed.

# Impartial Justice

For example, he writes to ⟪ar:زید بن ابیھی⟫ , who was deputy to the governor of Basra,

I swear by Allah an oath in all sincerity. If news reaches me that you have misappropriated the revenues of the Muslims, whether a small or large amount, I shall inflict such a severe punishment upon you, that it will lighten your wealth, break your back, and degrade your affairs.

Look at the severity of these words. We also know from narrations that he was extremely severe with his own relatives, especially if there was even a hint of a request for favors, that required the abuse of public funds. In one sermon, he refers to an incident when his own blind brother ⟪ar:عقیل ابن ابی طالب⟫ made a request for funds from the treasury which went beyond his due.

His elder brother ⟪ar:عقیل⟫was in a miserable state of poverty. But when ⟪ar:علی⟫became ⟪ar:خلیفۃ⟫, and ⟪ar:عقیل⟫saw his brother in that position of power as Caliph, he came to him and asked for some extra help for his needy family. Maulana Ali said that he had no money in hand at the moment. He asked him to wait till he received his salary. At that point ⟪ar:عقیل⟫asked Ali to use his authority to order some money for him from the treasury. There was a hot rod of iron close by. Ali asked his blind brother to open his palm, and brought that scorching hot metal close to his skin, barely touching his skin. ⟪ar:عقیل⟫, expecting to receive money, was shocked by the heat. He pulled his hand back saying, "Are you trying to scorch me?". At that, Maulana Ali said: "O ⟪ar:عقیل⟫. Do you shriek from a piece of iron that was heated by a man for his play, and drag me to a fire ignited by the Almighty for his wrath? " SubhanAllah!

This is the example of justice and trust Maulana Ali set in his dealing with his own "blind" brother. Look what's happening around us today. Look how leaders misappropriate wealth today for their own benefits. If you're on good terms with them, all your requests are approved by default. Your loans get approved; or they get forgiven; and if you're on bad terms with them, you requests are denied. Rulers misappropriate public funds for their own personal gains, right? Buying properties, enjoying luxuries.

When Maulana Ali was informed about the misappropriation of funds by his cousin and long-standing ally, ⟪ar:عبد اللہ ابن عباس⟫his response was a thundering reproach. He orders his cousin:

Fear Allah and return the property to these people. If you do not comply, and Allah gives me authority over you, I will excuse myself before Allah regarding you, and your wrongdoing. I shall indeed strike you with my sword, with which whoever I have struck, entered the hellfire. By Allah, even if ⟪ar:حسن⟫and ⟪ar:حسین⟫had done what you have done, I would not grant them any leniency.

The mention of the Prophet's grandsons is quite significant here. What's being taught here is that regardless of how close the relationship might be between oneself and a wrongdoer; irrespective of how exalted their status might be; the principle of justice demands absolute impartiality. There can be no favors for one's favorites.

Forget ⟪ar:عبد اللہ ابن عباس⟫, what did Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) say to his own daughter?
یا فاطمۃ بنتِ محمد اِتَّقِ اللہ۔  فأِنِّی لا أَملکُ لَکِ مِنَ اللہ شئیً

O Fatima, daughter of Muhammad, you need to have your own piety. You need to be cautious and aware of Allah; because I will have not authority without that, when I stand before Allah in your favor.

We're not talking about you and me here, we're talking about ⟪ar:مولاتنا فاطمۃ⟫ the mother of all Imams. We're talking about ⟪ar:حسن⟫and ⟪ar:حسین⟫the ⟪ar:سید الشباب اھل الجنۃ⟫the leaders of the youth in paradise. This impartial attitude, not only express the very opposite of the nepotism that literally was the hallmark of his predecessor ⟪ar:عثمان⟫administration, it is also precisely what the Quran instructs:

Women [4:135]
[ سورۃ ۴] النسا

‏ يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ كُونُوا۟ قَوَّمِينَ بِٱلْقِسْطِ شُهَدَآءَ لِلَّهِ وَلَوْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ أَوِ ٱلْوَلِدَيْنِ وَٱلْأَقْرَبِينَ ۚ إِن يَكُنْ غَنِيًّا أَوْ فَقِيرًۭا
فَٱللَّهُ أَوْلَىٰ بِهِمَا ۖ فَلَا تَتَّبِعُوا۟ ٱلْهَوَىٰٓ أَن تَعْدِلُوا۟ ۚ
وَإِن تَلْوُۥٓا۟ أَوْ تُعْرِضُوا۟ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ خَبِيرًۭا ‎۱۳۵
